# Fairband method, as built

| | |
|---|---|
| Method version | `fairband-mechanical/1.5` (`fairband.engine.METHOD_VERSION`) |
| Policy version | `fairband-policy-v16` (`src/fairband/policy/fairband-policy-v16.toml`) |
| Status | The single statement of the method the engine runs. Written 2026-11-02 from the code and the specs. |
| Rule of precedence | Where this document and any other document disagree, this document describes what runs. If the code disagrees with this document, one of them has a defect; fix the defect and keep the two aligned. |
| Update rule | Every change to the engine, the classifier, the universe rule or a policy value changes this document in the same commit, and bumps the method version when the value of any row can move. |

The reasons behind the rules are not restated here. They live in `docs/legacy/design.md` (the original design and the literature), `docs/legacy/research.md` (the founder's decisions D1 to D17), the mechanical screen spec, the calibration spec, the growth routes spec and the 2026-11-03 spec of the rate limiter, the classifier fixes and the designation (`2026-11-03-classifier-designation-ends-design.md`) and the 2026-11-04 spec of the Methodology page, the settings and the watchlists (`2026-11-04-methodology-settings-watchlists-design.md`) and the 2026-11-06 spec of the engine round (`2026-11-06-engine-round-design.md`) and the 2026-11-06 spec of the agents, Tier 2 (`2026-11-06-tier-2-agents-design.md`), and the 2026-11-09 spec of the engine round 2 (`2026-11-09-engine-round-2-design.md`), and the 2026-11-10 spec of the foreign filers (`2026-11-10-foreign-filers-design.md`), and the 2026-11-13 spec of the research round (`2026-11-13-research-round-design.md`), and the 2026-11-14 spec of the inputs an agent could not set (`2026-11-14-agent-inputs-design.md`), under `docs/superpowers/specs/`. Section 12 maps each rule to its source.

## 0. How to read this document

- **r** is the required return, **g_T** the terminal growth, **N** the runway in years (on the revenue-growth route, its explicit period, section 5a; on the Decline route, the run-off's horizon, section 6a). Rates are fractions. Totals are in the reporting currency, US dollars for a company that reports in them, and per-share figures in that currency a share; the price, the value and the levels are in US dollars a share, a share being an ADS for an ADR (section 7).
- "Trailing" means the trailing twelve months: the last four reported quarters summed, or the last two half-years of a company that reports twice a year (section 4.2). "Fiscal years" means the annual series.
- Every number the engine reads is either DATA (from a vendor, with its source and dates), RULE (from the effective policy: the policy file, with a source line, under the user's settings, 4.1) or an estimate under a named method. Every valuation constant that the literature or the founder set lives in the policy file (4.1); the code holds only the method's structural constants (4.1a).
- A phase is one of Damodaran's six: Start-up, Young Growth, High Growth, Mature Growth, Mature Stable, Decline. A route is the valuation method for a phase, and every phase has one: Mature Growth (section 5), the revenue-growth route that High Growth and Young Growth share (section 5a), Mature Stable (section 6), Decline (section 6a) and Start-up (section 6b), which gives no value by design: its row is always a refusal that keeps its figures. A foreign filer takes its phase's route in its reporting currency, its value and levels translated to US dollars (section 7).
- A refusal is a result with no value and a reason. It is never an exception, and the row is never missing.
- Determinism: the same inputs under the same policy and method version and the same settings hash give the same result, bit for bit.
- The Methodology page (`web/static/methodology.html`) is written from this document and changes with it, in the same commit.

## 1. The universe

One FMP `company-screener` call per refresh, reused for 24 hours. A listing is admitted when all of these hold:

1. the exchange is NYSE, NASDAQ or AMEX;
2. it is not an ETF or a fund, and it is actively trading;
3. its market cap, in US dollars from the screener, is at least $2 billion;
4. its FMP industry is not one of the excluded industries: banks, insurance and REITs in every FMP spelling, Shell Companies (SPACs), and `Asset Management`, `Financial - Capital Markets`, `Financial - Credit Services`, `Financial - Mortgages`, `Financial - Conglomerates` and `Investment - Banking & Investment Services`. `Financial - Data & Stock Exchanges` stays admitted. The match ignores case and dash style, and an industry that starts with an entry and a space is excluded too, so `Banks—Regional` is out while `Banking Software` is in. A symbol on the manual admission list (`manual_admissions`: V, MA, GPN, payment companies that do not lend) passes rule 4 whatever its industry; a symbol on both lists is excluded. A listed symbol is admitted without the patterns of rules 5 and 6 being checked, and the match is exact on the exchange form (`BRK.B`), as the manual exclusion list's is;
5. its symbol does not match the preferred, warrant, unit or rights patterns (`[-.]P[A-Z]?$`, `[-.]W[ST]?$`, `[-.]U$`, `[-.]R$`) and is not on the manual exclusion list (empty today). A share class such as `BRK.B` passes;
6. its name does not match the note, debenture and preferred-share patterns, compared ignoring case (`\bnotes?\b`, `\bdebentures?\b`, `\bdue 20\d\d\b`, `\d(\.\d+)? ?%(\s|$)`, `\bpreferred\b`, `\bpreference\b`). They catch the notes, debentures and preferreds whose symbols carry no suffix, such as ABXL, "Abacus Global Management, Inc. - 8.875% Fixed Rate Senior Notes due 2028"; a rate may have a space before its percent sign ("5.25 % Debentures") or end the name ("5.625%"). No name pattern covers units, rights or depositary shares: rule 5 covers the first two, a partnership's name can carry "Common Units" or "Limited Partnership Units", and an ADR's name can carry "American Depositary Shares".

A listing that fails rule 1, 2 or 3 is skipped and not recorded (the screener request already asks for these filters; the rules are applied again on the rows). A listing that fails rule 4, 5 or 6 is recorded with its reason (`industry: <industry>`, `name pattern`, `symbol pattern` or `manual exclusion`) and gets no screen row. A listing on the manual exclusion list carries `manual exclusion` whatever else it fails; one that fails more than one of the other rules carries the first of the industry, the name and the symbol rule. A later row whose symbol is already recorded is skipped. An admitted listing gets a cap band: Mega from $200 billion, Large from $10 billion, Mid from $2 billion.

A listing admitted by the manual admission list is recorded as admitted like any other, and the refresh names the list's admissions in one log line in screener order, `manual admission: V, MA, GPN`. A universe is built by a full refresh only (section 10; a revalue values the latest stored universe), so the six financial industries and the admission list apply from the next full refresh.

## 2. The data the method reads

All from FMP unless named. The depth is set in `refresh/fetch.py`.

| Need | Source | Depth |
|---|---|---|
| Income statement, balance sheet, cash flow, annual | `income-statement`, `balance-sheet-statement`, `cash-flow-statement` | 11 fiscal years: ten for the ROIC aggregate plus the opening balance. Among the lines kept, for the Decline route: `grossProfit` and `interestExpense` (income statement), `totalAssets`, `totalLiabilities` and `retainedEarnings` (balance sheet), `depreciationAndAmortization` (cash flow); for the revenue-growth, Decline and Start-up routes, `minorityInterest` (balance sheet) |
| As-reported balance sheet, annual | `balance-sheet-statement-as-reported` | 11 fiscal years; supplies the issuer's total intangibles excluding goodwill, and the lease lines |
| Income statement, balance sheet and cash flow, quarterly | the same endpoints with `period=quarter` | 8 periods: quarters, or a half-year reporter's half-years (4.2). The income statement and cash flow give the trailing figures, `grossProfit`, `interestExpense` and `depreciationAndAmortization` among the lines kept. The balance sheet, the dataset `balance-quarter`, keeps the annual sheet's lines, `minorityInterest` among them, and reads a zero for goodwill or intangibles as not presented, as the annual sheet does (4.4); the newest balance sheet, a quarter's or a fiscal year's, is chosen by 4.2's rule. The quarterly calls are not required |
| Consensus EPS, revenue, net income with analyst counts | `analyst-estimates`, annual and quarterly | 6 annual rows and 40 quarterly rows; the engine picks periods by date, never by row order. FMP gives two analyst counts, for EPS and for revenue; the net-income count reads the EPS count. Assembly reads each of the two from one stored answer, the newest that holds a row for the company, and reads no row of an older answer: FMP moves a period end by a day between answers (AAPL's, from the 27th to the 26th, between the answers of 3 and 5 November 2026), and the older answer's row would stand beside the new one. An empty answer stores no rows, so the earlier answer's rows still serve. Inside the answer, as a guard, assembly keeps one row per period: a row whose period end lies within seven days of the period's first row is that period, whatever the fiscal labels, and the row kept has the larger EPS analyst count, a tie or no count keeping the later period end; no stored answer held two such rows on 2026-11-13 |
| Street actual EPS by quarter | `earnings` | 12 rows, for the stock-compensation basis test |
| Next earnings date | `earnings` | the same rows; the next date on or after today |
| Quote: price, market cap | `quote` | current |
| Profile: country, ADR flag | `profile` | current |
| Daily closes | `historical-price-eod/full` | five years, for the price columns |
| Peers | `stock-peers` | one call a company, reused for 24 hours; not required; usable when it names a peer other than the company or is exactly the empty list; a list of objects without a symbol, or anything but a list, is unusable. The symbols stay in the stored payload; the peers' figures come from their own stored statements (5a.4); when the list leaves fewer than `peer_min` peers that count for the target margin, the company's FMP industry supplies them (5a.4) |
| Stock splits | `splits` | every split on file, newest first, stored as two observations per split (the numerator and the denominator on the split date); not required; an empty list is a usable answer (a company with no split). FMP lists a split from its effective date, never before (checked 2026-11-10) |
| Exchange rates | `historical-price-eod/light` on the pair `USD<code>` | three years (`refresh.fx_history_years`) of daily closes in units of the currency per USD, one call a currency in the `rates` stage, reused for 24 hours, stored whole as the price history is; not required: a company whose currency has no usable history is refused (section 7) |
| Pair quotes | `batch-quote` on the pairs; `quote` for a single pair | a price refresh, one call per hundred pairs (one pair alone, one `quote` call), stored as `fx-quote` observations |
| Moat score; the depositary receipt ratio (ordinary shares per ADS, 0 for a direct listing) | GuruFocus `summary` | current; reused for 7 days |
| EPS trailing and EPS without non-recurring items | GuruFocus `keyratios` | current; reused for 7 days |
| Statutory tax rate | the packaged table `tax-rates-oecd-v2.toml`: the OECD series' 146 jurisdictions, plus Taiwan and Cyprus by hand | by country and fiscal year |

A full refresh reuses a company's vendor data observed within the last 24 hours, not fetching it again; a scoped refresh fetches every call again, whatever the reuse window (section 10). FMP calls are paced to `fmp_calls_per_minute`, one pace shared by every worker; a 429 holds every FMP worker for the vendor's numeric `Retry-After` capped at `rate_limit_hold_s`, else `rate_limit_hold_s`, and the call retries after the hold. A GuruFocus 429 sleeps the same hold before its retry and holds no other worker. The retry counts and the run's 429 path are unchanged. Every response is stored as received with any key scrubbed; normalized facts cite the response they came from. Vendor numbers are stored as decimal text; the engine computes in floats.

Four endpoints are required: the three annual statements and the quote. When one of them fails, the company's row is the refusal "data fetch failed: <the failed required endpoints>". A failure of any other call leaves the company valued on what it has.

Where a field has several sources, the order is: market cap from the quote, else the profile, else the screener row; the ADR flag from the profile, else the screener row; the country from the profile (the headquarters), else the screener row, else "unknown", which has no tax rate.

## 3. The phase classifier

### 3.1 The series

The fiscal-year series runs from the newest annual income statement back, at most eleven years, one row per fiscal year with none skipped: a year the vendor lacks keeps its place with no values. Each row carries revenue, operating income, operating cash flow, investing cash flow, financing cash flow and capital spending (signed as the cash-flow statement signs it, so spending is negative), and the reporting currency of its income statement. A year without one takes the year before's; leading years without one take the first known currency after them; with none known, the currency is "unknown". Each row also carries the income statement's net income, as reported, which no rule of the classifier reads; the ten-year median net margin does (section 8).

### 3.2 Revenue growth

Growth is the compound annual rate from the fiscal year three years back to the last fiscal year:

    g3 = (revenue_last ÷ revenue_first) ^ (1 ÷ span) − 1

- With two or three fiscal years on file, the span is what exists.
- When the first year of the span has a missing, zero or negative revenue, growth runs over the longest run of consecutive positive revenues that ends at the last year.
- Growth cannot be measured when the last revenue is missing or not positive, or that run is shorter than two years.

A **Start-up** is a company whose revenue is zero now, or was zero three years ago. A year not on file is not zero.

### 3.3 Deciles and the growth word

At every full refresh the universe is ranked. The ranking takes every admitted company whose series has no data gap (3.8), is not a Start-up, has a measurable growth and has an operating margin in the last five fiscal years. The nine decile edges are the inclusive quantiles of those growth rates. A value's decile is one plus the number of edges at or below it, at most 10.

| Deciles | Growth word |
|---|---|
| 9 to 10 | Very High |
| 7 to 8 | High |
| 5 to 6 | Moderate |
| 3 to 4 | Low |
| 1 to 2 | Close to zero or negative |

The deciles are stored with the refresh. A scoped refresh (named tickers) and a revalue reuse the latest stored deciles, and each snapshot cites the refresh its deciles came from. With no deciles stored, every company is refused "no universe deciles yet". A full refresh whose fetch stops on an HTTP 429 values the companies it fetched on the stored deciles and saves no new ones; one whose deciles rank no company fails and saves none.

### 3.4 Trends, the margin and its direction

A trend is the least-squares slope of a value against the year index over the last five fiscal years. A year without a value is skipped but keeps its index. A trend needs at least three values.

The operating margin of a year is operating income ÷ revenue, defined only when both are present and revenue is positive. The margin that decides the phase is the margin of the last of the last five fiscal years that has one. The margin trend is the slope of the yearly margins.

The margin direction exists for a margin at or above zero with a trend. The positive-margin companies' margin slopes are ranked into deciles at the refresh, as the growth rates are:

- decile 9 or higher and a slope above zero: **Rising**;
- decile 2 or lower and a slope below zero: **Declining**;
- otherwise **Stable**, which points to no phase (3.6).

### 3.5 The rules

The first rule that fits decides the phase.

1. A Start-up (3.2) that is not established is **Start-up**. An established company whose revenue was zero three years ago goes on to rules 2 to 5, its growth measured over the run of positive revenues after the zero (3.2), and is flagged `revenue-gap`; one whose revenue is zero now has no measurable growth and is unclassified (3.8).
2. A growth word of "Close to zero or negative" with a falling five-year revenue trend and revenue that fell in at least three of the last four year-over-year steps is **Decline**, whatever the margin. The steps are those between the last five fiscal years; a step is a fall when both years have a revenue and the later is below the earlier, and a step with a missing year is not a fall. With fewer than three steps on file the rule cannot fire.
3. With no operating margin in the last five fiscal years, the company is unclassified ("no operating margin").
4. A negative margin:
   - (4a) of an established company takes rule 5's phase by the growth word, as if the margin were positive, and is flagged `loss-year`;
   - (4b) of any other company is **High Growth** when the margin trend is above zero, else **Young Growth** (a missing trend or a slope of exactly zero counts as not improving).
5. A margin at or above zero with a growth word of "Very High", "High" or "Moderate" is **Mature Growth**; with "Low" or "Close to zero or negative" it is **Mature Stable**. A margin of exactly zero counts as positive and is flagged.

A company is **established** when at least three of the last five fiscal years on file, or at least five of the fiscal years on file (3.1, eleven at most), have an operating margin above zero; a year without a margin counts for nothing in either count.

### 3.6 The checks

A check flags the company and never changes its phase.

| Check | Flag code | When it fires |
|---|---|---|
| Growth word | `growth-word-disagrees` | Negative margin of a company that is not established (rule 4b). The growth word points to another phase: Very High → Young Growth, High → High Growth, Moderate → Mature Growth, Low → Mature Stable, Close to zero or negative → Decline. |
| Loss year | `loss-year` | Rule 4a decides the phase: a negative margin of an established company. The sentence names the fiscal year of the deciding margin, how many of the last five fiscal years have a margin above zero and how many of the fiscal years on file do. |
| Revenue gap | `revenue-gap` | Rule 1 lets an established company whose revenue was zero three years ago through to rules 2 to 5. The sentence names the fiscal year of the zero and the two counts of the established test. An unclassified row is not flagged. |
| Revenue falling | `revenue-falling` | A growth word of "Close to zero or negative" with a falling five-year revenue trend, and revenue that fell in fewer than three of the last four year-over-year steps (rule 2). The sentence names the falls. |
| Margin direction | `margin-direction-disagrees` | Margin at or above zero with a direction of Rising or Declining. The direction points to another phase: Rising → Mature Growth, Declining → Decline. Stable points to no phase and never fires. |
| Free cash flow sign | `fcf-sign` | The last fiscal year's operating cash flow plus capital spending is negative in Mature Growth, Mature Stable or Decline, or positive in Start-up, Young Growth or High Growth. A zero does not fire. |
| Short history | `short-history` | Fewer than five fiscal years on file, or a growth span shorter than three years. |
| Zero margin | `margin-zero` | The deciding margin is exactly zero. |
| Currency change | `currency-change` | The reporting currency changes between fiscal years. Growth is still computed from the reported figures, and the route values the row in the newest year's currency (section 7). |

### 3.7 The Dickinson stage

Shown beside the phase, never deciding and never flagging. From the signs of the last fiscal year's operating (CFO), investing (CFI) and financing (CFF) cash flows:

| CFO | CFI | CFF | Stage |
|---|---|---|---|
| − | + | any | Decline |
| − | − | + | Introduction |
| + | − | + | Growth |
| + | − | − | Mature |
| every other pattern | | | Shake-out |

A zero has neither sign and lands in Shake-out unless the pattern is Decline. A missing flow gives no stage.

### 3.8 Unclassified companies

A company is unclassified, with the reason as its refusal, when its series has fewer than two fiscal years, no revenue for the last fiscal year, a negative last revenue, no measurable revenue growth (a company rule 1 does not make a Start-up with no positive run of two years, among them an established company whose revenue is zero now), or no operating margin when the margin must decide. An unclassified company does not enter the deciles.

## 4. The engine's shared pieces

### 4.1 Policy values

Every value comes from `fairband-policy-v16.toml` with a source line, save the inflation table, a packaged table of its own as the tax table is (4.3). The table lists the values the method reads and every setting: a value the user may change on the Settings view, marked yes in the column Setting. The other universe and refresh values are in sections 1 and 2.

| Value | Policy name | Setting | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| r = 9% | `required_return` | yes | stocks method v1.7 section 3 |
| g_T = 3% | `terminal_growth` | yes | same |
| Runway N: None 5, Narrow 10, Wide 20 years | `runway_years` | yes | same |
| Margin of safety: None 45%, Narrow 35%, Wide 25% | `margin_of_safety` | yes | method v1.7 Step 10 |
| Fair band 0.85 to 1.15 × value | `fair_band` | yes | Step 10 |
| Trim at 1.5 × value | `trim_multiple` | yes | Step 10 |
| Wide-moat terminal spread above r: 4 points | `wide_terminal_roic_spread` | yes | design.md 6.6: the cap is r + the spread, 13% at the defaults; spec 2026-11-04 8.3 |
| Moat: score 8 to 10 Wide, 4 to 7 Narrow, else None | `moat_wide_min`, `moat_narrow_min` | no | mech/1.2 |
| Base-rate medians by sales class (eleven classes) | `base_rates` | no | Base Rate Book, exhibit 4 |
| Held-rate cap by sales class: the 90th-percentile five-year sales growth (eleven classes) | `base_rates`, `sales_p90_5y` | no | Base Rate Book, Sales Growth exhibit 4; growth routes spec 5.4; founder decision 2026-11-03 |
| Inflation addition 2 points | `inflation_addition` | yes | mech/1.2; policy value |
| Marginal return span 5 years | `marginal_return_span_years` | no | calibration spec 5.1; policy value |
| Marginal usability: capital grew by at least 20% | `marginal_capital_growth_min` | no | calibration spec 5.1; policy value |
| Fallback return window 3 years | `fallback_return_years` | no | calibration spec 5.1; policy value |
| Cap on the return without goodwill: 25% (policy v12) | `return_without_goodwill_cap` | no | founder 2026-11-12, on the low-values investigation of 2026-11-11; no source gives the number; 4.6 rule 5 |
| Fade 5 years | `fade_years` | yes | calibration spec 5.4; Damodaran's template |
| High-growth years 5 | `high_growth_years` | yes | growth routes spec 5.4; Damodaran's template H2, H3; policy value |
| Margin convergence year 10 | `margin_convergence_year` | yes | growth routes spec 5.5; Damodaran's template H2; policy value |
| Peer minimum: 3 mature peers | `peer_min` | yes | design.md 6.2 and 6.7; no source gives the number |
| Industry peers: at most 10 | `industry_peer_max` | yes | growth routes spec 5.5; no source gives the number; founder decision 2026-11-03 |
| Peer margin: 5 fiscal years at least | `peer_margin_min_years` | no | growth routes spec 5.5; Damodaran H64; policy value |
| Bounds of the margin the price needs: −50% to 100% | `implied_margin_bounds` | no | no source (growth routes spec 5.11) |
| Own sales-to-capital bound: at least 0.5 × the mature peers' median | `own_sales_to_capital_min_share` | no | Damodaran H13, H14 (the industry sales-to-capital serves where the company's own history cannot); no source gives the number; founder decision 2026-11-06 |
| Loss ramp bound: a trailing margin below −100% | `loss_ramp_margin_bound` | no | spec 2026-11-03 9.2 (the Start-up ramp moves operating income in dollars); no source gives the number; founder decision 2026-11-06 |
| Return sensitivity 15% and 9% | `roic_sensitivity` | yes | no source |
| Thin consensus: fewer than 5 analysts | `analyst_min` | yes | method v1.7 section 3 |
| Thin revenue step: a Mature Growth consensus revenue step above 50% whose landing year has fewer than 5 revenue analysts, or no count, is not read; exactly 50% is read (policy v16) | `thin_revenue_step_max` | no | spec 2026-11-14 6.2, as narrowed on 2026-11-15: on a copy of refresh 61, of 404 such steps on too few revenue analysts four were above 40% (310%, 90.7%, 69.6%, 61.0%), the next 39.6%; leaving all 404 unread moved 298 values, 221 down; no source gives the number |
| Large non-recurring item: 5% of trailing EPS | `nri_flag_share` | yes | spec 6.7 |
| Value spread flag: 30% | `value_spread_flag` | yes | no source |
| Long runway flag: 10% growth | `long_runway_growth_flag` | yes | design.md 6.5 change 3 |
| Margin flag: 1.5 × median, 10% above the high, 6 years at least | `margin_flag_ratio`, `margin_flag_above_high`, `margin_flag_min_years` | yes | mech/1.2 |
| Heavy capex: above 50% of operating cash flow | `capex_ocf_flag` | yes | method v1.7 Step 0 |
| Equity raise: issuance at or above 1% of market cap | `equity_raise_share` | yes | method v1.7 Step 0 |
| Stock-compensation basis test: 50% of after-tax SBC a share | `sbc_detect` | no | mech/1.2 |
| Material stock pay: after-tax stock pay a share at 2% of the street trailing EPS or more, on a street EPS above zero (policy v13) | `sbc_material_share` | no | spec 2026-11-13 7.1: on a revalue of a copy of the live data on 14 Nov 2026 under method 1.3, of 1,598 rows, 748 of them valued Mature Growth rows, `sbc-basis-uncertain` showed on 135, and on 75 the stock pay was material and the first or second reason held (the one-time-item reason on 50); no source gives the number |
| Low tax for the stock-pay basis: a trailing effective tax rate at or below 0.5 × the statutory rate (policy v13) | `sbc_basis_tax_share` | no | spec 2026-11-13 7.1: on the same revalue of 14 Nov 2026, the low-tax reason held on 44 rows; no source gives the number |
| Wide gap for the stock-pay basis: street trailing EPS above GAAP by 10 × the after-tax stock pay a share or more (policy v13) | `sbc_basis_gap_multiple` | no | spec 2026-11-13 7.1: on the same revalue of 14 Nov 2026, the gap reason held on 60 rows; no source gives the number |
| Share-count band: the market cap ÷ (the price × the diluted count) from 0.7 to 1.3, an edge inside (policy v13) | `share_count_gap_band` | no | spec 2026-11-13 8: on refresh 34, 32 of 1,598 eligible rows were outside it; no source gives the numbers |
| Sheet-shift multiple: a quarter's minority interest more than 3 × the newest annual sheet's, or the annual sheet's more than 3 × the quarter's, exactly 3 × silent (policy v15) | `sheet_shift_multiple` | no | spec 2026-11-13 12.5: 3M's quarter of 30 Sep 2026 reads $1,013 million where every sheet since 2015 read $39 million to $71 million; about 13 rows expected from plan 2's scratch run; no source gives the number |
| Forward P/E unit error: below 2 | `forward_pe_unit_error` | no | mech/1.2 |
| Validity fallback: 91 days | `validity_fallback_days` | yes | design.md 8 |
| Aging at 0.6667 of the window | `aging_fraction` | yes | design system |
| Repricing horizon 3 years | `repricing_horizon_years` | yes | stocks method v1.7 Step 9; design.md 1; spec 2026-11-04 8.3 |
| FMP pace: 2,850 calls a minute | `refresh.fmp_calls_per_minute` | yes | the founder's Ultimate plan, 3,000 a minute, less 5%; spec 2026-11-03 5.2; policy value |
| 429 hold: 60 seconds | `refresh.rate_limit_hold_s` | yes | one plan window; spec 2026-11-03 5.2; policy value |
| Excluded industries: banks, insurance, REITs and Shell Companies (section 1), and six financial ones: `Asset Management`, `Financial - Capital Markets`, `Financial - Credit Services`, `Financial - Mortgages`, `Financial - Conglomerates`, `Investment - Banking & Investment Services` | `universe.excluded_industries` | no | design.md 6.1; decision 2026-10-26; spec 2026-11-03 6.4 |
| Manual admissions: V, MA, GPN | `universe.manual_admissions` | yes | founder 2026-11-03: payment companies that do not lend; spec 2026-11-03 6.4 |
| Exchanges: NYSE, NASDAQ, AMEX | `universe.exchanges` | yes | design.md 2; spec 6.1 |
| Size floor: $2 billion of market cap | `universe.cap_floor_usd` | yes | design.md 2; spec 6.1 |
| Cap bands: Mega from $200 billion, Large from $10 billion, Mid from $2 billion | `universe.cap_bands` | yes | design.md 2; spec 6.1 |
| Manual exclusions: none | `universe.manual_exclusions` | yes | design.md 2; spec 6.1 |

| The inflation table: for 42 currencies, the IMF World Economic Outlook's October 2026 projections of consumer price inflation of the currency's economy for 2027 to 2031, retrieved 2026-11-10, whose mean π restates a nominal USD rate in the currency by the differential (1 + π) ÷ (1 + π_USD) − 1 (spec 2026-11-10 7.3) | `inflation-imf-weo-v1.toml` | no | IMF World Economic Outlook, `PCPIPCH`, the datamapper API; built by `scripts/build_inflation_table.py`, its `[meta]` naming the vintage and the retrieval date |

The run-off, distress-sale, rating, default-probability, Z″ and F values are read by the Decline route (section 6a); the burn-cover value by the Start-up route (section 6b). The override ranges and the evidence rule bound an agent's overrides, the secondary values its secondary valuation and the stance words its report (spec 2026-11-06 Tier 2, 10.2, 10.3, 11 and 13.1); no mechanical route reads them. The inflation table and the three values of policy v11 serve a company reporting in another currency (section 7): the table's differential restates `required_return`, `terminal_growth`, `inflation_addition`, `return_without_goodwill_cap` and each class's `sales_p90_5y` in the currency, the inflation bound refuses a currency whose π is above it, the history depth sets how far back a full or scoped refresh fetches each currency's daily rates, and the stale-rate bound how old a rate may be before the row carries the note `fx-stale`.

The user's settings are an overlay: one stored value for each setting changed from the file's. The effective policy is the file's values with the overlay applied, built once for each run and each request. It keeps the file's version, and every range and cross-field rule (spec 2026-11-04 8.2 and 8.9) is checked when it is built, as when the file loads. A RULE input cites the policy version and carries the effective value. Every result cites the settings hash beside the policy version: the sha256 of the overlay's entries for the valuation and flag settings (groups A and B of spec 2026-11-04 8.2) and `validity_fallback_days`, as JSON with sorted keys; an empty overlay hashes `{}`. The universe and refresh settings and `aging_fraction` do not enter the hash: the first two change which companies are valued and how they are fetched, and `aging_fraction` is read when a row is shown, never at valuation.

Policies v1 to v15 stay in the package unchanged, because the snapshots valued under them cite their hashes. The engine loads v16 only.

### 4.1a Constants in code

These set the method's structure, not a valuation judgment, and live in the code: the ROIC window of ten years and its minimum of five; the growth span of three years; the trend window of five years with at least three points; the margin-direction deciles 9 and 2 (3.4); the established company's three of the last five fiscal years, or five of the fiscal years on file, with a margin above zero (3.5); the Decline rule's three falls in the last four year-over-year steps (3.5); the 80 to 100 days between consecutive quarters and the 170 to 195 days between consecutive half-years (4.2); the margin flag's ten-year window; the implied-growth scan from −50% to 200% in steps of 1% to a tolerance of 1e-7, on the Mature Growth and the revenue-growth routes alike, and the same step and tolerance for the margin the price needs within its policy bounds; the peers' ten-year margin window; the lease-accounting date of 15 December 2019; the Altman Z″ coefficients of the non-manufacturer form, 6.56, 3.26, 6.72 and 1.05 (Altman 2000), and Piotroski's nine F signals (Piotroski 2000), both for the Decline route's distress signals (6a.8); the Decline route's implied decline rate, scanned from −50% to 0 in steps of 0.1% to a tolerance of 1e-7, and the scan's lower bound, −50%, as the floor of the decline rate (6a.2); the normalized margin's window, the last five fiscal years with at least three margins (6a.3); the Start-up requirement's ramp of ten years and its solve, bisection on [0, 10^13] to a relative tolerance of 1e-9 (6b.2); the secondary valuation's grid, three rates and three terminal growths one point apart (15.5), and its terminal growth's floor of −100%, the most a business can shrink (15.1); a sensitivity reading's axes, one to five values each, and the 1e-12 within which a value is the reading in force (14.8).

### 4.2 Trailing figures and the share count

The company's cadence is read from its quarterly statement rows: quarterly when the two newest end 80 to 100 days apart, half-yearly when 170 to 195 days apart (178 to 184 on file, with 52/53-week years in reach) and the row before them, if there is one, does not end 80 to 100 days before the older of the two; FMP's quarterly rows of a company that reports twice a year are its half-years (spec 2026-11-10 8). The second test keeps a quarterly filer missing its second-newest quarter, whose two newest rows end about half a year apart after rows a quarter apart (BNY), from summing two quarters as a year: its rows give no cadence. A half-year reporter missing an older half-year (MAAS: 2024-12-31, 2025-12-31, 2026-06-30) still reads half-yearly. The trailing figures are the sums of the last four quarterly income statements when the company reports quarterly and each ends 80 to 100 days after the one before, or of the last two when it reports half-yearly (the two 170 to 195 days apart), joined to the quarterly cash-flow statements by period end: revenue, operating income, net income, diluted EPS, stock compensation, operating cash flow, capital spending, dividends, buybacks and stock issued. A sum is missing when any period lacks its part. Otherwise the trailing figures are the last fiscal year's, flagged `trailing-on-fiscal-year`, "The statement periods on file are neither four consecutive quarters nor two consecutive half-years, so the trailing figures are fiscal 2025's." The fallback keeps the cadence the rows gave, quarterly when they gave none, and reads its NTM window on it (5.2): a half-yearly reporter sent to its fiscal year by a period in another currency still reads two half-years. The last fiscal year stands in too when any of the periods' income statements names another currency than the newest annual one, the reporting currency (section 7), for a sum of periods in two currencies means nothing; the note's sentence then names those quarters or half-years and their currency, "The quarter to 2026-09-30 is in USD while the fiscal years are in CAD, so the trailing figures are fiscal 2025's."

Four more trailing figures sum the same periods, or read the same fiscal year: the interest expense, the depreciation and amortization (the cash flow's), the income tax (`incomeTaxExpense`) and the pre-tax income (`incomeBeforeTax`); the stock-pay basis note reads the last two (5.11, section 8). As every trailing figure, each is missing when any period lacks its line, or, on the fiscal-year fallback, when the year lacks it; a sum of fewer periods never stands for twelve months. The interest expense counts each period by its size, never netted, since FMP signs it either way across companies; the depreciation and the two tax lines keep FMP's sign.

Beside the trailing stock compensation, assembly reads the stock pay on file for the `stock-pay-missing` note (section 8): how many of the trailing periods (the four quarters or the two half-years, or the fiscal year on the fallback) hold a stock compensation of exactly zero in their cash flow, and the newest fiscal year whose cash flow holds one above zero, with its figure.

**The newest balance sheet.** The newest balance sheet is the stored sheet with the latest period end among the newest annual sheet and the quarterly sheets (`balance-quarter`, section 2; a half-year reporter's are its half-years); on a tie, the annual one, which also settles a quarterly sheet that ends on the fiscal year's day with other figures. A quarterly sheet whose currency is neither the reporting currency (section 7) nor missing is skipped, as a trailing period in another currency is, and the newest of the rest stands against the annual one; with no reporting currency on file, none is skipped. The sheet is read whole: a line it does not present counts as nil and is named in the input's note, as on an annual sheet, and no line is taken from another sheet. It is named by its own fiscal year and period as FMP labels them, "fiscal 2026 Q3" for a quarter's, "fiscal 2026 Q2" for a half-year's, "fiscal 2025" for a year's; FMP labels a quarter after a March year end with the next fiscal year ("fiscal 2027 Q2" for the quarter to 30 September 2026), and a sheet without a fiscal year, or a quarterly sheet without a fiscal period, is "the period ending 30 Sep 2026". It is cited as `balance_sheet`, as of its period end, and its date is its own: it moves neither the data date nor the dates of the `statements` source (4.10). It gives the debt (short-term plus long-term debt) and the cash (cash and equivalents plus short-term investments) of the revenue-growth route's equity (5a.9), of the Decline route's going-concern value, distress-sale value and rating (6a.5, 6a.7) and of the Start-up route's requirement, cash floor and burn cover (6b.2, 6b.4), and the secondary valuation's default debt and cash (15.3); the lines of the Decline route's liquidation floor (6a.6); and the minority interest (5a.9, 6a.5 to 6a.7, 6b.2, 6b.4). It does not give the capital years of the ROIC and of sales-to-capital (4.4, 5a.5), the balance years of Altman's Z″ and Piotroski's F (6a.8) or the report's solvency table (16.5): those stay on fiscal years. Assembly also hands the engine five lines of the sheet as it presents them, short-term debt, long-term debt, cash and equivalents, short-term investments and the minority interest, and, where the sheet is a quarter's, the same five lines of the newest annual sheet, named as that sheet is ("fiscal 2025"), a line it does not present none; only the note `sheet-shift` reads them, and no value (section 8).

The diluted share count is the newest quarterly row's weighted average diluted shares (a quarter's, or a half-year's), else the newest fiscal year's. One share count serves every year of every route. An agent's `shares.diluted` replaces it, period-tied, for a share class or units that convert to shares that it leaves out; an agent's `shares.committed` adds to it, period-tied, the shares the company has committed to issue that neither it nor the analysts' EPS holds yet, in the count in force, which divides every value among the owners (14.4).

**Stock splits.** Every share count and per-share figure is read on the share basis of the quote date, each trailing period (the four quarters or the two half-years, or the fiscal year on the fallback) by its own period end. A split on file (section 2) dated after the oldest trailing period's end and at or before the price date is pending, and the row carries the note `split-pending` (section 8). FMP restates its statements, street actuals, consensus and closes to the new basis, but not on a known day, so the rule scales only what the data shows pre-split; below, a count lies within another when it is within `split_implied_count_tolerance` (15%) of it. The diluted count is judged against the quote's own count (market cap ÷ price) by the ratio of the splits after the newest period's end: it is pre-split, and read times that ratio, when the count times the ratio, and not the count itself, lies within the quote's count; with every pending split before the newest period's end it is on the quote's basis already. When the count lies within the quote's count on both bases or neither, or the quote gives no count, the statements are read as stored, every period with them. Each trailing period with a split after its end is judged on its own count, by the ratio of the splits after its own end: the newest against the quote's count, as the diluted count is, each older one against the newest period's post-split count (the diluted count as read), and the fiscal-year fallback by the year's own count. A pre-split period's diluted EPS and its street actual are divided by its ratio, so a trailing sum of periods on two bases is read on one; a period without a count, or whose count fits both bases or neither of the count it is judged against, is read as stored and named with its reason. FMP's figures are called post-split only when the diluted count and every period judged read post-split. The trailing EPS and the street trailing EPS are then the sums of the periods' figures. Each consensus EPS, annual or quarterly, ending after the newest statement period end, is divided by the ratio of the splits after that end when its implied count (the net-income mean ÷ the EPS mean) lies within the pre-split count and not the post-split one; a row without a net-income mean, with an EPS of zero, or whose implied count lies within both counts or neither is left as stored and named with its reason, and without a diluted count every consensus row is left as stored, as the statements are. GuruFocus's trailing EPS and EPS without non-recurring items are divided by the splits after their observation. The closes are FMP's, already adjusted. Several pending splits compound. A reverse split prints its inverse: ÷10 on the count and ×10 on the per-share figures for a 1-for-10. The count, the trailing EPS, the street trailing EPS, GuruFocus's two EPS figures and the NTM EPS, when scaled, are estimates under the method `split-scaled` and carry a note naming the split, and the share of the quarters scaled when not every one was ("on 3 of 4 quarters", or "on 1 of 2 half-years" for a half-yearly reporter, whose periods the note's sentence names half-years too); the note's sentence names the rest. A price refresh applies no split rule: on a split day the stored value meets the new quote until the next refresh that runs assembly, and the remedy is a scoped refresh of the company (section 10).

**The listed-share basis.** A row's statements are read on the quote's share basis before the split rule when its GuruFocus depositary receipt ratio ρ (ordinary shares per ADS, section 2) is neither 0 nor 1, whatever FMP's ADR flag (JBS and MAAS on the live data carry the flag 0 and a ratio), the quote gives its count and a diluted count is on file; a row with no ratio or a ratio of 0 (a direct listing) or 1 reads its counts as the split rule alone reads them. FMP states an ADR's count and EPS per ADS, the unit the quote prices, but on some periods holds a count off by ρ or its inverse. A count fits a factor f of 1, ρ and 1 ÷ ρ, tried in that order, when the count × f, as it is or × the ratio of the pending splits after its period's end, lies within `split_implied_count_tolerance` (15%) of the count it is judged against. The diluted count is judged against the quote's count, then each trailing period (the four quarters or the two half-years, or the fiscal year on the fallback) on its own count: the newest against the quote's count, each older one against the diluted count on the quote's basis. One factor fitting, or two that read the count at one multiple (a depositary's ratio change that FMP lists as a split), is the period's: its count reads × f and its diluted EPS ÷ f, and the trailing EPS is the sum of the periods'; the split rule then judges the counts so read as it judges any. A period whose count fits no factor, or two at different multiples, is read as stored and named with its reason, and so, when the row carries the note, is a period without a count; the diluted count so is the refusal "share basis unclear: the newest period's count <c> against the quote's <Q> (the ADR ratio <ρ>)", the counts in millions to one decimal, every figure as stored. The street actuals, the consensus, GuruFocus's EPS and the closes are per ADS already and take no factor. The count and the trailing EPS so scaled are estimates under the method `basis-scaled`, which takes the place of `split-scaled` on a figure both rules scaled, and carry a note naming the factor and the ratio: "×10 to the quote's share basis: the stored count, 145.8 m, is a tenth of the quote's, 1,450.8 m (the ADR ratio 10)" on the count, "÷10 to the quote's share basis (the ADR ratio 10)" on the trailing EPS, which adds "on 1 of 4 quarters" ("on 1 of 2 half-years") when not every period scaled; a ratio below one prints its ADSs to a share, "three ADSs to one share (the ADR ratio 0.3333)"; on a figure both rules scaled the split's note comes first. The row carries the note `basis-scaled` (section 8) when a factor other than 1 scaled a figure or a period is read as stored for the basis; the ratio is a DATA input of the row (5.12).

### 4.3 The tax rate

NOPAT uses the statutory marginal rate: the OECD combined central and sub-central corporate rate for the company's country and the fiscal year. The table is `tax-rates-oecd-v2.toml`: every jurisdiction of the OECD series (dataflow `DSD_TAX_CIT@DF_CIT`, measure `CIT_C`, sector `S13`, targeting `ST`), 146 at the retrieval of 2026-11-06, each year from 2014 the series gives, each jurisdiction named by its ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code; and, written by hand because the series lacks them, Taiwan (17% from 2014 to 2017 and 20% from 2018 to 2026; the Income Tax Act, article 5, Ministry of Finance) and Cyprus (12.5% from 2014 to 2025; the Tax Department). A rate cites the table's source, the OECD series, except on a row written by hand, which cites its own source line; a carried-forward estimate cites the newest year's row. `scripts/build_tax_table.py` regenerates the table from the series' CSV export. `tax-rates-oecd-v1.toml`, the sixteen jurisdictions of 0.8 and earlier, stays in the package for the snapshots that cite it. The country is the headquarters country of the FMP profile, else the universe row's. A fiscal year later than the newest year in the table takes the newest year's rate as an estimate under method `tax-rate-latest-oecd-year-v1`, flagged `tax-carried-forward`. An earlier missing year, or a country the table lacks, has no rate: the first fiscal year of the eleven-year span with an operating income and no rate refuses the company, "no statutory tax rate for <country> <year>", a foreign filer as any other, before the currency's refusals (section 7). The newest fiscal year's rate also converts stock compensation to after tax in the anchor (5.2), and taxes every forecast year of the revenue-growth route (5a.6).

### 4.4 Invested capital and NOPAT

Invested capital at a fiscal year end, from the operating side:

    capital = (total current assets − cash and short-term investments)
            − (total current liabilities − short-term debt − current lease liability)
            + net property and equipment + lease right-of-use assets + goodwill + intangibles

- Total current assets, total current liabilities and net property and equipment are required; without one of them the year has no capital.
- Cash and investments are cash plus short-term investments; one missing counts as nil.
- Intangibles are the issuer's as-reported total intangibles excluding goodwill (method `bs10-intangibles-total-v1`) where the as-reported balance sheet has it, else FMP's normalized line. On FMP's normalized balance sheet a zero for goodwill or intangibles is read as not presented, never as a reported zero; on the as-reported balance sheet a zero is a reported zero.
- The lease lines come from the as-reported balance sheet only. A balance sheet ending before 15 December 2019 with no lease assets is flagged `leases-not-on-balance-sheet`; nothing is reconstructed.
- Any other optional line the issuer does not present counts as nil under method `not-presented-as-nil-v1` and is flagged `line-not-presented:<field>`. Cash and investments, short-term debt, the current lease liability, lease assets, goodwill and intangibles are the optional lines.

NOPAT for a fiscal year = operating income × (1 − statutory rate).

### 4.5 The ten-year aggregate ROIC

A ROIC year needs its NOPAT and both its opening and closing capital. Its average capital is (opening + closing) ÷ 2, so ten years need eleven balance sheets. The last ten such years, oldest first, are the kept years.

    aggregate = Σ NOPAT ÷ Σ average capital, over the kept years

- Fewer than five kept years: no aggregate, flag `roic-unavailable`.
- Five to nine: flag `roic-on-n-years`.
- A capital sum at or below zero: no aggregate, flag `ten-year-roic-unavailable`.

The aggregate is shown and feeds the "ten-year" sensitivity (5.8). It never enters the charge. The kept years also give the revenue-growth route its own sales-to-capital (5a.5) and the Decline route its own-latest measure (6a.4).

### 4.6 The runway return

The return the charge starts at: it holds through the hold K and then fades to the terminal return in year N (4.8). From the kept years, in this order:

1. **Unavailable** with fewer than five kept years: the charge uses the terminal return (4.7) in every year.
2. **The five-year marginal return**: with at least six kept years, (NOPAT_last − NOPAT_first) ÷ (capital_last − capital_first), "last" the newest kept year and "first" the kept year five positions earlier, capital the average capital. Usable when capital grew, by at least a fifth of the first year's capital in size, and NOPAT grew.
3. **The three-year aggregate** when the marginal is not usable: Σ NOPAT ÷ Σ average capital over the last three kept years.
4. **Charge off** when the marginal is not usable and the three-year capital sum is at or below zero: the runway years are not charged (payout 100%), flag `charge-zeroed`. The terminal still charges (4.7).
5. **Below r, the return without goodwill**: a chosen measure below r is read again on capital without goodwill and intangibles. A past purchase's price sits in the capital and can push the measure below r while the business earns more on what it reinvests. Each kept year's average capital is taken less the average of goodwill plus intangibles at its opening and closing balance sheets (a line not presented nil, 4.4), its NOPAT unchanged, and rules 2 and 3 run on that capital: the five-year marginal when usable there, else the three-year aggregate. When that return is above r, the charge starts at it, at most `return_without_goodwill_cap` (25%), since the measure leaves out capital the company paid for; flag `return-without-goodwill`. When the marginal is not usable there and the three-year capital without goodwill sums to zero or below while the three years' NOPAT sums above zero, the business needs no capital of its own, and the charge starts at the cap under the same flag. One case keeps the floor whatever those figures read, **bought growth**, where purchases carried the growth of the capital over the marginal span. Three tests stand together: the marginal with goodwill is usable (rule 2: capital grew by a fifth, and NOPAT grew); the marginal without goodwill is not; and goodwill and intangibles are higher in the newest kept year than in the kept year five positions earlier. The level of return on the business's own capital then counts the bought earnings and none of their price, so it says nothing of what the growth costs, and it is not read; the `return-floored` sentence says so.
6. **The floor**: otherwise a chosen measure below r is replaced by r, flag `return-floored`.

Four edges of rule 5 are known and left to the agent's `return.runway` (14.2), which the recorded measures inform. A chosen measure at or above r stands, with no cap, and never reads the return without goodwill: so a measure just below r and one just above it can start the charge far apart. For a measure just above r that a purchase holds down, the override raises the start, and the Wide moat's terminal return reads the override (4.7, 14.4). The intangibles are the balance sheet's whole line: it holds what a purchase put there and also what the business operates on, a licence, spectrum, a franchise, since the vendor's line does not split them, so a row rich in the second kind starts too high. A business that bought and also grew its own capital by a fifth in the same years reads the marginal without goodwill, which counts the bought earnings too: the cap bounds it, and the fifth is a cliff, as rule 2's is. And purchases that grew the capital by less than a fifth leave no usable marginal with goodwill, so the bought-growth test does not see them, and the level without goodwill is read.

The row records which measure it used ("marginal", "three-year", "without-goodwill", "floor", "off", "unavailable") with the marginal, its usability, the three-year and the ten-year figures, and the value the charge starts at. On every row with five kept years it records the measures on capital without goodwill and intangibles, whichever measure the charge took (`runway_return.without_goodwill`: `marginal`, `marginal_usable`, `three_year`, none when its capital sums to zero or below, `measure`, the usable marginal else the three-year; `capital_free`, no capital without them and NOPAT above zero; and `bought`, the three tests of bought growth, under which the charge reads none of these), and each kept year carries its `average_goodwill_and_intangibles` (`roic.by_year`). The row also records the newest year's marginal return (`runway_return.one_year`), the change in NOPAT over the change in average capital from the kept year before the newest to the newest, shown and never used: it has no usability test, and holds no figure with fewer than two kept years or no change in capital. The `heavy-capex` sentence (8) names it beside the measure the charge starts at, since a capital-spending surge still under way after the newest stored year is the agent's judgment, through `return.runway` (14.2), which replaces the value the charge starts at.

The row records one more figure, shown and never used: the five-year marginal return at a constant margin (`runway_return.constant_margin`), the newest kept year's NOPAT over that fiscal year's revenue, times the revenue added over the average capital added across the marginal span, the span's own sales-to-capital (5a.5). The marginal return of rule 2 divides the whole change in NOPAT by the capital added, so a margin that rose on the revenue already there reads as a return on new capital, though it took none, while the charge pays for revenue growth alone (4.8). This figure leaves the margin's change out; where the margin fell it reads above the marginal, and at a steady margin the two agree. It holds no figure with fewer than six kept years, either year of the span without a revenue, a newest revenue at or below zero, or a span over which capital did not grow by a fifth or revenue did not grow. Which of the two says what new capital will earn is the agent's judgment, through `return.runway`: a margin gain from costs cut, or from prices at a cycle's peak, does not repeat on new capital, and one from a mix moving to a line with a higher margin, where the new capital went, is a return on that capital. The engine keeps the marginal (founder 2026-11-15): read on the stored years of 358 companies, neither measure nor the three-year aggregate said clearly better than the others what the growth of the following years took in capital, each a factor of 1.6 to 1.9 off at the median.

### 4.7 The terminal return

The return in year N and in the terminal value.

- Narrow or None moat: r.
- Wide moat: the chosen measure of 4.6 before its floor, capped at r plus the spread (`wide_terminal_roic_spread`, 4 points: 13% at the defaults) and never below r. On a row whose charge starts at the return without goodwill (4.6 rule 5): that start, under the same cap. When the runway charge is off: the cap. When the runway return is unavailable: r.

The same rule, applied to a given return, gives the terminal return for each sensitivity value (5.8), and on the revenue-growth and Start-up routes for the return on new capital at the target margin (5a.7, 6b.2). Under an agent's `return.runway` (14.4) the override is the return in force, and the rule reads it in place of the measure on every row: Wide, the override capped at r plus the spread and never below r; Narrow or None, r.

### 4.8 The charge and the value

Earnings in year t are a total, on the one share count. Each year's reinvestment pays for the next year's growth h_{t+1} at the year's return:

    cash_t = earnings_t × (1 − h_{t+1} ÷ return_t)

with no floor. A year whose growth outruns its return pays out less than nothing, the owner's money going in; the first such year raises `growth-above-return`, which counts the rest. When the charge is off (4.6 rule 4), cash_t = earnings_t for t < N. An infinite return charges nothing.

**The growth the charge pays for, h.** The charge pays for revenue growth, not earnings growth: earnings growth above the revenue growth is margin change on the capital already in place and needs no new capital, and earnings growth below it still pays for the revenue. On the Mature Growth route h_2 and h_3 are the consensus revenue steps (5.3), with an agent's revenue step (`revenue.step_2`, `revenue.step_3`, 14.4) in its place where one applies; a missing one, or one whose earlier year is not positive, takes the earnings step of the same year as resolved, an override included, unless an agent's revenue step stands there. So does a thin one, a consensus revenue step above `thin_revenue_step_max` (50%) whose landing year has fewer than `analyst_min` (5) revenue analysts, or no count, which the charge does not read (5.3). Years 4 to 3 + the fade years fade from h_3 to the belief in equal steps, as 5.5 fades the earnings path (none when h_3 is at or below the belief), the years after grow at the belief, and the runway cuts the path as it cuts the earnings path; so h and the earnings growth differ only in the consensus and fade years. h_{N+1} = g_T. On the Mature Stable route h = g_T in every year (6.3).

**The return by year.** The runway return R (4.6) holds through the hold K, then fades in a straight line to the terminal return T (4.7), which year N takes:

    return_t = R                                   for t ≤ K
    return_t = R + (T − R) × (t − K) ÷ (N − K)     for K < t ≤ N

K is the last year t in 1 … N − 1 whose next earnings growth g_{t+1} is above the belief, 0 when none is, so a flat step inside the consensus steps or the fade does not end it. K is read once, from the row's own path at the belief in force (the mechanical belief or an override's, 14.2), and the implied growth (5.7) and every sensitivity (5.8) hold it fixed while the belief, the runway or the return moves, so the value stays continuous in the belief. With K = 0 (every Mature Stable row) the return fades from year 1; with K ≥ N − 1 (a short runway cut mid-fade) only year N takes T, the shape before method 1.0.

    terminal multiple = (1 + g_T) × (1 − g_T ÷ T) ÷ (r − g_T)
    value = Σ_{t=1..N} cash_t ÷ (1 + r)^t + earnings_N × terminal multiple ÷ (1 + r)^N

The value a share is the total ÷ the count in force: the diluted share count (4.2), plus any committed shares (14.4). The intermediates carry `charge`: `growth_by_year`, h for years 2 to N + 1 (g_T last); `return_by_year`, years 1 to N; `hold_years`, K; `revenue_steps`, the two revenue steps the charge read, the consensus's or an agent's in its place, none where the earnings step stands in; `revenue_steps_found`, the two consensus revenue steps as found, none where missing; and `revenue_step_source`, for each of years 2 and 3 where the step read came from, `consensus`, `agent`, `thin` (the consensus's step left out as thin, the earnings step standing in) or `none` (on a Mature Stable row none, none and `none`; spec 2026-11-14 6.3). Beside it, `charge_off` says whether the runway years went uncharged (4.6 rule 4): false under a `return.runway` override, which charges them (14.4), whatever the measure (`runway_return.used`) reads. `charge.revenue_steps` and `charge.hold_years` are rebuild keys: a price refresh and the cross-check (6.4) rebuild the same charge from them.

### 4.9 The moat class

From the GuruFocus Moat Score: 8 or more Wide, 4 or more Narrow, below 4 or no score None. The moat enters in three places: the runway N, the terminal return and the margin of safety. The revenue-growth route's explicit period does not read it, so there the moat enters the terminal return and the margin of safety only. On the Decline route it enters the margin of safety only (6a.5). On the Start-up route it enters the terminal return only (6b.2): a Start-up row has no levels.

### 4.10 Levels, designation and validity

On the value at belief, each level the exact decimal product:

- buy level = value × (1 − margin of safety for the moat);
- fair band = value × the band's low to value × its high (0.85 and 1.15 at the defaults);
- trim level = value × the trim multiple (1.5 at the defaults).

For a company in another currency (section 7) the value is the route's value a share ÷ the price date's rate, and the levels are the products above on that USD value: the value, the levels and the price are US dollars a share, per ADS for an ADR, and the route's figure stays in the intermediates as `value_in_currency`.

The levels are computed once at valuation and shown as figures; nothing in the engine reads the price against them. On every valued row the engine reads the price against the value at belief, price ÷ value as a plain division, and gives it a word by the designation edges e1 to e4. The edges are derived from the policy, not set: e1 = 1 − the Narrow margin of safety, e2 = the fair band's low, e3 = the fair band's high, e4 = the trim multiple (0.65, 0.85, 1.15, 1.50 at the defaults; method v1.7 Step 10), so a changed margin of safety, band or trim multiple carries them along. The ratio is compared with each edge as written:

| price ÷ value | Code | Screen word |
|---|---|---|
| below e1 | `well-below` | Well below |
| from e1, below e2 | `below` | Below |
| from e2 to e3, ends included | `near` | Near |
| above e3, below e4 | `above` | Above |
| at or above e4 | `well-above` | Well above |

The word is the band's, never an action. The designation is a fact about the price: a price refresh recomputes it; a refusal and a Start-up row have none. A snapshot valued under method 0.5 carries a stance and no designation; it reads with none until a revalue replaces it.

The data date is the date of the newest observation among the company's stored statement facts, those of the annual income statement, balance sheet, cash flow and as-reported balance sheet and of the quarterly income statement and cash flow (today, when it has none). The quarterly balance sheet is not among them, so a quarterly sheet fetched later moves no data date. The newest balance sheet (4.2) carries its own date, its period end. A result is valid until the next earnings date when that date is after the data date, else `validity_fallback_days` (91) days after the data date. It is current until `aging_fraction` (0.6667) of the window has passed, then aging, then expired after the window's end. An expired row keeps its figures.

### 4.11 What the price needs

On a row whose price is well above its value (the designation `well-above`: a price at or above the trim level), the value alone says only that the method cannot reach the price. The company's detail then carries `priceNeeds`, one or two sentences on what the price asks of the business, written from the figures the row already solves at its price (`engine.price_needs`):

| Route | The price needs | Against |
|---|---|---|
| Mature Growth | the implied growth (5.7), "long-run earnings growth of 11.9% a year to year 20" | the belief, "The route holds 5.3%." |
| Mature Stable | the growth-route cross-check's implied growth (6.4), "On the growth route's path, …"; no sentence when the cross-check refused, which solved nothing at the price | terminal growth |
| Revenue growth | the implied held rate of years 4 to H, and the margin the price needs (5a.10), "a target operating margin of 35.2%": the target the margin path moves to by the convergence year, which a setting can put past the forecast's last year, so the sentence names no year; under a `high_growth_years` of 3, which holds no year at the held rate, the margin alone | the held rate and the target margin |
| Decline | the implied decline rate (6a.9), "a decline rate of −0.8% a year": the route's own rate, which starts a fade to zero (6a.2), so the sentence names no years | the decline rate |

Where a solve found no rate in its range, the sentence says so in place of a figure: "No long-run earnings growth rate in the range the route reads reaches the price." On the revenue-growth route each of the two solves speaks for itself, and when neither finds a rate one sentence says so and names what the route holds.

A mature row charged at r in every year, year N's terminal return included, is told apart: a floored row under any moat, a row whose return is unavailable, or one whose `return.runway` an agent set at r where the terminal return is r too. There growth adds no value: the solve's rate is often none, and one it finds comes from the uncharged margin change alone and never measures the business's growth (5.8). "No rate in the range" would give the wrong reason and such a rate would mislead, so the test comes before the solve's rate, and the sentence reads "The route charges growth at the hurdle rate, so growth adds no value and no growth rate reaches the price." On Mature Growth it goes on with the growth the price needs at the return sensitivity's highest return above r that found one (`implied_by_roic`, 5.8): "At a return of 15.0% on new capital, the price needs long-run earnings growth of 10.7% a year to year 20." The r is the row's own, the `required_return` input its route read, restated in the currency of a company in another one (7.5); a row whose runway years go uncharged (4.6 rule 4) is never read as one.

The sentences are written for the value the drawer's meter draws: the review's, when the review on the latest snapshot counts and has a value, from the review's own result at its word at the latest snapshot's price; else the snapshot's. The drawer and the report page lead the band with them, above the meter. Nothing is stored and nothing moves: the value, the levels and the designation stand, and the sentences are built when the detail is read. A row not well above its value has none; neither has a Start-up row, a refusal with no reviewed value, or a row whose record lacks the figures.

## 5. The Mature Growth route

A reverse DCF on net income with the reinvestment charge: year 1 from the quarterly consensus (on a thin window, its last fiscal year's level from the annual consensus, 5.2), years 2 and 3 from the annual consensus, a fade to a base-rate belief, the belief to the runway, then the terminal.

### 5.1 Guards and refusals

Before any route, in this order: no universe deciles yet; the assembly's refusal, the first of "no financial statements", the missing tax rate of 4.3, which refuses every company, the currency's refusals of section 7 ("reporting currency unknown", "no exchange rate for <code>", "no inflation projection for <code>", "<code>: expected inflation <π>% above the 10% bound") and the listed-share basis's "share basis unclear" (4.2); an unclassified company (3.8); no positive price; no positive diluted share count. The ADR flag refuses nothing. A company in another currency then takes its route in that currency (section 7). A Start-up row then takes its route (6b), which no missing trailing figure refuses; every other row is refused without trailing figures. Every phase has a route: Mature Growth and Mature Stable their own, High Growth and Young Growth the revenue-growth route of section 5a, Decline the run-off of section 6a and Start-up the requirement of section 6b.

Then the route's own, in this order: no trailing revenue; no statutory tax rate; an incomplete NTM consensus (5.2); no positive forward anchor; a forward P/E below 2 (a unit error); consensus earnings that turn negative within the path (a step at or below −100%); a value a share at or below zero under the charge (the intermediates are kept).

### 5.2 Year 1: the NTM anchor

Year 1 is the sum of the consensus periods after the last reported one that cover the next twelve months, the NTM window: four consecutive quarters, or two consecutive half-years for a half-yearly reporter (4.2); the base fiscal year holds most of them, a tie taking the later (5.3). The periods are the first consensus periods ending after the trailing period end, by period end. A company that reports quarterly, a fiscal-year fallback whose rows gave no cadence among them (4.2), reads the first four, and only when each ends 80 to 100 days after the one before; never two rows about half a year apart, which on a consensus with gaps would sum two quarters as a year. A half-yearly reporter reads the first two when the second ends 170 to 195 days after the first and no consensus row ending after the trailing period end sits 80 to 100 days from either of them; else the first four quarters as a quarterly reporter does: the window its consensus cadence gives, for a company whose statements are half-years and whose consensus rows are quarters (ABVX). The second test keeps two quarters with the one between them missing from reading as two half-years: WSE's consensus has quarters ending 2027-03-31 and 2027-09-30 without 2027-06-30, beside one ending 2027-12-31, so after a period ending 2026-12-31 it has no window. Each period of the window needs an EPS mean. NTM EPS raw = their sum, corrected on a thin window as below. They are read from the newest stored answer that holds consensus rows, no row of an older answer (section 2), and from one row per period in it, a row ending within seven days of the period's first row being that period and the row with the larger EPS analyst count kept (a tie or no count, the later period end): a guard for two rows of one period inside one answer, which would break the chain. A row ending within seven days after the trailing period end is the period just reported, whose end in the consensus can lie a day or a few days from the statements', and is skipped, so the first NTM period is the one after it.

A thin window (spec 2026-11-13 9a). A period of the window is thin when it has fewer than `analyst_min` (5) EPS analysts or no count. Let F be the last fiscal year the window reaches. No period of F is reported: F starts inside the window, or the window is F whole. Where a period of the window in F is thin and the annual figure of F exists:

    NTM EPS raw = the sum of the window's EPS means
                + (annual EPS mean of F − the sum of the EPS means of F's periods)
                  × (the window's periods in F ÷ the periods in a year)

with four periods in a year on the quarterly cadence and two on the half-yearly one. The annual consensus sets the level of F; the periods' consensus sets the split inside F. A window that is F whole reads F's annual estimate. A calendar company whose last reported quarter is the third keeps its fourth quarter's estimate as it is, and adds three quarters of the gap between next year's annual estimate and next year's four quarters. The annual figure of F exists when F has an annual consensus row with an EPS estimate (a mean of zero whose EPS analyst count is missing or zero is no estimate, as 5.3 has it) on 5 EPS analysts or more, and the periods' consensus holds every period of F, as many as a year holds, each with an estimate by the same test. A period's fiscal year and an annual row's are the labels the path matches by (5.3). The fiscal year that is partly reported is never corrected: the window's periods before F keep their own estimates, thin or not, and that year's annual estimate is not read. Where no period of the window in F is thin, or the annual figure of F does not exist, the sum of the periods stands; `thin-ntm` shows on a thin period left as it is (5.11). Each period still needs an EPS mean: without one the window refuses "incomplete NTM consensus", whatever the annual consensus holds. The stock-compensation adjustment below, still scaled by the window's revenue, comes off the raw figure so found, and the refusals below, `earnings.ntm` and `earnings.cycle_gap` (14.4) read that figure as they read the periods' sum; the basis test, the base year and the path are untouched. The rule holds wherever the anchor is read: the Mature Growth value, the growth-route cross-check of a Mature Stable or revenue-growth row (6.4, 5a.11) and the forward EPS of the revenue-growth route's multiples (5a.10). Only the first moves a value. The NTM revenue of the revenue-growth and Decline routes stays the sum of the window's revenue means (5a.2, 6a.2). The `anchor` entry records the source and the correction (5.12).

The error it accepts: the gap of F is spread evenly over F's periods. Where the annual consensus and the periods' consensus of F agree, the rule changes nothing, whatever the seasons or the growth inside the year. An offline test of the rule on a scratch copy of the store of 2026-11-14 left 91% of the 450 rows tested within 5% of the periods' sum and 97% within 10%.

The stock-compensation basis test: the street trailing EPS is the sum of the street actuals whose periods are the trailing period ends, the four quarters' or the two half-years' (missing when one is absent or the trailing figures are a fiscal year). After-tax SBC a share = trailing stock compensation × (1 − the newest fiscal year's statutory rate) ÷ diluted shares. When street EPS − GAAP trailing diluted EPS ≥ 0.5 × after-tax SBC a share, the consensus excludes stock compensation, and the anchor subtracts after-tax SBC a share × (NTM consensus revenue ÷ trailing revenue, the window's revenue means summed; the factor is 1 when any NTM period lacks a revenue mean or trailing revenue is missing or not positive). When any of the three inputs is missing, the basis is unknown, the consensus stands as it is, flag `sbc-basis-unknown`.

NTM EPS = raw − adjustment. NTM net income = NTM EPS × diluted shares. An NTM EPS at or below zero refuses; so does a price ÷ NTM EPS below 2.

In a review (14.4) an agent's two corrections a share come off in this order (spec 2026-11-14 5):

    NTM EPS = raw − adjustment − one-time amount − cycle gap

The one-time amount is an `earnings.ntm_nri` override, an item that the consensus carries and that does not recur, gains on an asset outside the business say; the cycle gap is an `earnings.cycle_gap` override, the correction to mid-cycle earnings. A negative figure of either, a one-time loss or a trough, raises year 1. Under an applied `earnings.ntm` the guided EPS stands in for raw − adjustment, the consensus with its items, so the one-time amount is not read and the cycle gap still comes off:

    NTM EPS = guided EPS − cycle gap

The refusal "no positive forward anchor" reads the NTM EPS so found. The unit-error check, the forward P/E of 5.9 and the `ntm_eps` and `ntm_net_income` DATA entries read raw − adjustment, the consensus as found. Each correction is a share on the diluted shares, the count the consensus is on (below), so NTM net income moves by it × that count. The growth-route cross-check (6.4) reads the cycle gap, and neither the guided EPS nor the one-time amount.

The diluted shares of the stock pay a share and of NTM net income are the diluted count of 4.2, an agent's `shares.diluted` in its place: the count the consensus EPS is on. An agent's committed shares (`shares.committed`, 14.4), which neither that count nor the consensus holds yet, enter neither figure; they join the count in force, the diluted count plus them, which divides the value among the owners (5.6). So x committed shares on a count of n leave NTM net income and the stock-pay adjustment as they were and lower the value a share by x ÷ (n + x).

### 5.3 Years 2 and 3: the consensus path

The base year is the fiscal year that holds most of the NTM window's periods (5.2); a tie takes the later year, so a window of two half-years, one in each of two fiscal years, lands where a quarterly reporter's window lands. An estimate's fiscal year is FMP's label when the row carries one, else the year derived from the company's fiscal calendar: the month and day of the newest annual income period end, where a period ending up to seven days past a year's end still belongs to that year. Its quarter label is FMP's `fiscalPeriod` when the row carries one, else the calendar's: 4 less the days from the period end to its fiscal year's end counted in quarters of 365.25 ÷ 4 days, rounded and held between 1 and 4. Neither the fiscal year nor the quarter label decides which rows are one period; the period ends do (section 2). The path reads the annual consensus for the base year and the next two:

- on net income when all three years have a positive net-income mean, else on EPS, flag `path-on-eps`;
- a mean of zero with no analyst count, or a count of zero, is no estimate;
- step g_2 = year(base + 1) ÷ year(base) − 1, step g_3 = year(base + 2) ÷ year(base + 1) − 1; a step is missing when either year is missing or the earlier is not positive, and a missing step takes the belief;
- a present step whose landing year has fewer than 5 analysts, or no count, flags `few-analysts`. The count is FMP's EPS analyst count on either series;
- the path also reads the consensus revenue steps on the same years, by the same rule on the revenue mean and its analyst count (a mean of zero with no revenue analyst count is no estimate), and keeps them beside the earnings steps for the charge of 4.8. A missing revenue step, or one whose earlier year is not positive, takes the earnings step of the same year in the charge; an earnings step override (`path.step_2`, `path.step_3`) moves that fallback and never a revenue step the consensus gives; an agent's `revenue.step_2` or `revenue.step_3` (14.4) replaces the consensus revenue step of its year in the charge, or stands where the consensus gives none, so an earnings step override no longer moves the charge of that year;
- a consensus revenue step above `thin_revenue_step_max` (50%) whose landing year has fewer than `analyst_min` (5) revenue analysts, or no count, is thin and not read (spec 2026-11-14 6.2, method 1.5). A step at or below 50% is read on any count, since the earnings step that stands in carries the change of margins and is a poor stand-in for revenue growth.
