The count is FMP's revenue analyst count of the landing year alone; the earlier year's is not read. The charge takes the earnings step of that year as resolved, as for a missing revenue step, so an earnings step override moves the charge of that year, and an agent's `revenue.step_2` or `revenue.step_3` still stands over the rule, since an agent's step is never thin. The path keeps the step found beside the steps read (4.8). No flag says a step is thin; `few-analysts` reads the earnings path's EPS count, as above. The Decline route leaves out a consensus year on too few revenue analysts past year 3, by the count alone and whatever its step (6a.2);
- both earnings series are read: where a step has a rate on net income and a rate on EPS more than `step_series_gap_max` (5 points) apart in size, the note `step-series-disagree` names the step, its two rates and the share counts the two series imply for its two years (the net-income mean over the EPS mean) against the diluted count (4.2); a gap of exactly 5 points is silent. The path stays on the series chosen above, and the note names the step's override, `path.step_2` or `path.step_3`, as what decides. A step an override replaced drops out of the note, which names the steps the consensus still gives.

### 5.4 The belief

The growth for years 4 to N, before the fade: the Base Rate Book median ten-year net-income growth for the company's sales class plus 2 points. The sales class is the first class whose upper bound is at or above trailing revenue in current dollars; the bounds are in 2015 dollars, and no index is applied.

### 5.5 The growth path

With belief b and year-3 growth g_3: if g_3 > b, years 4 to 8 fade in five equal steps,

    g_t = b + (g_3 − b) × (8 − t) ÷ 5, for t = 4 … 8,

so g_8 = b, and years 9 to N grow at b. If g_3 ≤ b, years 4 to N grow at b. The runway cuts the path: with N = 5 the path ends at g_5 mid-fade.

Earnings_1 = NTM net income; earnings_t = earnings_{t−1} × (1 + g_t).

### 5.6 The value

Section 4.8 on this path, N by the moat: each year pays for the next year's revenue growth h at the runway return (4.6), held through the hold K and faded to the terminal return (4.7) in year N, which the terminal takes too. The value a share is the value ÷ the count in force: the diluted count, plus any committed shares (5.2, 14.4).

### 5.7 The implied growth

The belief b at which the value equals the price × the count in force (5.6; the price less any value outside the business, 14.4), with both fades, the earnings path's and h's (4.8), recomputed at each trial b and the hold K fixed at the row's own. Solved by a scan from −50% to 200% a year in steps of 1% for the first sign change, then bisection to 1e-7. None when no belief in that range reaches the price. Also solved at each runway and at each return of the sensitivity.

### 5.8 Sensitivities

- By runway: the value at N = 5, 10 and 20 under the row's own charge, its hold K included (at a runway of K + 1 or less, only year N takes T).
- By return, four keys: `runway` (the row's own charge); `0.15` and `0.09`, each as the runway return R with the moat's terminal rule (4.7) applied to it as T and the charge on in every runway year; `ten-year`, the ten-year aggregate the same way, not floored, or none when the aggregate is unavailable or not positive. Each holds the row's own K, so its return holds and fades as the row's does (4.8). At a return key equal to r (`0.09` at the defaults) the charge takes every dollar of revenue growth, so the value is the NTM earnings over r plus the present value of the uncharged margin change, the earnings growth above the revenue growth in the years the two paths differ. The belief enters that value only through those years' figures, so the implied growth at that key is often none and never measures the business's growth; the key is shown for its value a share.

The value-spread flag reads the first three return keys, and separately the three runways, each the business's own value before any value outside it (14.4): it fires when (highest − lowest) ÷ lowest is 30% or more, with a positive lowest value.

### 5.9 EPV, growth share and multiples

- Clean trailing EPS = GAAP trailing diluted EPS − the non-recurring gap, where the gap = GuruFocus EPS trailing − EPS without non-recurring items (nil without both).
- EPV a share = clean trailing EPS ÷ r. It never sets a level. Growth share of price = 1 − EPV ÷ price.
- Trailing P/E reported and clean, forward P/E on NTM EPS; a P/E exists only for a positive EPS.

### 5.10 The expected return over the repricing horizon

H is the repricing horizon in years, `repricing_horizon_years` (3 at the defaults; 4.1).

    income    = (−dividends − buybacks − stock issued) ÷ market cap, trailing flows, FMP's signs
    growth    = ((1 + g_2)(1 + g_3) … (1 + g_{H+1}))^(1/H) − 1, the path's own rates
    repricing = ln(value ÷ price) ÷ H
    total     = income + growth + repricing

The growth term is the geometric mean of the growth over years 2 to H + 1. Past the runway the path grows at g_T, the terminal value's own rate, so a horizon longer than the path never fails: a runway of 4 years gives the rates of years 2 to 4, and a horizon of 6 reads g_T for years 5 to 7. At H = 3 the growth term is ((1 + g_2)(1 + g_3)(1 + g_4))^(1/3) − 1.

Without a positive market cap the income term is nil and the basis says "unavailable".

### 5.11 Flags

In order: `sbc-basis-unknown`, `stock-pay-missing`, `sbc-basis-uncertain`; `path-on-eps`, `few-analysts`, `thin-ntm`, `step-series-disagree`; the ROIC flags of 4.5, the `line-not-presented` flags, then `charge-zeroed`, `return-without-goodwill` or `return-floored`; `growth-above-return`; the trailing flags (`margin-flag`, `equity-raise`, `negative-fcf`, `heavy-capex`, `large-nri`, `consensus-basis-unknown`); `long-runway-high-growth`; `value-spread`. The result puts the assembly's flags, then `share-count-gap` (section 8), and the classifier's checks before these, and keeps the first flag of each code.

`growth-above-return` reads "In year t the charge pays for h of revenue growth at a return of r_t, so the year pays out less than nothing: the owner funds the growth." where the year's h is a revenue step, the consensus's or an agent's (`revenue.step_2`, `revenue.step_3`, 14.4), or the fade from one; "of growth" where it is not (an earnings step standing in, the belief or g_T); naming the first such year, then the count of the rest, "1 more such year." or "k more such years.". `step-series-disagree` names each disagreeing step the consensus still gives and the share counts against the diluted count (5.3). The `heavy-capex` sentence names the return the charge starts at and the newest year's marginal return (8). `consensus-basis-unknown` follows `large-nri` and never fires without it.

The two stock-pay notes (spec 2026-11-13 7.1) say when the stock-pay charge of 5.2 may be wrong; neither moves a figure. `stock-pay-missing` fires when the trailing stock compensation is zero, or one of its periods' is, while a fiscal year on file holds one above zero (4.2): the stored figure may be missing or short, and the charge with it. The sentence names the newest such fiscal year and its figure, "The trailing stock pay is nil while fiscal 2024 shows $69.8 million, so the stored figure may be missing and the stock-pay charge on the consensus nil.", or, for a trailing period at zero, "The stock pay is nil in 1 trailing period while fiscal 2025 shows $1,330.0 million, so the trailing figure may be short, and the stock-pay charge on the consensus with it." `sbc-basis-uncertain` fires when the basis test may misread, with one sentence for each reason that holds. Stock pay is material when its after-tax figure a share, as 5.2 reads it, is `sbc_material_share` (2%) of a street trailing EPS above zero or more. Reason 1: the test reads `includes-sbc` on a material stock pay and `large-nri` is on the row (the non-recurring gap in force, an agent's `earnings.nri_gap` included), so a one-time item may sit in GAAP earnings and hide the gap. Reason 2: the test reads `includes-sbc` on a material stock pay and the trailing effective tax rate, the trailing income tax over the trailing pre-tax income (4.2), is at or below `sbc_basis_tax_share` (0.5) times the statutory rate, so a low tax may close the gap; a pre-tax income at or below zero, or a tax line missing, gives no reading. Reason 3: the test reads `excludes-sbc` on a stock pay above zero and the street trailing EPS lies above the GAAP trailing EPS the test read (as reported, less an agent's correction under `earnings.nri_gap`, 14.4) by `sbc_basis_gap_multiple` (10) times the after-tax stock pay a share or more, so the adjusted figure leaves out far more than stock pay, and the figures cannot say whether it leaves out stock pay too. Each edge fires: 2% exactly, half the rate exactly, 10 times exactly. Each figure a share in the sentences is in the reporting currency (section 8). The two notes show on a valued row, on the refusal "value not positive under the reinvestment charge", and on the refusal "no positive forward anchor" (5.1), which a stock-pay charge can itself cause, right after the place `sbc-basis-unknown` takes; not on the anchor's other refusals, an incomplete NTM consensus and the unit error, which no charge causes, nor on the path's, "consensus earnings turn negative within the path". In a review the two notes read the figures the anchor read, each is silent under the input that decides it, and both are silent under an applied `earnings.ntm`, whose anchor is the guidance: the guided EPS stands in for the consensus less the stock-pay adjustment, so neither stock-pay input moves the value (14.4).

`thin-ntm` (spec 2026-11-13 9 and 9a), word "Thin NTM consensus", a note, says when year 1 of 5.2 still rests on thin evidence; it moves no figure. It fires where a thin period of the NTM window, the four quarters or the two half-years the anchor sums, one with fewer than `analyst_min` (5) EPS analysts or no count, is left as it is: a thin period before F, the last fiscal year the window reaches, which nothing corrects, or a thin period in F where the annual figure of F does not exist (5.2). The sentence counts those periods, then names the thinnest of them, a missing count being the thinnest and of two equal counts the earlier period named, with its period end, then why the annual consensus does not correct it, by the thinnest period's fiscal year, the first reason that holds: the year is before F, "fiscal 2026 is partly reported and takes no annual correction"; F has no annual EPS estimate, "fiscal 2027 has no annual EPS estimate"; F's annual consensus has no EPS analyst count, "the annual consensus of fiscal 2027 has no analyst count", or fewer than 5, "the annual consensus of fiscal 2027 has 4 EPS analysts" ("1 EPS analyst"); else "not every period of fiscal 2027 has an estimate". So: "1 of the 4 periods the NTM EPS sums has fewer than 5 EPS analysts or no count: the thinnest, ending 31 Dec 2026, has 3 analysts, and fiscal 2026 is partly reported and takes no annual correction, so year 1 rests on thin evidence." A missing count reads "has no count". It takes the place right after `few-analysts`, and in a review it is silent under an applied `earnings.ntm`, whose anchor is the guidance (14.4).

`long-runway-high-growth` fires on a 20-year runway when the belief or the implied growth is 10% or more.

### 5.12 Tagged inputs

DATA: the fiscal years on file, price, diluted shares, next earnings date, daily closes, and, on every result of an ADR or of a row with a ratio on file, `adr_ratio` (unit `ratio`, GuruFocus `summary`; `None` for an ADR without one; absent for a direct listing); on every result of a company in another currency at a rate, `fx_rate`, the rate its price and market cap came into the currency at (unit `<code> per USD`, cited to the source of the close, as of the close's date: FMP's history of the pair, `FMP historical-price-eod/light USD<code>`, or, where a pair quote a price refresh stored supplied it, and on every row repriced at a pair's quote (7.4), the quote, `FMP quote USD<code>`), and the RULE input `inflation_differential` (unit `ratio`, cited to the inflation table as of its retrieval date, its note naming both means: "TWD 1.600% against USD 2.120%, the IMF World Economic Outlook October 2026 means of 2027 to 2031"), neither on a USD row; market cap, trailing revenue, operating income, EPS, dividends, buybacks and stock issued; the aggregate ROIC and the runway return (estimates under the intangibles method when invested capital took the as-reported total); the newest tax rate (with its country, year and source); moat score; GuruFocus EPS and EPS without non-recurring items; trailing stock compensation; NTM EPS and NTM net income; the two path steps; street trailing EPS. RULE: r, g_T, the moat class, the margin of safety, the terminal return, the runway years, the belief with its sales class.

Units. The price is the USD quote, unit `USD`; every other money input is in the reporting currency, its unit the code for a total (`USD`, `TWD`) and `<code> a share` for a figure a share, the market cap the one the route read, in the currency. On a company in another currency, r, g_T and the belief are the policy's restated by the differential (section 7), the figures the route read. This holds on every route.

Intermediates. Every result of the route, a refusal too, carries `margin_history`, the ten-year median operating and net margins with their counts of years (section 8), as every route's does (5a.14, 6.5, 6a.10, 6b.6).

The count (spec 2026-11-14 4). The intermediates' `shares` is the count in force, the diluted count plus any committed shares, and under a `shares.committed` override alone `shares_committed` stands beside it, the committed shares; `ntm_net_income` is on the diluted count (5.2). A price refresh rebuilds the path from `ntm_net_income` and solves the implied growth to the price × `shares` (5.7), so it reproduces both. Every route's intermediates carry the two keys alike, a refusal's that keeps `shares` too (6.1, 5a.14, 6a.10, 6b.6).

The one-time item (spec 2026-11-14 5). Under an `earnings.ntm_nri` override alone the intermediates carry `ntm_nri`, the one-time amount a share the anchor took off year 1: the override's figure, or nil under an applied `earnings.ntm`, where nothing came off (5.2). The `anchor` entry's `ntm_eps` is the NTM EPS after it, and `ntm_net_income` that figure on the diluted count, from which a price refresh rebuilds the path, so it reproduces the value. The DATA entries `ntm_eps` and `ntm_net_income` stay on the consensus as found; the override's JUDGMENT entry stands beside them (14.4).

The revenue steps (spec 2026-11-14 6.3). The `charge` entry (4.8) holds `revenue_steps`, the steps the charge read, an agent's `revenue.step_2` or `revenue.step_3` included, from which a price refresh rebuilds the charge, so it reproduces the value under an agent's step; `revenue_steps_found`, the consensus's steps as found, which is the engine's reading of the two inputs (14.4); and `revenue_step_source`, for each of years 2 and 3, `consensus`, `agent`, `thin` (5.3) or `none`. No DATA entry cites a revenue step; an override's JUDGMENT entry stands in place of none (14.4). A result stored before the two keys holds `revenue_steps` alone, the steps as found, since no agent's step could stand in it then.

The NTM anchor's source (spec 2026-11-13 9a). The `anchor` entry holds the raw NTM EPS, the stock-pay adjustment a share and the NTM EPS, then `source`, what the raw figure read (5.2): `periods`, the sum of the window's EPS means, or `annual`, that sum corrected by the annual consensus of F, the last fiscal year the window reaches; `periods_sum`, the sum of the window's EPS means on either source; and `annual`, on the annual source the correction, none on the periods' source: `fiscal_year` F, F's annual EPS mean `eps` and its EPS analyst count `analysts`, `year_sum`, the sum of the EPS means of F's periods, `share`, the window's periods in F over the periods in a year, and `correction`, (`eps` − `year_sum`) × `share`, a share. The DATA entries `ntm_eps` and `ntm_net_income` cite the quarterly consensus on either source. On the annual source their note gives the correction, the share as a reduced fraction: "the periods' sum plus 3/4 of the gap between fiscal 2027's annual consensus and its periods' sum: a period of the NTM window has fewer than 5 EPS analysts or no count"; a window that is F whole reads "fiscal 2027's annual consensus in place of its periods' sum: a period of the NTM window has fewer than 5 EPS analysts or no count". Where assembly holds a note for the entry, the split's on the NTM EPS (4.2), the two notes are joined with "; ", assembly's first. No flag says the anchor is corrected: the entry and the note say it.

### 5.13 Worked numbers

The tests pin these. Terminal multiple at r 9%, g_T 3%, within 0.05: 17.2× at an infinite return, 15.5× (exactly 15.45) at 30%, 13.7× at 15%, 13.2× at 13%, 11.4× at 9%.

The charge (spec 2026-11-09 13): r 9%, g_T 3%, runway return 20%, terminal return 13% (terminal multiple 13.2051), year-1 earnings 100, consensus earnings steps 25% and 20%, revenue steps 15% and 10%, belief 5%, fade 5 years.

- Runway 5: earnings 100, 125, 150, 175.5, 200.07; the growth paid for 15%, 10%, 9%, 8%, then g_T; K = 4, so year 5 alone takes T. Cash by year 25, 62.5, 82.5, 105.3, 153.9; the years' present value 313.8675, the terminal's 1,717.0862, the value 2,030.9538. As 0.9 charged it (the earnings growth, floored at zero): cash 0, 0, 22.5, 52.65, 153.9 and the value 1,871.7834; the floor off alone, the earnings growth charged: −25, 0, 22.5, 52.65, 153.9 and 1,848.8476.
- Runway 10: K = 6; the return 20% in years 1 to 6, then 18.25%, 16.5%, 14.75% and 13%. Cash by year 25, 62.5, 82.5, 105.3, 130.0455, 155.4544, 174.1333, 175.5221, 174.7913, 213.5764; the years' present value 745.0974, the terminal's 1,548.7248, the value 2,293.8222 (2,116.085 as 0.9 charged).
- A missing year-3 revenue step takes the earnings step: on the runway-5 path h is 15%, 20%, then the fade from 20%, so years 3 and 4 pay for 17% and 14%; cash 25, 0, 22.5, 52.65, 153.9 (year 2 pays for 20% at a 20% return), the years' present value 177.6329 and the value 1,894.7191.
- The Mature Stable path of 6.3: 1,315.0993.
- `step-series-disagree`: net income 8.33 to 10.00 and EPS 0.83 to 1.10 on 10.0 shares give 20.0% against 32.5%, a gap of 12.5 points, which fires; 20.0% against 24.0% does not.
- `value.outside` of $12 a share on a route value of $100 (14.4): the value $112, the levels on $112, the implied growth solved to a target of the price less $12.
- `shares.diluted` from 1,000 to 1,060 with an NTM EPS of $5 (14.4): NTM net income 5,300, not 5,000, and the value a share stands but for the stock pay a share.
- `shares.committed` (14.4) on the tests' Mature Growth company, 1,000 m shares at an NTM EPS of $5.90: 60 m committed shares leave NTM net income at 5,900 m and divide the value by 1,060 m, so the value a share is the mechanical one × 1,000 ÷ 1,060, 5.7% lower; with a `shares.diluted` of 1,100 m as well, NTM net income is 6,490 m, the count in force 1,160 m and the value a share the mechanical one × 1,100 ÷ 1,160.

## 5a. The revenue-growth route (High Growth and Young Growth)

Damodaran's revenue DCF on the firm, one route for both phases: year 1 from the quarterly consensus revenue, years 2 and 3 from the annual consensus, the year-3 rate held to year 5 and faded to g_T by year 10; an operating margin that moves in a straight line to the mature peers' median; reinvestment at a sales-to-capital ratio; the free cash flow and the terminal discounted at r; then debt, the minority interest and cash to equity.

Here N is the explicit period, the years forecast one by one before the terminal value: H + F = 10 years whatever the moat, with H = `high_growth_years` (5) and F = `fade_years` (5). C is `margin_convergence_year` (10).

### 5a.1 Which rows, and the refusals

A company the classifier puts in High Growth or Young Growth (3.5, rule 4b), past the guards of 5.1, in its reporting currency (section 7). The result's route is `revenue-growth`; the phase says which of the two it is.

The route's own refusals, in this order:

1. "no trailing revenue": trailing revenue missing or not positive;
2. "no trailing operating income": without it there is no trailing margin to start from (5a.4);
3. "no statutory tax rate": no rate for the newest fiscal year (4.3);
4. "incomplete NTM consensus": no NTM window of 5.2, four consecutive quarters or, for a half-yearly reporter, two consecutive half-years, or a period of it without a revenue mean (5a.2);
5. "no positive consensus revenue" (5a.2);
6. "consensus revenue falls to zero or below within the path" (5a.2);
7. "no target margin: <n> mature peers" (5a.4);
8. "no sales-to-capital" (5a.5);
9. "value not positive under the reinvestment charge" (5a.9).

What a refusal keeps beside the price facts every result carries: refusals 1 to 5 nothing; refusal 6 the path's flags; refusal 7 the rebuild keys and `balance_sheet` beside them (5a.14), the margin the price needs (5a.10) and the path's, the capital build's and the bridge's flags and `peers-from-industry` (5a.13); refusal 8 those flags and no intermediates; refusal 9 every value-side intermediate, the margin the price needs and every flag of 5a.13. A row with neither a target margin nor a sales-to-capital gives refusal 7, its margin the price needs None.

### 5a.2 Year 1 and the path

Year 0 is trailing revenue (4.2). Year 1 is the NTM consensus revenue: the sum of the revenue means of the NTM window, picked as 5.2 picks it (the first consensus periods ending more than seven days after the trailing period end, by period end: four quarters, each ending 80 to 100 days after the one before, or for a half-yearly reporter two half-years 170 to 195 days apart with no consensus row 80 to 100 days from either, else four such quarters), read from the newest stored answer that holds consensus rows (section 2), and from one row per period where that answer gives two within seven days, the row with the larger EPS analyst count (a tie or no count, the later period end). Without such a window, or with a period that lacks a revenue mean, the row is refused "incomplete NTM consensus"; the periods' EPS means are not needed here. An NTM revenue at or below zero is refused "no positive consensus revenue".

    g_1 = NTM revenue ÷ trailing revenue − 1

The base year is 5.3's: the fiscal year that holds most of the NTM window's periods, a tie taking the later. The path reads the annual consensus revenue for the base year and the next two:

- a revenue mean of zero whose revenue analyst count is missing or zero is no estimate;
- step g_2 = revenue(base + 1) ÷ revenue(base) − 1, step g_3 = revenue(base + 2) ÷ revenue(base + 1) − 1; a step is missing when either year has no estimate or the earlier is not positive;
- a missing step holds the rate of the year before: g_2 takes g_1, and g_3 takes g_2 as resolved. The row is flagged `path-held`, naming the fiscal years that left a step missing (a held step's earlier year when it has no estimate or is not positive, its later year when it has no estimate) and the rate each held year took;
- a resolved g_2 or g_3 at or below −100% refuses the row "consensus revenue falls to zero or below within the path";
- a present step whose landing year has fewer than 5 revenue analysts, or no count, flags `few-analysts`. The count is FMP's revenue analyst count. A held step lands on no consensus and is never thin.

### 5a.3 Years 4 to 10

Years 4 to H grow at the held rate: g_3 as resolved, capped at the sales class's 90th-percentile five-year sales growth (the class as 5.4 picks it from trailing revenue; the cap is the class's `sales_p90_5y`), or the rate the implied-growth solve tries (5a.10), which the cap does not touch. When g_3 is above the cap, the row is flagged `consensus-above-base-rate` naming both rates and the class, and `revenue_path.hold` carries the cap so a price refresh rebuilds the same path; the consensus steps g_2 and g_3 are not capped. Years H + 1 to N fade from the held rate to g_T in F equal steps,

    g_t = g_T + (held rate − g_T) × (N − t) ÷ F, for t = H + 1 … N,

so g_N = g_T exactly. With H = 5 and F = 5, years 4 and 5 hold and years 6 to 10 fade. A held rate below g_T rises to g_T on the same line.

    revenue_1 = NTM revenue
    revenue_t = revenue_{t−1} × (1 + g_t), for t = 2 … N

### 5a.4 The margin path and the target margin

The margin starts at the trailing operating margin, m_0 = trailing operating income ÷ trailing revenue, and moves in a straight line to the target margin m*, which it reaches in year C and keeps:

    m_t = m_0 + (m* − m_0) × min(t, C) ÷ C, for t = 1 … N

A trailing margin above the target moves down the same line.

**The dollar ramp.** The margin path stands for a trailing margin m_0 at or above −100% (`loss_ramp_margin_bound`). Below it the loss is larger than revenue, a cost base rather than a margin, and operating income moves instead in dollars, in a straight line, from the trailing operating income to the target margin's share of revenue in year C, as the Start-up ramp of 6b.2 does:

    E_0 = m_0 × revenue_0, the trailing operating income
    E* = m* × revenue_C
    EBIT_t = E_0 + (E* − E_0) × t ÷ C, for t = 1 … C
    EBIT_t = m* × revenue_t, for t = C + 1 … N

When C is past N, revenue_C is not on the path: E* is m* × revenue_N, reached at year C, so year N's margin is below the target, as the margin path's is. Each year's margin is EBIT_t ÷ revenue_t, and `margin_path.by_year` carries it. Tax, the carry, NOPAT, reinvestment, the terminal and the bridge are 5a.5 to 5a.9's on these EBITs. The row records `margin_path.ramp`, "dollars" on the ramp and "margin" on the margin path, cites `loss_ramp_margin_bound` as a RULE input, and is flagged `margin-ramp-in-dollars`, "The trailing loss is larger than revenue (margin −150.0%), so operating income moves in dollars from −$15,000.0 million to the target margin's $7,034.3 million by year 10, as the Start-up ramp does." On a row in another currency (section 7) the ramp moves in that currency and the sentence names it in place of the dollars: "moves in SEK from −SEK 15,000.0 million to the target margin's SEK 7,034.3 million". A trailing margin of exactly −100% keeps the margin path.

**The mature peers.** The candidates are the symbols of the latest usable `stock-peers` response stored for the company (section 2), in its order, in the exchange form (`BRK.B`), the company itself and repeats dropped. A candidate is kept when the latest universe lists it (the admitted companies of the newest refresh that admitted any) and its stored fiscal years classify, on the spot, as Mature Growth or Mature Stable on the deciles the company is valued on (3.3). A candidate outside that universe, without statements, unclassified or in another phase is dropped. Without deciles the company has no peers, and without a stored response no candidates. No vendor call is made for a peer: its figures come from the statements stored for it, its invested capital and NOPAT built by 4.4 at the statutory rate of its own country (4.3):

- its margin: the ten-year aggregate operating margin, Σ operating income ÷ Σ revenue over the last ten of its fiscal years that have both and a positive revenue, with the count of those years; none without such a year;
- its sales-to-capital: the revenue of its newest kept ROIC year (4.5) ÷ that year's average invested capital, both of the same fiscal year; none without a kept year, without a revenue for that year, or with that capital at or below zero;
- its ROIC: its ten-year aggregate (4.5), none when unavailable;
- its market cap: the one on its row in the latest universe.

**The industry fallback.** When the candidates kept leave fewer than `peer_min` peers with a margin on `peer_margin_min_years` years or more, the peers are instead the mature companies of the company's own FMP industry: every company of the latest universe whose row carries the same industry, the company itself excluded, whose fiscal years classify on the spot as Mature Growth or Mature Stable on the same deciles, the largest `industry_peer_max` of them by market cap (a company without one last, ties in symbol order), their figures read as above. The kept candidates are dropped, not merged. The row is flagged `peers-from-industry`, naming the count the candidates left, the industry and the count taken, whether the row is then valued or still refused for the target margin or for its sales-to-capital; such a row also cites `industry_peer_max` as a RULE input. A company without an industry on its universe row has no fallback. Without a stored response the candidates are none and the fallback applies.

**The target margin.** m* is the median margin of the peers with a margin on at least 5 fiscal years (`peer_margin_min_years`). With fewer than 3 such peers (`peer_min`), the row is refused "no target margin: <n> mature peers", n counting them. The row records those peers with their margins and market caps, their count, the lowest and the highest margin, and the largest of them by market cap with its margin (none when none has a market cap); the largest peer sets nothing.

### 5a.5 Sales-to-capital and reinvestment

Sales-to-capital is the revenue a dollar of new invested capital buys. The route takes, in this order:

1. **The company's own five-year marginal measure**, over the kept ROIC years of 4.5: (revenue_last − revenue_first) ÷ (capital_last − capital_first), "last" the newest kept year and "first" the kept year 5 positions earlier (`marginal_return_span_years`), capital the average invested capital, revenue that fiscal year's. Used when it is usable: capital grew, by at least a fifth of the first year's capital in size (`marginal_capital_growth_min`), and revenue grew, and the measure is at least half the mature peers' median of step 2 (`own_sales_to_capital_min_share`, 0.5) when that median exists. With no peers' median the own measure stands on the first two conditions. A measure below the bound is not usable and the peers' median is used; the row cites `own_sales_to_capital_min_share` as a RULE input. There is no own measure with fewer than six kept years, with either year lacking a revenue, or with no change in capital.
2. **The mature peers' median**: the median of the positive sales-to-capital among all the peers of 5a.4, when at least 3 (`peer_min`) are positive.
3. Neither: the row is refused "no sales-to-capital".

The row records the measure used ("own" or "peers", none when neither stands), the own measure and whether it is usable, whether the bound alone rejected it (`own_below_bound`), the peers' median and the value used.

Reinvestment in year t pays for year t + 1's revenue; year N pays for the terminal growth at the terminal return (5a.7), as the mature routes' year N does:

    reinvestment_t = max(0, (revenue_{t+1} − revenue_t) ÷ sales-to-capital), for t = 1 … N − 1
    reinvestment_N = terminal NOPAT × g_T ÷ terminal return

A year before a fall in revenue reinvests nothing, never a negative amount; year N has no floor. The terminal NOPAT is year N's at the full tax rate (5a.7).

### 5a.6 Tax and NOPAT

    EBIT_t = revenue_t × m_t, or on the dollar ramp 5a.4's EBIT_t

The tax rate is the newest fiscal year's statutory rate (4.3), in every year. Tax is charged after the forecast's own losses, the carried loss starting at zero: a year whose EBIT is below zero pays no tax and adds its size to the carried loss; any other year pays

    tax_t = max(0, EBIT_t − carried loss) × tax rate

and reduces the carried loss by EBIT_t, not below zero.

    NOPAT_t = EBIT_t − tax_t

No history of tax losses is stored, so a forecast with a year whose EBIT is below zero, or a trailing margin m_0 below zero (a trailing loss implies losses the store does not hold), is flagged `tax-loss-history-unknown`.

### 5a.7 The free cash flow, the terminal and the firm value

    FCFF_t = NOPAT_t − reinvestment_t, for t = 1 … N
    terminal NOPAT = EBIT_N × (1 − tax rate) when EBIT_N > 0, else EBIT_N
    terminal value = terminal NOPAT × (1 + g_T) × (1 − g_T ÷ terminal return) ÷ (r − g_T)
    firm value = Σ_{t=1..N} FCFF_t ÷ (1 + r)^t + terminal value ÷ (1 + r)^N

The terminal multiple is 4.8's. A negative free cash flow is the method, not a refusal. The terminal value and reinvestment_N read year N's operating income at the full statutory rate, untaxed when it is not positive: a carried loss that outlasts year N − 1 shields year N's own NOPAT, and so FCFF_N, but never the perpetuity. When the carry ends before year N, as in the worked numbers, the terminal NOPAT is NOPAT_N.

The terminal return is 4.7's moat rule on the return on new capital at the target margin:

    return on new capital = m* × (1 − tax rate) × sales-to-capital

Narrow or None moat: r. Wide moat: that return, capped at 4.7's cap (r plus the spread, 13% at the defaults) and never below r. The runway return of 4.6 does not enter this route's charge; the cross-check reads it (5a.11).

### 5a.8 The required return

r in every year and in the terminal. Under the band of every valued row on this route the drawer says so: "Discounted at the required return of <r> in every year; Damodaran's template starts higher and falls to it by year 10".

### 5a.9 Equity and the value a share

    equity = firm value − debt − minority interest + cash
    value a share = equity ÷ the count in force

The count in force is the diluted share count (4.2), plus any committed shares (14.4).

From the newest balance sheet (4.2), a quarter's or a fiscal year's: debt is short-term plus long-term debt (`shortTermDebt`, `longTermDebt`), cash is cash and equivalents plus short-term investments (`cashAndCashEquivalents`, `shortTermInvestments`). No other line enters. Each input's note names the sheet's period, "the balance sheet of fiscal 2026 Q3". A line without a value counts as nil, and assembly's note on the input names it after the period ("the balance sheet of fiscal 2026 Q3; longTermDebt not presented"); a figure with both its lines missing is unknown and counts as nil. Either case flags `line-not-presented:debt` or `line-not-presented:cash`, the sentence naming the sheet's period and the missing lines: "The newest balance sheet (fiscal 2026 Q3) does not present all of the short- and long-term debt (longTermDebt not presented); what is missing counts as nil in the bridge from firm value to equity." The period alone raises no flag. Without a balance sheet on file both figures are unknown and the sentence names no period.

The minority interest is the same sheet's `minorityInterest` line: the share of the company's subsidiaries that outside owners hold, at book (spec 2026-11-13 13). A market value would be the better figure where one can be estimated, as Damodaran takes it; the book figure is the mechanical floor, and an agent sets another through `balance.minority` (14.4). A sheet that does not present the line, a figure at or below zero, or no balance sheet on file counts as nil, with no flag: most companies have none. The route cites the line as the sheet presents it, the DATA input `minority_interest` (empty where the sheet does not present it, a figure at or below zero as it stands), and the intermediate `minority_interest` holds the figure in force. The Mature Growth and Mature Stable routes do not read it: their earnings are after the minority's share.

A value a share at or below zero refuses the row "value not positive under the reinvestment charge", which keeps its figures (5a.1). Otherwise the levels, the designation and the validity are 4.10's, with the moat's margin of safety.

### 5a.10 What moves with the price

- **The implied growth**: the held rate of years 4 to H at which the value a share equals the price (the price less any value outside the business, 14.4), the fade recomputed from it; years 1 to 3, the margin path, the terminal return and the sales-to-capital stay. On the dollar ramp (5a.4) each trial rebuilds the ramp from the trial path's revenue_C. Solved as 5.7 solves: a scan from −50% to 200% in steps of 1% for the first sign change, then bisection to 1e-7; None when the scan finds no sign change.
- **The margin the price needs**: the target margin at which the value a share equals the price (less any value outside the business, as above), the margin path, or the dollar ramp, rebuilt from it and the terminal return re-read at each trial from the return on new capital there (5a.7). Solved the same way between the policy's bounds, −50% and 100% (`implied_margin_bounds`); None without a sales-to-capital or when the scan finds no sign change. It is computed on a valued row and on the two refusals that keep the rebuild keys, "no target margin" and "value not positive".
- **The multiples and the growth share** of 5.9: trailing P/E reported and clean; the forward P/E on the anchor's NTM EPS (5.2) when the anchor stands, none when it refuses; a P/E only for a positive EPS; the growth share of price when EPV exists.
- **The expected return** of 5.10, its growth term on the revenue path's own rates over the repricing horizon, g_T past year N.
- **The cross-check's implied growth** (5a.11).

The rebuild keys (5a.14), with the policy's r, g_T, margin convergence year and `loss_ramp_margin_bound` (and, for the margin solve, its bounds and the wide-moat cap), rebuild the path, the margin path or the dollar ramp, and the charge, so a price refresh moves each of these figures from the intermediates alone (section 10). `margin_start` and `revenue_path` give the ramp's E_0 and revenue_C; `debt`, `cash`, `minority_interest` and `shares` give the bridge to equity of 5a.9 at each trial.

### 5a.11 Sensitivities, the cross-check and the implied-return check

- **By target margin**: the value a share at the lowest, the median and the highest margin of the counted peers, keys `low`, `median`, `high`, each with its own terminal return from the return on new capital at that margin, and on the dollar ramp its own ramp to that margin. `median` is the row's value. The value-spread flag reads the three on 5.8's rule, with one addition: when the value at the lowest margin is at or below zero and the median's is positive, where 5.8's rule is silent, the flag fires with "The value is at or below zero at the lowest mature-peer margin of <x.x%> and <y.yy> a share at the median, so the spread across the peers' margins is unbounded."
- **The growth-route cross-check**: as on a Mature Stable row (6.4), `growth_route` carries the Mature Growth route's value a share under the row's own moat and returns, with the keys that rebuild it and its implied growth at the price, or only the reason that route would refuse. It sets nothing.
- **The implied-return check**: the return on new capital at the target against the median ten-year aggregate ROIC of the peers of 5a.4 that have one. Above that median, the row is flagged `return-above-peers` with both figures. With no peer ROIC there is no check. It flags only.

### 5a.12 Young Growth

The same route: on the same inputs a Young Growth row has High Growth's value, levels and figures. Its intermediates add `failure_probability`, with no value and the reason "no survival table fits a listed company above $2 billion"; the value is the going-concern value, flagged `failure-not-charged`. The drawer shows the failure probability as unavailable with that reason, and says the value range is not computed in tier 1.

### 5a.13 Flags

In order: `sbc-basis-unknown`, when the cross-check has a value and its anchor's stock-compensation basis is unknown (5.2); the path's, `path-held`, `consensus-above-base-rate` (5a.3), then `few-analysts`; the capital build's, the ROIC flags of 4.5 then the `line-not-presented:<field>` flags of the ROIC years (4.4); `line-not-presented:debt`, then `line-not-presented:cash`; `sheet-shift` (section 8), on a row refused for its target margin or its sales-to-capital too; `peers-from-industry` (5a.4); `tax-loss-history-unknown` (5a.6); `margin-ramp-in-dollars` on the dollar ramp (5a.4); `return-above-peers`; `failure-not-charged` on a Young Growth row; the trailing flags (`margin-flag`, `equity-raise`, `negative-fcf`, `heavy-capex`, `large-nri`); `value-spread` over the three target margins (5a.11). The result puts the assembly's flags, then `share-count-gap` (section 8), and the classifier's checks before these, and keeps the first flag of each code.

Never raised here: `path-on-eps`, `charge-zeroed`, `return-without-goodwill`, `return-floored`, `growth-above-return`, `long-runway-high-growth`, nor the cross-check's earnings-path flags. The charge does not read the runway return, and the path is on revenue.

### 5a.14 Intermediates and tagged inputs

Intermediates: `ntm_revenue`; `path` (base_year, the revenue analysts of the two landing years, which steps were held, the sales class and its cap); `path_steps`, g_2 and g_3 as resolved; `growth_rates`, years 1 to N; `margin_path` (start, target, by_year, ramp, "margin" or "dollars"); `target_margin` (value, count, low, high, largest_symbol, largest_margin, the counted peers with each one's margin and market cap, source (fmp or industry) and industry); `revenue_by_year`, `ebit_by_year`, `tax_by_year`, `nopat_by_year`, `reinvestment_by_year`, `fcff_by_year`; `terminal_roic`, `terminal_nopat` (5a.7), `terminal_value`, `firm_value`, `equity`, `value_per_share`; `balance_sheet`, the newest balance sheet that debt, cash and the minority interest were read from (its period end, label and whether it is a quarter's; none without a sheet); `value_by_margin`; `return_on_new_capital`, `peer_roic_median`; `failure_probability` on Young Growth only; `eps`, `epv`, `dividend_yield`, `net_buyback_yield`, `income_basis`, `price_facts`, `margin_history` (section 8), `growth_route`; the rebuild keys; and at the price `implied_growth`, `implied_margin`, `multiples`, `growth_share`, `expected_return` and the cross-check's `implied_growth`.

The forward EPS of `eps`, and the forward P/E of `multiples` on it, and the cross-check's `growth_route` read the anchor of 5.2 on either source: on a thin window, the periods' sum corrected by the annual consensus of the last fiscal year the window reaches, where that consensus corrects it (spec 2026-11-13 9a). The route records no `anchor` entry and cites no NTM EPS; its value rests on the revenue path, whose year 1 is the sum of the window's revenue means.

The rebuild keys: `revenue_path` (revenue0, ntm_revenue, the consensus steps as found with None for a missing one, hold None, or the cap when it binds, high_growth_years, fade_years), `margin_start`, `tax_rate`, `moat`, `debt`, `cash`, `minority_interest` (the figure in force, 5a.9), `shares` and `sales_to_capital` (used, own, own_usable, own_below_bound, peers, value); on a valued row `margin_path`'s target and `terminal_roic` complete them. A refusal that keeps them keeps the moat class for the price refresh, and `balance_sheet` beside `debt` and `cash`, which rebuilds nothing; the screen shows the moat on a valued row only.

Tagged inputs. DATA: the fiscal years on file, price, diluted shares, next earnings date, daily closes, `adr_ratio`, `fx_rate` and `inflation_differential` as 5.12; market cap, trailing revenue, operating income, EPS, dividends, buybacks and stock issued; the aggregate ROIC and the runway return, as in 5.12, though neither enters this route's charge; the newest tax rate; the moat score; GuruFocus EPS and EPS without non-recurring items; `ntm_revenue`, from the quarterly consensus; `path_step_2` and `path_step_3`, the annual consensus steps as found, a held one empty with the note "no consensus step: the year holds the rate of the year before"; `target_margin`, source "FMP stock-peers and the peers' statements" (on the industry fallback, "the universe's <industry> industry and the peers' statements", the note naming the industry), note "the median ten-year operating margin of <n> mature peers"; `sales_to_capital`, either the own measure cited to the statements (an estimate under the intangibles method when invested capital took the as-reported total, 4.4) with the note "the company's own five-year marginal measure", or the peers' median cited to the peers' source with the note "the mature peers' median; the own measure is not usable", or, when the bound of 5a.5 alone rejected the own measure, "the mature peers' median; the own measure, 0.50, is below half the peers' median, 2.50" (on the industry fallback, "the mature peers' median (<industry> industry); " and the same reason); `debt` and `cash`, cited to the newest balance sheet (`balance_sheet`, as of its period end, 4.2), each noted with the sheet's period and, after it, assembly's note naming a missing line (5a.9); `minority_interest`, the sheet's line as it presents it, cited and noted with its period as `debt` is (5a.9). RULE: r, g_T, the moat class, the margin of safety, the terminal return, `high_growth_years`, `held_growth_cap` with the class and whether it binds in its note, `fade_years`, `margin_convergence_year`, `loss_ramp_margin_bound` on a row on the dollar ramp, `peer_min`, `own_sales_to_capital_min_share` on a row whose own measure the bound rejected, and on a row whose peers are the industry fallback's, `industry_peer_max`.

### 5a.15 Worked numbers

The tests pin these, to 0.01 unless a tolerance is named. r 9%, g_T 3%, tax 21%, None moat (terminal return 9%); trailing revenue 1,000, NTM revenue 1,300, consensus steps 25% and 20%; trailing margin −10%, target margin 20%; sales-to-capital 2.0; debt 100, cash 300, 100 shares.

- Growth, years 1 to 10: 30%, 25%, 20%, 20%, 20%, 16.6%, 13.2%, 9.8%, 6.4%, 3%.
- Revenue: 1,300.00; 1,625.00; 1,950.00; 2,340.00; 2,808.00; 3,274.13; 3,706.31; 4,069.53; 4,329.98; 4,459.88.
- Margins: −7%, −4%, −1%, 2%, 5%, 8%, 11%, 14%, 17%, 20%.
- Tax on EBIT of −91.00; −65.00; −19.50; 46.80; 140.40; 261.93; 407.69; 569.73; 736.10; 891.98: 0; 0; 0; 0; 2.46; 55.01; 85.62; 119.64; 154.58; 187.32. NOPAT is −91.00 in year 1 and 137.94 in year 5. The carried loss of 175.50 is used up in year 5, so the terminal NOPAT, 891.98 × 0.79 = 704.66, is year 10's NOPAT.
- The carry: EBIT of 100, −50, 80, 10 at a rate of 25% pays 25, 0, 7.5, 2.5.
- Reinvestment: 162.50; 162.50; 195.00; 234.00; 233.06; 216.09; 181.61; 130.23; 64.95; 234.89, year 10 at the terminal return.
- Free cash flow −253.50 in year 1 and 469.77 in year 10; terminal value 8,064.46; firm value 3,290.57; equity 3,490.57; value a share 34.91. A minority interest of 50 takes 0.50 off: equity 3,440.57, value a share 34.41.
- Value a share at target margins of 10% and 30%: 10.09 and 59.64; at a sales-to-capital of 3.0: 38.50; at a terminal return of 13%: 40.45.
- From a trailing margin of −50% the losses outlast year 9: year 10 pays no tax on its EBIT of 891.98, so its NOPAT is 891.98 and its free cash flow 657.09, while the terminal NOPAT is 704.66, reinvestment_10 234.89 and the terminal value 8,064.46; value a share 9.32. At a target margin of −5%, EBIT_10 is −222.99 and the terminal reads it untaxed.
- Return on new capital at the target: 0.20 × 0.79 × 2.0 = 0.316.
- At a price of 25.00, within 1e-5: implied growth 0.086275, margin the price needs 0.160014. At a price of 34.9057 both solves return 0.20 within 1e-4. With a Wide moat at 40.45 the margin solve returns 0.20 within 1e-3, the terminal at the 13% cap; at a 5% target the return on new capital is 7.9%, below r, so the terminal is r, and, with cash 1,000 (at the stated cash of 300 the value at a 5% target is below zero), the solve at that target's value returns 0.05 within 1e-5.
- The dollar ramp from a trailing margin of −150% (operating income −1,500), below the bound: EBIT −1,260.80; −1,021.60; −782.41; −543.21; −304.01; −64.81; 174.38; 413.58; 652.78; 891.98, the margins from −97.0% in year 1 to 20% in year 10. The carried loss shields every year through year 10 (NOPAT_10 891.98, untaxed); the terminal NOPAT is 704.66. Free cash flow −1,423.30 in year 1 and 657.09 in year 10; terminal value 8,064.46; firm value −31.33; equity 168.67; value a share 1.6867 within 1e-4. The margin path from −150% would give −61.94. At a price of 25.00 the margin the price needs is 0.277164 within 1e-5. With C at 5, years 5 to 10 earn the target on each year's revenue; with C at 12, year 10 is ten twelfths of the way to 20% of year 10's revenue.
- Own sales-to-capital, kept years with revenue and average capital from (1,000, 400) to (2,500, 900) five positions later: 1,500 ÷ 500 = 3.0, usable. Capital from 400 to 420 instead: 75.0, not usable, capital grew 5%. Falling revenue: not usable. Five kept years, or a year without revenue: none. Against a peers' median of 2.5, an own measure of 0.5 is below the bound of 1.25 and the median is used; 1.3 is used; 0.5 with no peers' median is used.
- Peers with margins of 10% on 10 years, 30% on 8, 20% on 6 and 40% on 3: target 20% on 3 peers, low 10%, high 30%, the largest by market cap the 30% peer; two peers give none. Peers' sales-to-capital 2.5, 1.5 and 3.0: median 2.5; two give none. Peers' ROIC 12%, 20% and 15%: median 15%.

The route's tests pin the refusal order, the flag order, the `path-held` and `few-analysts` sentences, Young Growth's equality with High Growth, and a price refresh that rebuilds every price-side figure from the intermediates.

## 6. The Mature Stable route

The stable-growth value on normalized trailing earnings, charged as the Mature Growth route charges.

### 6.1 Refusal

A company past the guards of 5.1, in its reporting currency (section 7). E = trailing net income − the non-recurring gap × diluted shares, the diluted count of 4.2, an agent's `shares.diluted` in its place; the cycle gap a share comes off on the same count (14.4). An agent's committed shares (`shares.committed`, 14.4) do not move E, a total already: every value a share of the route, EPV a share and the cross-check's (6.4) included, divides by the count in force, the diluted count plus them, so x committed shares on a count of n lower the value a share by x ÷ (n + x). A missing or non-positive E refuses: "no positive trailing earnings". The refusal keeps the growth-route cross-check of 6.4, with its value a share and its implied growth at the price or only its reason, and the intermediates `e_trailing` (E, or none when net income is missing), `nri_gap`, `eps`, `shares`, `moat` and `price_facts`. It raises no flag of its own and cites no input beyond the ones every result carries.

A value a share, plus any value outside the business (14.4), at or below zero refuses "value not positive under the reinvestment charge", which keeps the value side, the flags and the cross-check with its implied growth at the price less that figure. The mechanical value of a positive E, its returns at r or above, is always positive.

### 6.2 Normalized earnings

When the margin flag (8) fires and both the trailing operating margin and the ten-year median margin are positive, E is scaled by median margin ÷ trailing margin, and the row says so. Otherwise E stands.

### 6.3 The value

The path grows at g_T in every year from E (year 1 is E, with no (1 + g_T) factor) to the moat's runway N. The value is section 4.8 on that path with the row's charge: h = g_T in every year, and no year grows above the belief g_T, so the hold K is 0 and the return fades in a straight line from the runway return in year 1 to the terminal return in year N and the terminal, R + (T − R) × t ÷ N in year t; off in the runway years when 4.6 turns it off. When the runway return equals the terminal return with the charge on, this equals the one-stage formula E × (1 − g_T ÷ terminal return) ÷ (r − g_T). A row floored at r in every year values at EPV. A runway return above the terminal return, or a charge off, lifts the value, and more for a longer runway. Worked (spec 2026-11-09 13.3): E 100, R 20%, T 13%, N 10, the return 19.3%, 18.6%, 17.9% … 13%; cash 84.456, 86.3871, 88.3096, 90.2135, 92.0871, 93.9159, 95.6823, 97.365, 98.9375, 100.3672; the value 1,315.0993 (1,335.796 with R held to year 9, as 0.9 charged); EPV 1,111.11.

EPV = E ÷ r is shown as the no-growth value. The levels sit on the stable-growth value.

### 6.4 The growth-route cross-check

On a valued row and on the refusal of 6.1: when the row's inputs allow the Mature Growth route (a positive anchor, a path without refusal), the intermediates carry that route's value a share under the row's own moat and returns, with the keys that rebuild its path and its charge (`charge`, 4.8, its hold read on the cross-check's own path), and its implied growth at the price. When the growth route would refuse, only its reason is carried. The levels and the designation never read it.

The cross-check reads the consensus revenue steps as found and no agent's (`revenue.step_2`, `revenue.step_3`, 14.4), as it reads no agent's earnings step, and leaves a thin step out as the route does (5.3): its `charge` holds the steps found, and as read the same steps but none where a step is thin, each year's source `consensus`, `thin` or `none`. So the rule moves the cross-check of a Mature Stable row and of a revenue-growth row (5a.11) as it moves the Mature Growth value.

The cross-check's anchor is 5.2's on either source: on a thin window it reads the periods' sum corrected by the annual consensus of the last fiscal year the window reaches, where that consensus corrects it (spec 2026-11-13 9a), so its value a share and its `ntm_net_income` follow the corrected figure while the row's value does not. It records no `anchor` entry, and cites no NTM EPS.

The cross-check reads the two counts as the Mature Growth route does (5.2): its `ntm_net_income` and the stock pay a share on the diluted count, its value a share on the count in force, and its implied growth on the price × the row's `shares`. So committed shares (14.4) lower it by the same share as the route's value, on a Mature Stable row and on a revenue-growth row alike (5a.11).

### 6.5 Expected return, multiples, flags and inputs

The expected return of 5.10 with H rates of g_T. Trailing P/E reported and clean; no forward P/E. Flags: the ROIC flags of 4.5 and 4.6, `charge-zeroed`, `return-without-goodwill` and `return-floored` included, then the trailing flags, the `heavy-capex` sentence naming the return the charge starts at and `consensus-basis-unknown` following `large-nri` as on Mature Growth (8). The result puts the assembly's flags, then `share-count-gap` (section 8), and the classifier's checks before these, and keeps the first flag of each code. Never `sbc-basis-unknown`, `path-on-eps`, `few-analysts`, `step-series-disagree`, `growth-above-return`, `long-runway-high-growth` or `value-spread`; so a thin consensus behind the cross-check does not show on a stable row, a known gap. Inputs: the common DATA inputs of 5.12 plus trailing net income; the RULE inputs plus the runway years and a belief of g_T. Intermediates: `margin_history` (section 8), a refusal's too, beside the route's own `median_margin` (6.2), which the route reads and which is the same ten-year median operating margin.

## 6a. The Decline route

Damodaran's run-off on the firm: years 1 to 3 from the consensus revenue, then the consensus revenue where it reaches past year 3 on enough analysts, then the five-year trend's decline rate fading to zero by year N; one normalized operating margin in every year; the capital a fall in revenue releases, recovered in part; a flat terminal on year N's revenue; then debt, the minority interest and cash to equity, the net current asset value less the minority interest as a floor, and the distress-sale value weighted by the ten-year probability of default of a synthetic rating.

Here N is `runoff_years` (10) whatever the moat; K is the last year the consensus gives (6a.2); d is the decline rate (6a.2); m_n the normalized margin (6a.3).

### 6a.1 Which rows, and the refusals

A company the classifier puts in Decline (3.5 rule 2), past the guards of 5.1, in its reporting currency (section 7). The result's route is `decline`. Its NTM window is 5.2's, as on the revenue-growth route: four consecutive quarters, or two consecutive half-years for a half-yearly reporter (6a.2).

The route's own refusals, in this order:

1. "no trailing revenue": trailing revenue missing or not positive;
2. "no trailing operating income";
3. "no statutory tax rate": no rate for the newest fiscal year (4.3);
4. "consensus revenue falls to zero or below within the path" (6a.2);
5. "no sales-to-capital" (6a.4);
6. "value not positive in the run-off" (6a.7).

What a refusal keeps beside the price facts every result carries: refusals 1 to 3 nothing; refusal 4 the path's flags; refusal 5 the path's flags and `line-not-presented:debt`, `:cash` and `:totalLiabilities`, and no intermediates; refusal 6 every intermediate of 6a.10 but the three at the price, every flag, and its inputs, the tagged inputs a valued row cites (6a.10).

### 6a.2 The revenue path

Year 0 is trailing revenue (4.2). Years 1 to 3 are 5a.2's consensus path: year 1 the NTM consensus revenue, g_1 = NTM revenue ÷ trailing revenue − 1; years 2 and 3 the annual consensus revenue steps from the base year, a missing step holding the rate of the year before (`path-held`, with 5a.2's sentence), a present step landing on a year with fewer than 5 revenue analysts, or no count, flagged `few-analysts`. Nothing is capped: 5a.3's cap is the growth route's.

**Past year 3** the path keeps reading the annual consensus revenue, one fiscal year at a time from base + 3, each step from the year before's consensus revenue, while the year has a revenue mean above zero and at least `analyst_min` (5) revenue analysts. The first year that fails either test ends the consensus at the year before, with no refusal and no flag: nothing is held past year 3, a thin year is not read, so `few-analysts` never names a year past 3, and a year at or below zero is not read, so no step past year 3 falls 100% or more. No step runs from a base + 2 without a consensus revenue above zero, so a year 3 held for want of base + 2 ends the consensus at year 3 (a year 3 held for want of base + 1 still reads year 4 from base + 2). K is the last year the consensus gives, 3 at least and N at most: a year past N is not read. Each step past year 3 is cited as a DATA input (6a.10).

A resolved g_2 or g_3 at or below −100% refuses the row "consensus revenue falls to zero or below within the path".

**The fade.** From year K + 1 the rate falls in a straight line from the decline rate d to zero by year N:

    d = max(−50%, min(0, five-year revenue slope ÷ revenue of the last fiscal year))
    g_t = d × (N − t) ÷ (N − K), for t = K + 1 … N
    revenue_t = revenue_{t−1} × (1 + g_t)

so g_N = 0. With K = 3 and N = 10, g_4 = d × 6 ÷ 7 and the path reaches zero in seven steps; a consensus to year N leaves no year to fade.

The slope is the classifier's revenue trend (3.4), in dollars a year. A trend that shrinks revenue faster than 50% a year takes −50%, the lower bound of the implied-decline scan (4.1a), and the row is flagged `decline-rate-floored`, "The five-year trend shrinks revenue faster than 50% a year; the run-off uses −50%."

An incomplete NTM consensus (5a.2: no NTM window of 5.2, or a period of it without a revenue mean) or an NTM revenue at or below zero does not refuse: years 1 to 3 shrink at d, the annual consensus is not read, and the fade runs from year 4 as with K = 3. The row is flagged `path-on-trend`, "No usable consensus: years 1 to 3 shrink at the decline rate, <d>, which fades to zero by year <N>."

A consensus rate above zero in years 1 to K, as found, flags `consensus-recovery`, naming each such year and its rate: "The consensus has revenue growing <g> in year <t> while the decline rate is <d>." A held step is not the consensus's and is not named.

Both sentences name the decline rate as used, signed (−4.0%): on a floored row −50%, not the trend's own figure.

### 6a.3 Margin, tax and NOPAT

The operating margin held in every year and in the terminal is the normalized margin m_n: the median of the operating margins (3.4) of the last five fiscal years that have one, when at least three do; else the trailing margin m_0 = trailing operating income ÷ trailing revenue.

    EBIT_t = revenue_t × m_n

Tax and NOPAT by 5a.6, the carried loss starting at zero, flagged `tax-loss-history-unknown` when a year's EBIT is below zero or m_0 is below zero. The `margin-flag` (section 8) reads m_0, as on every route. The row records the margin used, which one ("median" or "trailing"), the median (none with fewer than three margins), m_0, and the fiscal years and margins read.

### 6a.4 Capital released, reinvestment and sales-to-capital

    release_t = max(0, (revenue_{t−1} − revenue_t) ÷ sales-to-capital) × runoff_recovery
    reinvestment_t = max(0, (revenue_t − revenue_{t−1}) ÷ sales-to-capital)
    FCFF_t = NOPAT_t − reinvestment_t + release_t, for t = 1 … N

A year whose revenue falls releases capital, recovered at `runoff_recovery` (0.6); a year whose revenue rises reinvests in full. Both fall in the year of the change.

Sales-to-capital, in this order:

1. **Own-latest**: the revenue of the company's newest kept ROIC year (4.5) ÷ that year's average invested capital, as 5a.4 measures a peer's. None without a kept year, without a revenue for that fiscal year, or with that capital at or below zero.
2. **The mature peers' median** of 5a.5 step 2, the peers of 5a.4 with their industry fallback. When the industry's peers give it, and only then, the row is flagged `peers-from-industry`.
3. Neither: the row is refused "no sales-to-capital".

The row records the measure used ("own-latest" or "peers") and its value.

### 6a.5 The terminal and the going-concern value

    revenue_{N+1} = revenue_N
    NOPAT_{N+1} = revenue_N × m_n × (1 − tax rate) when m_n is above zero, else revenue_N × m_n
    FCFF_{N+1} = NOPAT_{N+1}
    terminal value_N = FCFF_{N+1} ÷ r
    firm value = Σ_{t=1..N} FCFF_t ÷ (1 + r)^t + terminal value_N ÷ (1 + r)^N
    going-concern value a share = (firm value − debt − minority interest + cash) ÷ the count in force

The run-off ends in a business of year N's size that neither grows nor shrinks: no capital is released in the terminal (revenue is flat) and none reinvested (no growth). The terminal NOPAT is at the full tax rate, the carried loss left out, as 5a.7's. Debt and cash are 5a.9's, the newest balance sheet's (4.2), each line nil when missing, with `line-not-presented:debt` and `:cash`. The minority interest is 5a.9's too, the same sheet's line at book, nil where the sheet does not present it or holds it at or below zero, with no flag. The going-concern equity, the intermediates' `equity`, is the firm value less debt and the minority interest plus cash. The count in force is 5a.9's: the diluted share count, plus any committed shares (14.4). r in every year. The moat enters the margin of safety of the levels (4.10) and nothing else.

### 6a.6 The liquidation floor

From the newest balance sheet (4.2), a quarter's or a fiscal year's, the sheet debt and cash come from:

    NCAV = total current assets − total liabilities
    floor a share = (NCAV − minority interest) ÷ the count in force
    floored value a share = max(going-concern value a share, floor a share), when the floor a share is above zero

The minority interest is 6a.5's: the floor is the common owners' claim, as the going-concern value is, so it takes the minority interest off too. The count in force is 6a.5's, the diluted share count plus any committed shares (14.4). The intermediates' `ncav` is the sheet's own NCAV, and `ncav_per_share` the floor a share. The floor's two lines are cited under their own names, `floor_current_assets` and `floor_total_liabilities`, each noted with the sheet's period (6a.10), since the sheet can be a quarter's; Altman's Z″ and Piotroski's F read the fiscal years' lines (6a.8). When the floor a share is above zero and above the going-concern value a share and π is not 100% (an unavailable π included), the row is flagged `ncav-floor`, "The floored value is the net current asset value <x> a share; the run-off gives <y>.", <x> the floor a share; with a minority interest in force (above zero, the sheet's line or an agent's `balance.minority`) the sentence names the floor for what it is, "The floored value is the net current asset value less the minority interest, <x> a share; the run-off gives <y>." At π = 100% the floored value has no weight (6a.7, 6a.11) and the flag is not raised. A newest balance sheet without total liabilities gives no NCAV and no floor, flagged `line-not-presented:totalLiabilities`, "The newest balance sheet (fiscal 2026 Q3) does not present total liabilities, so no net-current-asset floor is applied."; with no balance sheet on file there is no floor either, and the flag says "No balance sheet is on file, so no net-current-asset floor is applied." Without total current assets there is no NCAV and no floor either.

### 6a.7 The distress probability and the distress-sale value

    value a share = floored value a share × (1 − π) + distress-sale value a share × π

π, the first rule that fits deciding, on 6a.5's debt and cash, never the minority interest:

1. cash at or above a debt on file: π = 0, "no net debt";
2. net debt and a trailing interest expense (4.2) missing or zero: no rating; π is unavailable and the value is the floored value a share, flagged `distress-not-charged`, "No interest expense on file: the distress probability is unavailable and the value is the floored going-concern value." A company whose debt lines are both missing is treated the same, whatever its cash, since unknown debt is not "no net debt": no rating, π unavailable, the debt nil in the bridge (6a.5), flagged `distress-not-charged`, "No debt line on file: the distress probability is unavailable and the value is the floored going-concern value.";
3. otherwise the coverage is EBIT_n ÷ the trailing interest expense, with EBIT_n = trailing revenue × m_n and the expense as its size; an EBIT_n at or below zero is rated D whatever the coverage; else the rating is the `synthetic_rating` row whose coverage range holds the coverage (from its lower bound to below the next row's);
4. π = `default_probability_10y` of the rating.

    distress proceeds = distress_sale_fraction × firm value + cash
    distress-sale value a share = max(0, distress proceeds − debt − minority interest) ÷ the count in force

with `distress_sale_fraction` 0.6, and the minority interest and the count in force 6a.5's (the diluted share count, plus any committed shares, 14.4): the distress-sale value is the common owners' claim, as the going-concern value and the floor are. The intermediates' `distress_proceeds` is the proceeds before debt and the minority interest. A value a share at or below zero refuses the row "value not positive in the run-off", which keeps its figures (6a.1). Otherwise the levels, the designation and the validity are 4.10's, with the moat's margin of safety.

### 6a.8 The distress signals

Shown on every Decline row valued or refused for its value (refusal 6), never deciding the value; the five earlier refusals carry none:

- **Altman Z″**, on the newest annual balance sheet and its fiscal year's operating income: 6.56 × X1 + 3.26 × X2 + 6.72 × X3 + 1.05 × X4, with X1 = (total current assets − total current liabilities) ÷ total assets, X2 = retained earnings ÷ total assets, X3 = operating income ÷ total assets, X4 = stockholders' equity ÷ total liabilities. Below `z_distress` (1.1) the zone is "distress", above `z_safe` (2.6) "safe", else "grey". A missing line, or total assets or total liabilities at or below zero, gives no score. A "distress" zone flags `z-distress`, "Altman Z″ is <z>, in the distress zone below 1.1."
- **Piotroski F**, on the newest two annual balance sheets with their fiscal years' income and cash-flow lines, one point each: return on assets (net income ÷ total assets) above zero; operating cash flow above zero; return on assets higher than the year before's; operating cash flow above net income; long-term debt ÷ total assets lower than the year before's; the current ratio higher; no common stock issued (`commonStockIssuance` at or below zero); gross margin higher; asset turnover (revenue ÷ total assets) higher. A signal whose line is missing, a missing stock-issued line included, or whose ratio has a zero denominator, is not read and scores nothing; the row records how many signals were read. With fewer than two annual balance sheets there is no score. A score at or below `f_score_low` (1), with at least one signal read, flags `f-score-low`, "Piotroski F is <f> of <n read>: the low group of Piotroski (2000)."
- **Reinvesting in decline**: trailing capital spending above trailing depreciation and amortization, both in size and neither missing, while year N's revenue is below trailing revenue, flags `reinvesting-in-decline`, "Capital spending <x> exceeds depreciation <y> while revenue falls: Damodaran's denial."

### 6a.9 What moves with the price

The value, the levels and the validity stay. These are recomputed:

- the designation (4.10);
- **the implied decline rate**: the d at which the value a share, rebuilt from the rebuild keys with the consensus years 1 to K as found, the fade from that d, the floor and π as built and the minority interest off each claim (6a.5 to 6a.7), equals the price. A scan from −50% to 0 in steps of 0.1%, bisected to 1e-7, returns the first root it finds scanning up from −50%; the value need not rise with d, so a second root is not sought. None when the scan finds no sign change;
- the multiples of 5.9: trailing P/E reported and clean; no forward P/E;
- the expected return of 5.10, its growth term the path's own rates (`growth_rates`) for years 2 to H + 1, and zero for a year past N.

A refused Decline row takes only the new price.

### 6a.10 Flags, intermediates and inputs

Flags, in order: `path-held`, `decline-rate-floored`, `path-on-trend`, `consensus-recovery`, `few-analysts` (years 2 and 3 only); `line-not-presented:debt`, `line-not-presented:cash`, `sheet-shift` (section 8), `line-not-presented:totalLiabilities`; `peers-from-industry` when the industry's peers gave the sales-to-capital; `tax-loss-history-unknown`; `ncav-floor` (never at π = 100%); `distress-not-charged`; `z-distress`; `f-score-low`; `reinvesting-in-decline`; the trailing flags (`margin-flag`, `equity-raise`, `negative-fcf`, `heavy-capex`, `large-nri`). The result puts the assembly's flags, then `share-count-gap` (section 8), and the classifier's checks before these, and keeps the first flag of each code. Never raised here: the ROIC flags of 4.5 and 4.6, `consensus-above-base-rate`, `return-above-peers`, `value-spread`, `failure-not-charged`, `path-on-eps`, `sbc-basis-unknown`, `growth-above-return`, `long-runway-high-growth`.

Intermediates: the rebuild keys; `balance_sheet` beside `debt` and `cash`, the newest balance sheet that debt, cash, the minority interest and NCAV were read from, as 5a.14 records it; `moat`; `growth_rates`, years 1 to N; `revenue_by_year`, `ebit_by_year`, `tax_by_year`, `nopat_by_year`, `reinvestment_by_year`, `release_by_year`, `fcff_by_year`; `terminal_value`, `firm_value`, `equity` (the going-concern equity, less the minority interest), `going_concern_per_share`, `ncav_per_share` (the floor a share, NCAV less the minority interest, 6a.6), `floored_per_share`; `rating` (the rating, EBIT_n, the interest expense, the coverage, π, the coverage range of its table row, and the note "no net debt", "no debt line on file" or "no interest expense on file"); `distress_proceeds` (before debt and the minority interest), `distress_per_share`, `value_per_share`; `altman_z` (the fiscal year, X1 to X4, the score, the zone; none without a score); `piotroski_f` (the two fiscal years, the nine signals as read, the score, the count read; none with fewer than two balance sheets); `capex_vs_depreciation`; `eps`, `epv`, `dividend_yield`, `net_buyback_yield`, `income_basis`, `price_facts`, `margin_history` (section 8); and at the price `implied_decline_rate`, `multiples` and `expected_return`.

The rebuild keys: `runoff_path` (revenue0, ntm_revenue, None without a usable consensus, the consensus steps for years 2 to K as found with None for a held one in years 2 and 3, consensus_years K, d, runoff_years), `margin` (6a.3), `tax_rate`, `sales_to_capital` (used, value), `runoff_recovery`, `debt`, `cash`, `minority_interest` (the figure in force, 6a.5), `shares`, `ncav`, `pi` (its value, rating and coverage) and `distress_sale_fraction`. With the policy's r they rebuild the value at any d (6a.9).

Tagged inputs. DATA: the fiscal years on file, price, diluted shares, next earnings date, daily closes, `adr_ratio`, `fx_rate` and `inflation_differential` as 5.12; market cap, trailing revenue, operating income, EPS, dividends, buybacks and stock issued; the newest tax rate; the moat score; GuruFocus EPS and EPS without non-recurring items; `ntm_revenue`, `path_step_2` and `path_step_3` as 5a.14, each noted "no usable consensus: years 1 to 3 shrink at the decline rate" on a `path-on-trend` row, and one `path_step_<t>` for each consensus year t from 4 to K, cited as `path_step_2` is; `interest_expense`, the trailing expense cited to the statements; `sales_to_capital`, the own-latest measure cited to the statements (an estimate under the intangibles method when invested capital took the as-reported total, 4.4), noted "the company's own newest year: revenue over average invested capital", or the peers' median cited as 5a.14 cites it, noted "the mature peers' median; the company has no newest-year measure" (on the industry fallback naming the industry); `debt`, `cash` and `minority_interest`, as 5a.14 cites them; `floor_current_assets` and `floor_total_liabilities`, the newest balance sheet's total current assets and total liabilities that NCAV reads (6a.6), cited to it (`balance_sheet`) and noted with its period, none without a sheet; the newest annual balance sheet's lines and its fiscal year's that Z″ and F read (`total_current_assets`, `total_current_liabilities`, `total_assets`, `total_liabilities`, `retained_earnings`, `equity`, `long_term_debt`, `revenue_fy`, `gross_profit_fy`, `operating_income_fy`, `net_income_fy`, `cfo_fy`, `stock_issued_fy`) and the year before's that F reads (the same names ending `_prior`), each noted with its fiscal year; `depreciation`, trailing. RULE: r, `runoff_years`, `runoff_recovery`, `distress_sale_fraction`, `synthetic_rating` (the lower bound of the row used, its range and the company's coverage in the note; none without a rating, the note saying why), `default_probability_10y` (the figure used; none without a rating), `z_distress`, `z_safe`, `f_score_low`, the moat class and the margin of safety, and `peer_min` and, on the industry fallback, `industry_peer_max` when the peers gave the sales-to-capital. Not cited: the aggregate ROIC, the runway return, g_T and the terminal return, which the run-off does not read. A row refused for its value (refusal 6) cites the same inputs.

### 6a.11 Chronic loss-makers

A normalized margin at or below zero makes EBIT_n at or below zero: with net debt the company is rated D, π = 100%, and its value is the distress-sale value alone, a fraction of a firm value at or below zero plus cash less debt and the minority interest, so usually zero; the floor has no weight at π = 100%, and `ncav-floor` is not raised. With net cash π is 0 and the value is the floored going-concern value, below zero when the run-off is. Either way the row is refused "value not positive in the run-off" and keeps every figure: the path, the margin, the rating, the signals and NCAV, with its inputs.

### 6a.12 Worked numbers

The tests pin these (spec 2026-11-03 13.1 and spec 2026-11-06 13.1), to 1e-9 a share. Trailing revenue 1,000 and operating income 80; fiscal margins 7%, 8%, 8%, 9%, 8% (m_n 8%); tax 25%; own sales-to-capital 2.0; the consensus −3% in years 1 to 3; d = −4%; r 9%; debt 200, cash 100, 100 shares; trailing interest 20 (coverage 4.0, A−, π 5%); current assets 300 and total liabilities 500 (NCAV −200, no floor).

- K = 3. Rates, years 1 to 10: −3%, −3%, −3%, −3.428571%, −2.857143%, −2.285714%, −1.714286%, −1.142857%, −0.571429%, 0.
- Revenue 970.00 in year 1 and 808.24 in year 10; free cash flow 67.20 and 48.49; FCFF_11 48.49 (no release); terminal value 538.83; firm value 607.48.
- Going concern 5.0748 a share; distress-sale value (0.6 × 607.48 + 100 − 200) ÷ 100 = 2.6449; value 5.0748 × 0.95 + 2.6449 × 0.05 = 4.9533 (method 0.8 gave 4.3180 on the same figures).
- The consensus reaching year 5 (base + 3 at +1% on 6 analysts, base + 4 at +2% on 6, base + 5 absent): K = 5; rates −3%, −3%, −3%, 1%, 2%, −3.2%, −2.4%, −1.6%, −0.8%, 0; revenue 940.24 in year 5 and 867.10 in year 10; FCFF_11 52.03; terminal value 578.07; firm value 622.19; value 5.0974; `consensus-recovery` names years 4 and 5. With base + 3 on 4 analysts, K = 3 and the row is 4.9533 with no `few-analysts`.
- No usable consensus: years 1 to 3 at −4%, then the fade; value 4.8626, `path-on-trend`.
- Cash 250: π = 0 and 6.5748. No interest expense with net debt: 5.0748, `distress-not-charged`. No debt line on file: no rating, 5.0748 + 2.00 = 7.0748 (the debt nil), `distress-not-charged`. Current assets 1,100 (NCAV 600): the floor 6.00, `ncav-floor`, value 6.00 × 0.95 + 2.6449 × 0.05 = 5.8322; with no interest expense as well, 6.00. A minority interest of 50 takes 0.50 off the going concern (4.5748), the distress-sale value (2.1449) and the value (4.4533), and the floor a share is (−200 − 50) ÷ 100 = −2.50; with current assets 1,100 the floor is (600 − 50) ÷ 100 = 5.50, `ncav-floor` "The floored value is the net current asset value less the minority interest, 5.50 a share; the run-off gives 4.57.", and the value 5.3322. The rating, π and the distress proceeds do not move. A trailing margin of −1% on the same fiscal margins: the same value and rating. Fiscal margins −2%, −3%, −3%, −3%, −3%: D, firm value −241.32, value 0, refused; with cash 250, −1.9132 a share, refused.
- Value at d = 0: 5.2247; at d = −50%: 3.1251. At a price of 4.9533 the implied decline rate is −4%; at 4.00, −21.8%; at 3.00 none, the value at −50% being above the price.

## 6b. The Start-up route

No value by design (design.md 6.8): a Start-up row is a refusal that keeps its figures. What the price needs is stated as one number, the revenue in year 10 that, at the mature peers' margin, makes the company worth its price today; beside it, the net cash a share as a floor and the years the cash covers the trailing burn. The market and the share that would reach that revenue are the agent's to judge.

### 6b.1 Which rows, and the result

A company the classifier puts in Start-up (3.5 rule 1), past the guards of 5.1 that come before its route: no universe deciles, the assembly's refusal (the currency's among them, section 7), no positive price, no positive diluted share count. A company in another currency reads its market cap in that currency, so its requirement, cash floor and burn are in it. "No trailing figures" does not refuse a Start-up row: a pre-revenue company may lack them, and the route reads what exists.
