Every Start-up row is the refusal "Start-up: no value by design", its route `start-up`, with no value, no levels and no designation (4.10), and with the intermediates and inputs of 6b.6. A figure the route cannot compute carries its own reason inside the intermediates; nothing refuses further.

### 6b.2 The price-implied requirement

    EV_price = market cap + debt + minority interest − cash

The market cap is section 2's: the quote's, else the profile's, else the screener row's. Debt and cash are 5a.9's, the newest balance sheet's (4.2), each line nil when missing and flagged `line-not-presented:debt` or `line-not-presented:cash`. The minority interest is 5a.9's too, the same sheet's line at book, nil where the sheet does not present it or holds it at or below zero, with no flag: the price buys the common owners' share, so the outside owners' claim is added to it, as debt is. The net cash is cash − debt − minority interest. When EV_price is at or below zero the requirement is 0, the net cash covering the price, and the row is flagged `below-net-cash`, "The price is covered by net cash: market cap <x> against net cash <y>."

Otherwise the requirement is R10, the year-10 revenue at which a ten-year ramp is worth EV_price:

    R_t = R0 + (R10 − R0) × t ÷ 10
    EBIT_t = E0 + (m* × R10 − E0) × t ÷ 10, for t = 1 … 10

R0 is trailing revenue, 0 without one. E0 is trailing operating income, else the last fiscal year's, else 0. m* is the target margin (6b.3). A pre-revenue company has no margin, so operating income moves in dollars, not as a margin.

- Tax and NOPAT by 5a.6 at the newest fiscal year's statutory rate (4.3), the carried loss starting at zero.
- Reinvestment by 5a.5 at the sales-to-capital of 6b.3: reinvestment_t = max(0, (R_{t+1} − R_t) ÷ sales-to-capital) for t = 1 … 9, and reinvestment_10 = terminal NOPAT × g_T ÷ terminal return.
- The free cash flow, the terminal NOPAT, the terminal value and the firm value by 5a.7, at r in every year (5a.8). The terminal return is 5a.7's, 4.7's moat rule on the return on new capital at the target margin, m* × (1 − tax rate) × sales-to-capital: r for a Narrow or None moat; for a Wide moat that return capped at 4.7's cap (13% at the defaults) and never below r. It reads the peers' margin and sales-to-capital and the company's statutory rate; the company has no return on capital to read.

EV(R10) is that firm value. R10 is the least revenue in [0, 10^13] at which EV(R10) is at or above EV_price: 0 when EV(0) already is, else found by bisection to a relative tolerance of 1e-9 (4.1a). EV rises with R10 for sensible inputs, the terminal dominating; when EV(10^13) is below EV_price, as at a target margin at or below zero or a sales-to-capital so low that reinvestment outruns the margin, there is none.

The requirement's reasons, the first that applies, each leaving R10 and the ramp uncomputed: "no target margin: <n> mature peers" (6b.3), "no sales-to-capital" (6b.3), "no statutory tax rate", "no market cap", "no revenue reaches the price". A reason is inside the intermediates; the row's refusal stays "Start-up: no value by design".

### 6b.3 The target margin and sales-to-capital

The target margin is 5a.4's: the median ten-year margin of the mature peers with a margin on `peer_margin_min_years` or more, at least `peer_min` of them, with its industry fallback, its record and its flag `peers-from-industry` as there. Sales-to-capital is 5a.5's step 2 only, the median of the peers' positive measures when at least `peer_min` are positive: a Start-up has no usable own measure, so step 1 is not read and the record says "peers".

### 6b.4 The cash floor and the burn cover

    cash floor = (cash − debt − minority interest) ÷ the count in force

the net cash a share, debt, cash and the minority interest as 6b.2 reads them, from the newest balance sheet, and the count in force the diluted share count, plus any committed shares (14.4). The `below-net-cash` flag of 6b.2 is the cash floor's test in total: the market cap at or below the net cash, the price at or below the cash floor when the market cap is the price times the count in force.

The burn is −(trailing operating cash flow + trailing capital spending) when that sum is below zero. The burn cover is cash ÷ the burn, in years; "not burning" when the sum is zero or above; none without both trailing figures or without a cash figure (an unknown cash is not a cash of nil: the burn shows, the cover does not, `cash_known` is false and no flag is raised). A cover below `burn_cover_flag_years` (2) flags `burn-cover-short`, "Cash covers <x> years of the trailing burn of <y> a year."

### 6b.5 What moves with the price

The market cap moves with the price: the market cap the row read × the new price ÷ the price it was read at, the quote's market cap at an unchanged share count. EV_price, the requirement and its ramp are rebuilt at it from the rebuild keys (6b.6) with the policy's r and g_T and the row's `debt`, `cash` and `minority_interest`, and `below-net-cash` is read again on the same three. A requirement whose reason is one of its inputs (the target margin, the sales-to-capital, the tax rate, the market cap) stays. The cash floor, the burn cover and every other flag stay.

### 6b.6 Flags, intermediates and inputs

Flags, in order: `line-not-presented:debt`, `line-not-presented:cash`; `sheet-shift` (section 8); `peers-from-industry`; `tax-loss-history-unknown` when the requirement's ramp starts from an operating loss or has a year whose EBIT is below zero, as a loss-maker's always does; `below-net-cash`; `burn-cover-short`. The result puts the assembly's flags, then `share-count-gap` (section 8), and the classifier's checks before these, and keeps the first flag of each code. Never raised here: the ROIC flags of 4.5 and 4.6, `return-above-peers`, `few-analysts` and the trailing flags; no path reads the consensus or the company's return.

Intermediates: `requirement` (`reason`, none when it is computed; `revenue_y10`; `ev_price`; `revenue_0`; `operating_income_0`; `target_margin`, 5a.14's record of the peers and their source; `sales_to_capital`, 5a.14's record with `used` "peers"; `tax_rate`; `terminal_roic`; `path`, the ten years of revenue, EBIT, tax, NOPAT, reinvestment and free cash flow; `terminal_value`; none where a reason leaves a figure uncomputed), `cash_floor`, `burn_cover_years`, `burn` (none when not burning), `debt`, `cash`, `minority_interest` (the figure in force, 6b.2), `balance_sheet` (the newest balance sheet the three were read from, as 5a.14 records it), `cash_known` (false when no cash figure is on file, `cash` then the nil the floor and the requirement count), `shares`, `market_cap`, `price_facts` and `margin_history` (section 8), which holds no margin where no fiscal year has a revenue above zero.

The rebuild keys: the requirement's `revenue_0`, `operating_income_0`, `target_margin`'s value, `sales_to_capital`'s value, `tax_rate` and `terminal_roic`; with `debt`, `cash` and `minority_interest` they rebuild EV_price and the requirement at a new market cap (6b.5).

Tagged inputs. DATA: the fiscal years on file, price, diluted shares, next earnings date, daily closes, `adr_ratio`, `fx_rate` and `inflation_differential` as 5.12; market cap; trailing revenue and operating income when there are trailing figures, and the last fiscal year's operating income (`operating_income_fy`, noted with its fiscal year) when E0 reads it; trailing operating cash flow and capital spending when there are trailing figures; the newest tax rate; the moat score; `target_margin` and `sales_to_capital` as 5a.14 cites them, when found, the latter noted "the mature peers' median; a Start-up reads no own measure"; `debt`, `cash` and `minority_interest`, as 5a.14 cites them. RULE: r, g_T, the moat class, the terminal return (noted with the moat), `peer_min`, `peer_margin_min_years`, `industry_peer_max` on the industry fallback, `burn_cover_flag_years`.

### 6b.7 Worked numbers

The tests pin these (spec 2026-11-03 13.2), to 0.01 unless a tolerance is named. Trailing revenue 0 and operating income −50; target margin 25%, the peers at 20%, 25% and 30%; the peers' sales-to-capital 1.5; tax 25%; r 9%, g_T 3%; no moat score (terminal return r, terminal multiple 11.4444); market cap 2,000, debt 0, cash 300, so EV_price 1,700; 100 diluted shares.

- EV(0) = −166.93: the loss shrinks by 5 a year to nothing in year 10, untaxed, with no reinvestment and no terminal value. EV(1,000) = 931.91.
- R10 = 1,725.73, with EV(R10) = 1,700.00 within 1e-4.
- Trailing operating cash flow −60 and capital spending −15: a burn of 75 a year and a burn cover of 4.0 years, no flag; cash 120 covers 1.6 years, `burn-cover-short`. Operating cash flow 15 is not burning.
- Cash floor 3.00 a share. A market cap of 250 gives EV_price −50, the requirement 0 and `below-net-cash`, "market cap 250 against net cash 300".
- A minority interest of 100 at a market cap of 250: EV_price 250 + 100 − 300 = 50, so the price needs revenue and `below-net-cash` is not raised; the cash floor is (300 − 100) ÷ 100 = 2.00 a share. At a price that moves the market cap to 150, EV_price is −50 and the flag is raised, "market cap 150 against net cash 200". At a minority interest of 40 the market cap of 250 is still covered, by net cash of 260.
- At twice the price the market cap moves to 4,000, EV_price to 3,700 and R10 to 3,616.66, what a valuation at that market cap gives.
- With a Wide moat the return on new capital at the target is 0.25 × 0.75 × 1.5 = 0.28125, so the terminal return is the 13% cap and R10 is 1,514.51.

## 7. Foreign filers

A foreign filer is a company that reports in another currency than USD, or whose shares trade as American depositary shares (ADS). It takes its phase's route on the rules of a domestic filer. Two things are its own: the share its per-share figures are on, and the currency its figures are in. Every comparison the engine makes happens in one currency, the reporting currency, and the translation to USD happens once, on the way out.

### 7.1 The listed-share basis

FMP states an ADR's share count, its EPS and its consensus per ADS, the unit the quote prices, and its street actuals in USD per ADS; GuruFocus states its EPS in USD per ADS. The ADR ratio, GuruFocus's depositary receipt ratio (ordinary shares per ADS), is not applied to them. It is read for the row's words, the DATA input `adr_ratio` (5.12), and, on a row whose ratio is neither 0 nor 1, for the statement periods whose count is off by the ratio or its inverse, which 4.2 reads on the quote's basis under the note `basis-scaled`, or, for the newest period, refuses "share basis unclear". A company reporting in USD with the ADR flag is valued as a domestic row: no rate, no differential, no restated policy and no input or intermediate of the currency; it carries the DATA input `adr_ratio`, as every ADR does (5.12).

### 7.2 The reporting currency

The reporting currency is the newest annual income statement's `reportedCurrency`, else the universe row's; a row whose statements and universe row name none is refused "reporting currency unknown". A trailing quarter whose income statement names another currency is not read: the trailing figures fall back to the fiscal year, flagged `trailing-on-fiscal-year` with a sentence naming the quarter and its currency (4.2). A fiscal-year series that changes currency is read as it is: the newest year's currency is the reporting currency, the growth ratios come from the reported figures, and the row carries `currency-change` (3.6); the ten-year ROIC aggregate mixes the years on such a row.

### 7.3 The rate

The rate of a currency at a date is FMP's last close of the pair `USD<code>` at or before the date, in units of the currency per USD, so a figure in the currency is ÷ the rate to USD and × the rate from it. The closes are the pair's daily history (section 2), fetched once a currency in the `rates` stage of a full or scoped refresh, merged with the pair's quotes a price refresh stores, a quote winning on its date. USD's rate is 1 on every day and fetches nothing. The row's rate is the one at the price date (at the day of valuation without a price): the DATA input `fx_rate`. A close more than `fx_stale_days` (7) before the date puts the note `fx-stale` on the row (section 8); no close at or before the date, or no usable history of the pair, is the refusal "no exchange rate for <code>".

### 7.4 What comes into the currency, and what goes out

Before any route, every USD figure the route reads comes into the reporting currency at the rate of its own date, and the routes then run as for a domestic filer, on one currency:

| Figure | Rate | Why that date |
|---|---|---|
| the price | the price date's | the quote's day |
| the market cap (the quote's, else the profile's or the screener's) | the price date's | the same quote |
| each street actual (one per trailing period) | its period end's | FMP translates each quarter at that quarter's rate |
| GuruFocus's EPS (trailing) and EPS without non-recurring items | the mean of the trailing periods' period-end rates | a trailing sum; GuruFocus does not say how it translates |
| the trailing revenue for the sales class (4.1's `base_rates`, the class bounds in USD millions) | the price date's, the figure ÷ the rate | the one place a currency total meets a USD bound |

Assembly brings the street actuals and GuruFocus's EPS into the currency, a period end without a rate leaving the figure unknown (the stock-compensation basis then unknown, 5.2), never a refusal; the engine brings in the price and the market cap and reads the sales class. Everything else the routes read is in the reporting currency already: the statements, the consensus, the tax rate (by country), the balance sums, the peers' ratios. The peers' market caps stay USD: they rank the peers against each other.

On the way out, the value a share is the route's value ÷ the price date's rate, and the four levels are the policy's on that USD value (4.10): US dollars a share, per ADS for an ADR. The price stays the USD quote; the price-to-value and the designation read the two USD figures. The result's intermediates carry `currency`, `fx_rate`, `fx_date`, `value_in_currency` (the route's value a share before translation, on a valued row) and `inflation_differential`, and every other intermediate in the reporting currency as the route made it; a USD row carries none of them. A row refused for its currency's own reason, its code known and no rate read ("no exchange rate for <code>", "no inflation projection for <code>", "<code>: expected inflation <π>% above the 10% bound", 7.6), carries `currency` alone: its figures are in that currency, and it has no rate, rate date or differential. A reader that needs the rate reads `fx_rate`'s presence, never a default: such a row's company read names the code with no rate, its pair is never quoted and a price refresh moves only its price. A row refused before the currency's step (its statements, its tax rate, 4.3) still reads its currency: in another currency with a rate on file it carries the full entry, the intermediates `currency`, `fx_rate`, `fx_date` and `inflation_differential`, the inputs `fx_rate` and `inflation_differential` and `fx-stale` when the rate is stale, as a valued row does; where the currency's own step would refuse, it carries none, the earlier refusal being its own. A row whose currency is unknown carries none. The P/E of the multiples history reads the price in the currency over the EPS in the currency, a ratio free of the currency. The screen compares like with like: its money columns are in US dollars, as the value, the levels and the price are, so the EPV, the growth route's cross-check value a share and the year-10 revenue the price needs (6b.2), which the route writes in the currency, are ÷ the rate on the screen and in the MCP `screen`; the drawer and the report page read them in the currency from the intermediates.

A price refresh (section 10) quotes the pairs of the currencies the repriced companies carry a rate in and reprices each such row at its pair's quote: the new USD price comes into the currency at the new rate, everything that moves with the price moves on the figures in the currency, and the value in the currency stays while its USD figure, and the levels with it, moves with the rate. A row whose pair quote fails reprices at the rate its snapshot holds, with `fx-stale` once that rate's date is more than `fx_stale_days` before the new price date.

The words (spec 2026-11-10 7.4). An input in the currency carries its code as its unit (5.12), and so does an override of a money input (14.2). The drawer and the report page print a figure in US dollars with `$`, and any other with its code before the number, a total to whole units and a figure a share to two decimals: "TWD 3,809,054,300,000", "TWD 12,807.36"; the rate to two decimals, four below one; the ADR ratio as a number of shares. Under the company's name each says what the figures are in, "Reports in TWD; one ADS is five ordinary shares; valued at TWD 32.96 per USD", and the report page adds "every per-share figure here is per ADS" on an ADR; a row refused for its currency, with no rate, has no "valued at" clause ("Reports in ARS; one ADS is two ordinary shares"); a company in US dollars that is no ADR has no such line. The company read (web and MCP `company`) carries `currency`, the snapshot's reporting currency; `isAdr`, the ADR flag as assembly reads it, the profile's, else the universe row's (section 2), none with neither; `adrRatio`, the `adr_ratio` input; and `fxRate` and `fxDate`, none on a row with no rate, in US dollars or refused for its currency.

Worked (spec 2026-11-10 11.2): a company at 8.0 per USD on the price date, quoting USD 50.00 for a market cap of USD 5,000 m, has its route read a price of 400.00 and a market cap of 40,000 m in the currency; a route value of 480.00 a share is USD 60.00, the buy level at a 25% margin of safety USD 45.00, the price-to-value 0.8333. Trailing periods ending at rates 7.8, 7.9, 8.1 and 8.2: street actuals of USD 0.60, 0.62, 0.64 and 0.66 read 4.680, 4.898, 5.184 and 5.412, the street trailing EPS 20.174; GuruFocus's EPS of USD 2.50 reads 2.50 × 8.0 (the mean) = 20.00. A trailing revenue of 8,000 m in the currency is USD 1,000 m for the sales class. A reprice to USD 55.00 at 8.5: the price in the currency 467.50, the value 480.00 ÷ 8.5 = USD 56.47, the price-to-value 0.9740. A close of 2026-11-01 read for a price date of 2026-11-10 is `fx-stale` (9 days); one of 2026-11-05 is not.

### 7.5 The rates in the currency

Five policy values are nominal USD rates: `required_return`, `terminal_growth`, `inflation_addition`, `return_without_goodwill_cap` (4.6 rule 5) and, in each Base Rate Book class of `base_rates`, `sales_p90_5y`, which caps the revenue-growth route's held rate (5a.3) and sets the evidence rule of 14.3. For a company in another currency each is restated by the inflation differential d, Damodaran's rule for a discount rate in another currency:

    d = (1 + π_currency) ÷ (1 + π_USD) − 1
    rate_currency = (1 + rate) × (1 + d) − 1

π is the currency's expected inflation from the inflation table (4.1): the mean of the IMF World Economic Outlook's projections of consumer price inflation of the currency's economy for the five years after the retrieval's year, 2027 to 2031 at the retrieval of 2026-11-10. USD is in the table and gives π_USD. The route reads the restated policy, its version and settings hash unchanged; every rate derived from the five follows them, the Wide moat's terminal return cap at r + 4 points (4.7) among them. The flag thresholds (`long_runway_growth_flag` and the others) and the margin bounds are not restated: a threshold is a reader's mark, and a margin has no currency. The RULE input `inflation_differential` names d with a note giving both means and the table's vintage (5.12); a USD row has d = 0 and carries neither the input nor a restated policy; a currency whose π equals USD's carries the input at 0 and reads the policy as it is.

Worked (spec 2026-11-10 11.1): with r 9%, g_T 3% and the inflation addition 2%, a currency at 4.04% against USD at 2.00% has d = 1.0404 ÷ 1.02 − 1 = 2.000%, r 11.180%, g_T 5.060% and the addition 4.040%. On the packaged table, USD at 2.120%: TWD at 1.600%, d −0.509%, r 8.445%, g_T 2.476%; JPY at 2.120%, d 0; BRL at 3.240%, d +1.097%, r 10.195%; CHF at 0.740%, d −1.351%, r 7.527%; KZT at 6.240%, d +4.034%, r 13.398%.

A currency the table lacks is the refusal "no inflation projection for <code>". A currency whose π is above `inflation_max` (10%) is the refusal "<code>: expected inflation <π>% above the 10% bound", π to one decimal: ARS (12.1%) and TRY (18.5%) on the packaged table; KZT (6.2%) is valued. Hyperinflation accounting (IAS 29) is not attempted.

The override ranges in USD or USD a share are read in the reporting currency at the snapshot's rate (14.2), and a guidance record's figures are in the reporting currency as the company states them.

### 7.6 The refusals

In this order, after "no financial statements" and the missing tax rate (4.3), which refuses every company: "reporting currency unknown"; "no exchange rate for <code>"; "no inflation projection for <code>"; "<code>: expected inflation <π>% above the 10% bound"; then the basis's "share basis unclear" (4.2). Each keeps its phase, its classifier evidence, its price and its price columns; the three that name a code carry the intermediate `currency` alone, with no rate (7.4). A snapshot valued before method 1.1 may carry the refusal "foreign filer: currency and share-unit handling not built yet", which the method no longer gives, nor the ADR flag as a ground for refusal.

Every phase has a route. A Start-up row's refusal is its route's, "Start-up: no value by design" (6b), in place of method 0.6's "Start-up: no value by design; the price-implied requirement comes with the Start-up route".

## 8. The flags

A flag warns and never changes a value, a level or a designation. Each has a code, a short word for the column and one sentence for the drawer. A sentence's money figures are in the row's reporting currency: `$` on a USD row, and on a row in another currency (section 7) its code before the figure. So the six sentences that name dollars name the currency instead: `consensus-basis-unknown`'s gap a share ("SEK 0.40 a share"), `margin-ramp-in-dollars`' ramp ("moves in SEK from −SEK 15,000.0 million"), `reinvesting-in-decline`'s spending and depreciation ("Capital spending SEK 1,234.5 million exceeds depreciation SEK 800.0 million"), `stock-pay-missing`'s fiscal year ("fiscal 2025 shows SEK 1,330.0 million"), `sbc-basis-uncertain`'s figures a share ("the stock pay of SEK 2.40 a share after tax") and `sheet-shift`'s two sheets' figures ("where fiscal 2025 showed SEK 435.9 million"). `share-count-gap` names the market cap and the price, which are in US dollars on every row.

Twelve flags are quality warnings: they say the value may be wrong because the business or its figures are unusual. They are `large-nri`, `value-spread`, `margin-flag`, `z-distress`, `f-score-low`, `reinvesting-in-decline`, `equity-raise`, `negative-fcf`, `heavy-capex`, `burn-cover-short`, `consensus-recovery` and `share-count-gap` (`QUALITY_WARNINGS` in `engine/flags.py`). A row's flag count is the number of its quality warnings; a refusal row has one too, since its flags are kept. Every other flag is a note: the classifier checks, the thin-consensus flags, the unknown inputs, the engine's mechanics (a floored return, a zeroed charge) and the bookkeeping flags (the lease and line flags, the balance sheet's shift, the carried tax rate). A note shows in the Flags column and the drawer and does not count. Where a review fills the row, the row's flags, its count and the drawer's list are the review's (14.5). The column Counts says which flags count.

| Code | Counts | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `growth-word-disagrees`, `loss-year`, `revenue-gap`, `revenue-falling`, `margin-direction-disagrees`, `fcf-sign`, `short-history`, `margin-zero`, `currency-change` | no | the classifier checks, 3.6 |
| `tax-carried-forward` | no | a fiscal year after the tax table's newest year took that year's rate |
| `leases-not-on-balance-sheet` | no | a balance sheet before 15 December 2019 shows no lease assets |
| `trailing-on-fiscal-year` | no | the statement periods on file are neither four consecutive quarters nor two consecutive half-years, "The statement periods on file are neither four consecutive quarters nor two consecutive half-years, so the trailing figures are fiscal <year>'s."; or one of the periods summed names another currency than the newest annual statement, and the sentence then names those quarters or half-years, their currency and the fiscal years'. The fiscal year keeps the cadence the rows gave, quarterly when they gave none, and its NTM window reads on it (4.2) |
| `sbc-basis-unknown` | no | the stock-compensation basis test lacked an input; on the revenue-growth route, only when the cross-check has a value |
| `stock-pay-missing` | no | a Mature Growth row whose trailing stock compensation is zero, or has a trailing period at zero, while a fiscal year on file holds one above zero (4.2), valued or refused "no positive forward anchor" or "value not positive under the reinvestment charge" (5.11); word "Stock pay missing", the sentence naming the newest such fiscal year and its figure and saying the stock-pay charge on the consensus may be nil or short (5.11); not raised under an applied `earnings.sbc` or `earnings.ntm` (14.4) |
| `sbc-basis-uncertain` | no | a Mature Growth row whose stock-pay basis test may misread, valued or refused "no positive forward anchor" or "value not positive under the reinvestment charge" (5.11), one sentence for each reason that holds: `includes-sbc` on a material stock pay with `large-nri` on the row; `includes-sbc` on a material stock pay with a trailing effective tax rate at or below `sbc_basis_tax_share` (0.5) times the statutory rate; `excludes-sbc` on a stock pay above zero with the street trailing EPS above GAAP, as the test reads it, by `sbc_basis_gap_multiple` (10) times the after-tax stock pay a share or more; word "Stock-pay basis uncertain"; not raised under an applied `earnings.sbc_basis` or `earnings.ntm`, nor `stock-pay-missing` under an applied `earnings.sbc` or `earnings.ntm` (5.11, 14.4) |
| `path-on-eps` | no | years 2 and 3 grow at the consensus EPS steps |
| `few-analysts` | no | a present path step lands on a year with fewer than 5 analysts or no count: the EPS count on the earnings path (5.3), the revenue count on the revenue path (5a.2, 6a.2); on a Decline row years 2 and 3 only, since a thin year past 3 ends the consensus (6a.2) |
| `thin-ntm` | no | a Mature Growth row whose NTM window (5.2) holds a period with fewer than `analyst_min` (5) EPS analysts or no count that is left as it is: one in a fiscal year before the last the window reaches, which nothing corrects, or one in the last where that year's annual consensus does not correct year 1, since it has no annual EPS estimate, no EPS analyst count or fewer than 5, or not every period of the year is estimated (5.2, spec 2026-11-13 9a); word "Thin NTM consensus", the sentence counting those periods and naming the thinnest count ("no count" for a missing one), its period end and why its fiscal year takes no correction; right after `few-analysts`; not raised under an applied `earnings.ntm` (5.11, 14.4) |
| `step-series-disagree` | no | a Mature Growth path step has a rate on net income and a rate on EPS more than `step_series_gap_max` (5 points) apart in size, exactly 5 points being silent; names each such step, its two rates and the share counts the two series imply for its two years (the net-income mean over the EPS mean) against the diluted count, "(A and B against the diluted count C)", the series the path is on, and the step's override, `path.step_2` or `path.step_3`, as what decides; a step an override replaced drops out (5.3). Never on a Mature Stable row, whose cross-check reads both series (6.5) |
| `path-held` | no | a consensus revenue step is missing, so its year holds the rate of the year before; names the fiscal years that left it missing and the rates held (5a.2, 6a.2) |
| `path-on-trend` | no | a Decline row without a usable NTM consensus: years 1 to 3 of the run-off shrink at the decline rate, which then fades to zero by year N; the sentence names the rate as used (−50% on a floored row) and N (6a.2) |
| `decline-rate-floored` | no | the five-year trend shrinks revenue faster than 50% a year, so the run-off uses −50% (6a.2) |
| `consensus-recovery` | yes | a consensus year of a Decline row, 1 to K, grows while the run-off declines; names each such year and its rate, and the decline rate as used (−50% on a floored row) (6a.2) |
| `consensus-above-base-rate` | no | the year-3 consensus revenue growth is above the sales class's 90th-percentile five-year sales growth, so years 4 and 5 hold the cap and the fade starts from it (5a.3) |
| `peers-from-industry` | no | FMP's peers left fewer than `peer_min` that count for the target margin, so the peers are the largest `industry_peer_max` mature companies of the company's FMP industry in the universe (5a.4, 6b.3); on a Decline row, only when they gave the sales-to-capital (6a.4) |
| `roic-unavailable` | no | fewer than 5 ROIC years |
| `roic-on-n-years` | no | 5 to 9 ROIC years |
| `ten-year-roic-unavailable` | no | the ten-year capital sum is at or below zero |
| `line-not-presented:<field>` | no | an optional balance-sheet line counted as nil in a ROIC year (4.4) |
| `line-not-presented:debt`, `line-not-presented:cash` | no | the bridge to equity counted a debt or cash line as nil: the figure is unknown, or assembly's note names a line the newest balance sheet (4.2), a quarter's or a fiscal year's, does not present; the sentence names the sheet's period (5a.9, 6a.5, 6b.2) |
| `line-not-presented:totalLiabilities` | no | the newest balance sheet of a Decline row (4.2), a quarter's or a fiscal year's, lacks total liabilities, or none is on file, so no net-current-asset floor is applied; the sentence names the sheet's period (6a.6) |
| `sheet-shift` | no | a revenue-growth, Decline or Start-up row, valued or refused wherever its route can raise `line-not-presented:debt`, whose newest balance sheet is a quarter's with an annual sheet on file (4.2), where a line of the quarter's differs sharply from the newest annual sheet's (spec 2026-11-13 12.5). A figure is nil when the sheet does not present it or holds it at or below zero. One sentence for each reason that holds, in this order: the quarter's short-term and long-term debt both nil while the annual sheet's sum above zero, "The balance sheet of fiscal 2026 Q3 shows no debt where fiscal 2025 showed $435.9 million, so the debt may be short, or the annual line held more than borrowings."; the quarter's cash and equivalents nil while the annual sheet's are above zero, "… shows no cash and cash equivalents where fiscal 2025 showed $1,587.1 million, so the cash may be short."; its short-term investments the same, "… shows no short-term investments where fiscal 2025 showed $730.2 million, so the cash may be short."; the two minority interests, each nil counting as zero, more than `sheet_shift_multiple` (3) times apart either way, "… shows a minority interest of $1,013.0 million where fiscal 2025 showed $45.0 million, more than 3 times apart, so the vendor's line may be wrong.", or against none, "… shows no minority interest where fiscal 2025 showed $45.0 million, so the vendor's line may be wrong." and "… shows a minority interest of $454.4 million where fiscal 2025 showed none, so the vendor's line may be wrong.", exactly 3 times being silent. The first sentence starts "The balance sheet of <the quarter's period> shows", each later one "It shows". A debt or a cash that only rose or fell between the two sheets raises nothing. Word "Balance sheet shift"; right after `line-not-presented:cash` (5a.13, 6a.10, 6b.6); never on the two mature routes. In a review a sentence does not show under the input that decides its figure: `balance.debt` the debt's, `balance.cash` the two cash lines', `balance.minority` the minority interest's; with none left the note does not show (14.4). It moves no value, and a price refresh keeps it as it was |
| `charge-zeroed` | no | the runway charge is off, 4.6 rule 4 |
| `return-without-goodwill` | no | the chosen return measure was below r and the return on capital without goodwill and intangibles was above it, or that capital sums to zero or below with NOPAT above zero, so the charge starts at that return under its cap (4.6 rule 5); names the measure below r, the measure without goodwill and the start |
| `return-floored` | no | the chosen return measure was below r, and the return without goodwill and intangibles was not above it, or purchases carried the growth of the capital, so the charge starts at r (4.6 rules 5 and 6); in the second case the sentence adds "The return on capital without goodwill and intangibles is not read: purchases carried the growth of the capital." |
| `growth-above-return` | no | a year's growth paid for, h_{t+1} (4.8), exceeds its return, so the year pays out less than nothing: the owner funds the growth; names the first such year, the growth it pays for and its return, and counts the rest |
| `tax-loss-history-unknown` | no | the revenue-growth forecast, the run-off or the Start-up ramp has a year whose EBIT is below zero, or the trailing margin (the ramp's starting operating income) is below zero; the tax carry starts at zero and the terminal is taxed in full (5a.6, 5a.7, 6a.3, 6b.6) |
| `margin-ramp-in-dollars` | no | a revenue-growth row's trailing margin is below `loss_ramp_margin_bound` (−100%), so operating income moves in dollars from the trailing loss to the target margin's share of revenue in the convergence year; names the trailing margin and the ramp's two ends (5a.4), in the reporting currency on a row in another: "moves in SEK from −SEK 15,000.0 million" |
| `return-above-peers` | no | the return on new capital at the target margin is above the mature peers' median ten-year ROIC (5a.11) |
| `failure-not-charged` | no | a Young Growth row: the failure probability is unavailable and the value is the going-concern value (5a.12) |
| `ncav-floor` | no | the net current asset value less the minority interest, a share, is above zero and above the run-off's going-concern value, so it floors it, and π is not 100% (an unavailable π included); at π = 100% the floored value has no weight; with a minority interest in force the sentence names the floor "the net current asset value less the minority interest" (6a.6) |
| `distress-not-charged` | no | a Decline row with no debt line on file, or with net debt and no trailing interest expense: no rating, π unavailable, the value is the floored going-concern value; the sentence says which (6a.7) |
| `z-distress` | yes | Altman Z″ is below `z_distress` (1.1) (6a.8) |
| `f-score-low` | yes | Piotroski F is at or below `f_score_low` (1), with a signal read (6a.8) |
| `reinvesting-in-decline` | yes | trailing capital spending is above trailing depreciation and amortization in size while the run-off's revenue falls (6a.8) |
| `below-net-cash` | no | a Start-up row's market cap is at or below its net cash, cash less debt and the minority interest, so EV_price is at or below zero and the price needs no revenue (6b.2, 6b.4) |
| `burn-cover-short` | yes | a Start-up row's cash covers fewer than `burn_cover_flag_years` (2) years of its trailing burn; never without a cash figure (6b.4) |
| `margin-flag` | yes | the trailing operating margin is 1.5 × its ten-year median or more (a positive median), or above its ten-year high by 10% of the high's size or more; needs 6 fiscal years with a margin. The word is "Newly profitable" when the median is at or below zero, "Possible cycle peak" when the window holds a loss year, else "Margin above history" |
| `equity-raise` | yes | trailing stock issued is 1% of market cap or more |
| `negative-fcf` | yes | trailing operating cash flow plus capital spending is below zero |
| `heavy-capex` | yes | trailing capital spending is above 50% of a positive operating cash flow. On a Mature Growth or Mature Stable row whose runway return is neither off nor unavailable (4.6), the sentence goes on: "The charge's return starts at R, <its measure>; the newest year's marginal return is Y%, on the capital added in fiscal <year>. The stored years end at fiscal <year>, so a return on a spending surge still under way is the agent's judgment: `return.runway`." (14.2). Under a `return.runway` override, on any measure: "starts at <the override>, the agent's override; <the measure> is <the measured return>; the newest year's …", a floored measure read before its floor, "the measured return is <X>, below the required return", a measure below r on a row that would start at the return without goodwill the same with "and the charge would start at <S>, on capital without goodwill and intangibles", and the measure left out when the measured return is off or unavailable. Without a newest-year figure it stops after the start |
| `large-nri` | yes | the non-recurring gap is 5% of trailing EPS or more, in either direction |
| `consensus-basis-unknown` | no | a Mature Growth or Mature Stable row with `large-nri`, which it follows and never fires without: FMP's consensus names no basis, so whether the NTM anchor and the path carry the non-recurring items is unknown; names the gap a share and its share of trailing EPS, and leaves the basis to a report that reads the filing: "through `earnings.nri_gap`, and on Mature Growth `earnings.ntm`" (14.2), since Mature Stable reads no NTM earnings |
| `split-pending` | no | a split on file falls after the oldest trailing period's end and at or before the price date (4.2), and so for about a year after a split, until every trailing period postdates it; the sentence names the split, its date and ratio, the newest trailing period it follows, and each figure read on the post-split basis with the periods it was scaled on, or that FMP's figures already read on it (when the diluted count and every period judged read post-split), or why the statements are read as stored (no quote count, or a count that fits both bases or neither) or the statements and the consensus both (no diluted count); and each trailing period read as stored, with its reason (no share count, or a count that fits both bases or neither of the count it is judged against), and each consensus row read as stored, with its reason (no net-income mean, an EPS of zero, or an implied count that fits both bases or neither) |
| `override-split-scaled` | no | a review with an applied override in dollars a share or in shares made on a snapshot whose figures had not seen a split on file that the figures of the snapshot reviewed saw, each snapshot's figures seeing the splits dated at or before its basis date, so the override applies on the new basis (14.6); word "Override scaled at a split", the sentence naming each such input in the bridge's order: "A split took effect after these inputs were entered, so each applies on the new basis: earnings.ntm, value.outside."; on the review's result only, never on the snapshot's; at the recompute it comes after the review's other flags, `fx-stale` last; a price refresh keeps it with the review, and a flag the reprice re-reads, `long-runway-high-growth` or `below-net-cash` (section 10), can land after it |
| `basis-scaled` | no | an ADR's diluted count or trailing EPS read on the quote's share basis by the ADR ratio or its inverse, or a trailing period read as stored for the basis (4.2); the sentence names the factor, the periods scaled, the stored count and the quote's with the ratio, and each period read as stored with its reason; any row whose ratio is neither 0 nor 1, whatever its ADR flag (a count that fits no share basis, or two, of the count it is judged against, or no share count). On a row with a pending split it stands beside `split-pending`, each naming its own scaling |
| `share-count-gap` | yes | the market cap ÷ (the price × the diluted count) is outside `share_count_gap_band` (0.7 to 1.3), an edge being inside; on every result with the three figures above zero, valued or refused, after the assembly's flags. The market cap and the price are the quote's, in US dollars on every row, so the ratio has one currency; where the quote holds no market cap, assembly takes the profile's, then the screener's (section 2), so on such a row the ratio can read two dates; the count is the diluted count, an agent's `shares.diluted` (14.4), else the count after the split rule and the ADR basis rule (4.2), so in a review a count that reconciles the two clears it; it leaves out an agent's committed shares (`shares.committed`, 14.4), since the market cap holds no share not yet issued. Word "Share count and market cap disagree"; the sentence gives both figures, the ratio to two decimals, the band and the two readings: "The market cap is $690,000.0 million and the price of $150.00 times the diluted count of 1,000.0 million shares is $150,000.0 million: a ratio of 4.60, outside 0.70 to 1.30. The count may leave out a share class or units that convert to shares, or the market cap may leave a class out." A price refresh keeps it as it was: the market cap and the price are one quote's, so the ratio does not move with the price (section 10) |
| `fx-stale` | no | a company in another currency whose rate, the last close of its pair at or before the price date, is more than `fx_stale_days` (7) before it; word "Rate stale", the sentence naming the pair and both dates: "The newest close of USDTWD on file is of 2026-11-01, 9 days before the price date 2026-11-10." A price refresh puts it on, or takes it off, by the rate it reprices at (section 7); last among the row's flags |
| `long-runway-high-growth` | no | a 20-year runway with a belief or an implied growth of 10% or more |
| `value-spread` | yes | the value spans 30% or more across the first three return keys or across the three runways; on the revenue-growth route, across the three target margins, or the value at the lowest is at or below zero while the median's is positive (5a.11) |

The three lease and line flags sit on nearly every valued row today; their redesign is a known item.

The ten-year median margins (spec 2026-11-13 11) are no flag: every result whose series is not empty, a refusal too, carries `margin_history` in its intermediates, which the research agent reads for its judgment of a cyclical company and which moves no figure. `operating` is the median of `margin-flag`'s window, operating income ÷ revenue over the last ten fiscal years that have both and a revenue above zero; `net` is net income ÷ revenue on the same rule, net income being the annual income statement's line (3.1). Each has `median` and `years`, the count of fiscal years behind it, fewer than ten on a shorter history; a margin no fiscal year has is absent, so a row with no revenue above zero carries an empty record. Both are ratios of the statements' own lines, as reported, in the reporting currency, so no rate enters on a company in another currency. A price refresh keeps the record as it was. Mature Stable's own `median_margin`, which that route reads (6.2), stays where it is.

## 9. The refusal reasons

| Reason | Where |
|---|---|
| no universe deciles yet | 3.3 |
| no financial statements | assembly |
| no statutory tax rate for <country> <year> | 4.3 |
| reporting currency unknown | 7 |
| no exchange rate for <code> | 7 |
| no inflation projection for <code> | 7 |
| <code>: expected inflation <π>% above the 10% bound | 7 |
| share basis unclear: the newest period's count <c> against the quote's <Q> (the ADR ratio <ρ>) | 4.2 |
| foreign filer: currency and share-unit handling not built yet (no longer given: a snapshot valued before method 1.1 may carry it) | 7 |
| fewer than two fiscal years; no revenue for the last fiscal year; negative revenue; no measurable revenue growth; no operating margin | 3.8 |
| no price; no diluted share count | 5.1 |
| Start-up: no value by design | 6b.1 |
| no trailing figures (never a Start-up row) | 5.1 |
| no trailing revenue; no statutory tax rate | 5.1, 5a.1, 6a.1 |
| no trailing operating income | 5a.1, 6a.1 |
| incomplete NTM consensus | 5.2, 5a.2 |
| no positive forward anchor; forward P/E below 2: unit error | 5.2 |
| no positive consensus revenue | 5a.2 |
| consensus revenue falls to zero or below within the path | 5a.2, 6a.2 |
| no target margin: <n> mature peers | 5a.4 |
| no sales-to-capital | 5a.5, 6a.4 |
| consensus earnings turn negative within the path | 5.3 |
| value not positive under the reinvestment charge | 5.6, 5a.9, 6.1 |
| value not positive in the run-off | 6a.7, 6a.11 |
| no revenue trend (only a row a `phase` override puts in Decline) | 14.4 |
| no positive trailing earnings | 6.1 |
| inside a Start-up row's `requirement`, not the row's refusal: no target margin: <n> mature peers; no sales-to-capital; no statutory tax rate; no market cap; no revenue reaches the price | 6b.2 |
| data fetch failed: <the failed required endpoints> | the refresh, when an annual statement or the quote fails (section 2) |
| data fetch failed: <error class>: <message>; data fetch incomplete: rate limited; valuation failed: <error class>: <message> | the refresh, when a company's fetch raises, is cut by an HTTP 429, or its valuation raises |

## 10. Refresh kinds and what moves

- **Full refresh**: the universe, the data of section 2, the splits among them, for every admitted company, then the exchange rates of the fetched companies' currencies, one call a currency, the deciles, then every company classified and valued, one snapshot per company. A company fetched within the reuse window (`refresh.reuse_hours`, 24 hours by default) is not fetched again.
- **Scoped refresh**: the named companies only, on the latest stored deciles (3.3). Each one's data of section 2, its statements, closes and splits among them, is fetched again, every call, whatever the reuse window, so a scoped refresh on a split's day reads the splits FMP lists from that day, then its currency's rates again; assembly then runs on it, and the company is classified and valued, one snapshot per company.
- **Price refresh**: fresh quotes for the latest universe's admitted companies (before any universe, every company with a snapshot), and the pairs' quotes for the currencies the repriced companies carry a rate in (a row refused for its currency names the code with no rate, so quotes no pair), so the price and the USD value sit on the same day's rate; then each latest snapshot repriced from its intermediates alone: the value, the levels and the validity window stay, except that a company in another currency translates its value in the currency, which stays, and its levels at its pair's new quote, its price coming into the currency at it (section 7), and a pair whose quote fails reprices at the rate the snapshot holds, with `fx-stale` once that rate is more than `fx_stale_days` before the price date. The rate is snapshot data, as the price is: the repriced snapshot holds the pair's quote and the quote's own date, which may be a day after the price date (a pair quoted after midnight UTC, before the stocks' first trade of the day, while the stocks' last trade is the day before), and the reproduction check of an override or a sensitivity reading (14.3) reads the snapshot's rate, not the store's at the price date. The designation, the implied growth and its sensitivities, the margin the price needs (revenue-growth rows), the implied decline rate (Decline rows, 6a.9), the growth-route cross-check's implied growth, the multiples, the growth share, the expected return and the long-runway flag are recomputed, each solve on the price targeting the price less a row's value outside the business (`outside_value_per_share`, 14.4); the price input becomes the new quote; it applies no split rule (4.2): a split between the stored statements and the new quote waits for a scoped refresh, which fetches the company's statements, closes and splits again, whatever the reuse window, and runs assembly. A refusal snapshot takes only the new price, its date and the price input, except a revenue-growth refusal whose intermediates carry `revenue_path`: the two refused for their target margin ("no target margin: <n> mature peers") or for a value not positive ("value not positive under the reinvestment charge"). Such a row also recomputes its margin the price needs from its rebuild keys (5a.10); its other figures stay. A Start-up row moves its market cap with the price and rebuilds its requirement and its `below-net-cash` flag at it (6b.5); its other figures stay. A Mature Stable row refused "no positive trailing earnings" or "value not positive under the reinvestment charge" moves its growth-route cross-check's implied growth (6.1, 6.4); a cross-check that is itself a refusal stays, and so do its other figures. The price columns (section 2, daily closes) do not move, nor does `share-count-gap` (section 8): one quote's market cap and price keep their ratio. The company's current review reprices with its snapshot (14.6); in another currency its mechanical value is the repriced snapshot's and each bridge line's change a share moves by the old rate ÷ the new, its readings in the currency staying, so the mechanical value and the changes still sum to the reviewed value. A snapshot valued under another policy version is not repriced, "policy changed: run a revalue"; under other settings (its settings hash), "settings changed: run a revalue"; under another method version, "method changed: run a revalue". A revalue writes the current versions and hash.
- **Revalue**: the engine on the stored facts of every company and the rates on file, with the latest stored deciles, no vendor call and no freshness window. The new snapshots carry the current method and policy versions and the settings hash.

Every refresh that values (full, scoped, revalue) first backfills the facts of lines added since the payloads were stored, reading the stored responses, so a balance sheet stored before 2026-11-14 gains its `minorityInterest`; a price refresh does not.

Snapshots are appended, never changed. The screen reads the latest snapshot per company.

| 0.9, policy v9 | 2026-11-08 | The secondary valuation only where the route refuses, and refused where the route or the review values the company (15.4, 16.3); the list mark and the table row in the check (16.3); the row's levels from the reviewed value (14.5). No version changes: the arithmetic of every value is as before. |
| 1.0, policy v10 | 2026-11-09 | The engine round 2 (spec 2026-11-09). The charge (4.8; 5.6 to 5.8; 6.3) loses its floor at zero, so a year whose growth outruns its return pays a negative cash flow, the owner's money going in; and it pays for the next year's revenue growth, not its earnings growth, since margin change on the capital already in place needs no new capital; in the belief years the two rates are one, so nothing moves there. The runway return (4.6) holds through the years that pay for growth above the belief, the consensus steps and the growth fade, then fades in a straight line to the terminal return (4.7) in year N, in place of holding for N − 1 years; the `heavy-capex` sentence names the newest year's marginal return, shown and never used, and the `return.runway` override as the remedy for a capital-spending surge the stored years cannot see. Two notes (section 8): `consensus-basis-unknown` beside `large-nri` on the two mature routes, since FMP's consensus names no basis; and `step-series-disagree` on Mature Growth, when a consensus step read on net income and the same step on EPS differ by more than `step_series_gap_max`. Two inputs an agent may set with evidence (14.2 to 14.5): `value.outside`, a figure a share outside the business, nil by default, added to the route's value a share as its own bridge line; and `shares.diluted`, the diluted share count of 4.2 for shares not yet in the reported count, period-tied only. Policy v10 adds `step_series_gap_max`, `split_implied_count_tolerance` and the two inputs' pairs in `override_ranges`, none a setting (4.1); every v9 value unchanged. The charge moves every Mature Growth and Mature Stable value; at the defaults every row on the revenue-growth, Decline and Start-up routes keeps its figures. Plan 2 (2026-11-10): the `splits` dataset (section 2) and the split basis of 4.2 with the `split-pending` note; no version change, since no figure moves until a split is on file. |
| 1.1, policy v11 | 2026-11-10 | Foreign filers (spec 2026-11-10). A company reporting in another currency is valued in that currency, the reporting currency of its newest annual income statement. The exchange rate of a currency at a date is FMP's last close of the pair `USD<code>` at or before it, in units of the currency per USD, from a new dataset, each pair's daily closes for `refresh.fx_history_years`, fetched once a currency in a `rates` stage of a full or scoped refresh; USD's rate is 1. Before any route, the price and the market cap come into the currency at the price date's rate, each street actual at its period end's and GuruFocus's EPS at the mean of the trailing periods' rates, and the trailing revenue goes to USD for the sales class's bounds; the routes then run on one currency as for a domestic filer, and the value a share and the four levels go back to USD per ADS at the price date's rate, so the price-to-value and the designation read two USD figures. The policy's nominal USD rates, `required_return`, `terminal_growth`, `inflation_addition` and each class's `sales_p90_5y`, are restated in the currency by the inflation differential d = (1 + π_currency) ÷ (1 + π_USD) − 1, as (1 + rate) × (1 + d) − 1, π the mean of the IMF World Economic Outlook's projections in the inflation table (4.1) for the five years after the retrieval's year, 2027 to 2031 at the retrieval of 2026-11-10; the flag thresholds and the margin bounds are not restated, and the override ranges in USD are read in the currency at the price date's rate. A price refresh quotes the pairs too and translates at the new rate, the value in the currency unchanged; a pair whose quote fails reprices at the rate the snapshot holds. The listed-share basis: FMP states an ADR's count, its EPS and its consensus per ADS, the unit the quote prices, so the ADR ratio is not applied to them; on an ADR whose GuruFocus ratio is known and not one, each trailing period's count is judged against the quote's as the split rule judges it (4.2), with the ratio and its inverse as two more factors, a period that fits only by one of them scaled to the quote's basis under the note `basis-scaled`, and a newest period that fits no basis, or two, refused "share basis unclear". The refusals "reporting currency unknown", "no exchange rate for <code>", "no inflation projection for <code>" and "<code>: expected inflation <π>% above the 10% bound" (`inflation_max`) come after "no financial statements" and the missing tax rate and before the basis refusal; "foreign filer: currency and share-unit handling not built yet" goes, with the ADR flag as a ground for refusal, and the missing tax rate refuses every company. A trailing quarter in another currency than the newest annual statement sends the trailing figures to the fiscal year, flagged `trailing-on-fiscal-year`. A rate whose last close is more than `fx_stale_days` before the date it is read at puts the note `fx-stale` on the row. The inputs `fx_rate`, `adr_ratio` and `inflation_differential` name the rate, the ratio and the differential, and a figure's unit names its currency. The secondary valuation runs in the currency on the restated rates, its totals in the currency and its value a share, its levels and its grid's cells in USD at the snapshot's rate, which the record stores (15.3); it loses its foreign-filer refusal, and a snapshot refused for its currency or its share basis, as one with no diluted share count, can have none, so its report needs none and Appendix A names the refusal (15.4, 16.3, 16.6). Policy v11 adds `inflation_max`, `fx_stale_days` and `refresh.fx_history_years`, none a setting (4.1); every v10 value unchanged. Every domestic row keeps its figures bit for bit, since each rule is conditioned on the currency, the ADR ratio or a period in another currency: a company reporting in USD with the ADR flag is valued as a domestic row, with no rate, no differential, no restated policy and none of the five currency intermediates, and BVC, whose trailing quarter in CNY now sends its trailing figures to the fiscal year, is the one domestic row that moves. The half-year window (plan 2 of the spec, 2026-11-11, under the same version, so a 1.1 snapshot valued before that day reads a half-year reporter on its fiscal year): a company whose two newest quarterly statement rows end 170 to 195 days apart, the row before them not 80 to 100 days before the older, reports half-yearly; its trailing figures sum its last two half-years and its street trailing EPS the two actuals at their ends (4.2); its NTM window is two consecutive half-years with no consensus row 80 to 100 days from either, else four consecutive quarters, on every route that reads one (5.2, 5a.2, 6a.2); the fiscal-year fallback keeps the cadence the rows gave; the split and basis notes name half-years. Every quarterly company keeps its figures bit for bit. |
| 1.2, policy v12 | 2026-11-12 | The return without goodwill (founder 2026-11-12, on the low-values investigation of 2026-11-11). On the two mature routes a runway measure below r is read again on capital without goodwill and intangibles, and above r there the charge starts at that return, at most `return_without_goodwill_cap` (25%), in place of r; a business with no capital of its own without them, and NOPAT above zero, starts at the cap; where purchases carried the growth of the capital, the floor stands (4.6 rule 5). The Wide moat's terminal return follows that start (4.7). A `return.runway` override on a row started at the return without goodwill sets the terminal return too, so an agent can take the whole lift back; every other row keeps the rule it had (14.4). The new note `return-without-goodwill` takes the place of `return-floored` on the rows that start at the new return (section 8); every row with five kept years records the measures without goodwill and each kept year its average goodwill and intangibles (4.6). Policy v12 adds the cap, not a setting, restated in another currency as r is (7.5); every v11 value is unchanged. Only a row whose measure is below r and whose return without goodwill starts its charge moves; it moves up, save a row whose consensus revenue falls, where a higher return lowers what the shrinking releases (4.8). A revenue-growth or Mature Stable row's growth-route cross-check (6.4, 5a.11) reads the same returns, so its value a share moves on such a company too. Every other row keeps its figures and gains the recorded keys. On a revalue of a copy of the live data (2026-11-12): of 466 rows at the floor on the two mature routes 167 start at the new return and 299 stay; no value fell; the 167 rose by 9.1% at the median (4.8% to 23.4% between the quartiles); every other value in its currency was unchanged. What the price needs (4.11) is new and moves no value: on a row whose price is well above its value, the company's detail carries one or two sentences from the figures the row already solves at its price, and the drawer and the report page lead the band with them. |
| 1.3, policy v13 | 2026-11-13 | The research round, plan 1 (spec 2026-11-13). The runway return an agent may set, by an override or on a sensitivity axis, takes above 0% to 500% in place of 100%, so a grid can hold the engine's own measure, 332.6% on AAPL; the bound still catches a percent typed as a whole number (14.2, 14.8). The range bounds an agent's input alone, so it moves no mechanical figure. Assembly reads each consensus dataset from the newest stored answer that holds rows and from no older one (2, 5.2, 5a.2), so a period end FMP moved by a day between answers no longer leaves its old row beside the new one, where the old row could be the one kept, as it was on 53 companies on 2026-11-13. Policy v13 adds `sbc_material_share`, `sbc_basis_tax_share`, `sbc_basis_gap_multiple` and `share_count_gap_band`, none a setting (4.1), and the pair of `earnings.sbc` in `override_ranges`; every other v12 value is unchanged. A period-tied override drops when a newer period is on file than the one it corrected or when the engine's reading of its figure changes, no longer when the statements are fetched again on a later day, and every result with trailing figures carries its trailing period end, `trailing_end` (14.6). Two notes on a Mature Growth row, neither counted and neither moving a figure (5.11, section 8), say when the stock-pay charge may be wrong: `stock-pay-missing` when the trailing stock pay is zero, or one of its periods' is, while a fiscal year on file holds stock pay above zero, and `sbc-basis-uncertain` when the basis test may misread on a one-time item, a low tax or a gap far above the stock pay, the trailing figures and the stored quarters gaining the income tax and the pre-tax income for it (4.2, 16.9). Two inputs an agent may set with evidence on a Mature Growth row (14.2 to 14.5), their bridge lines after the non-recurring gap's: `earnings.sbc`, the trailing stock pay the anchor reads in the basis test and the adjustment, period-tied only, and `earnings.sbc_basis`, the basis in place of the test; the intermediates gain `sbc_ttm`, the stock pay the anchor read, the two notes read the figures the anchor read, and each note is silent under the input that decides it. A twelfth quality warning, `share-count-gap` (section 8), counted, so the row leaves the Clean filter: on every result, valued or refused, whose market cap is outside 0.7 to 1.3 times the price times the diluted count in force, an agent's `shares.diluted` clearing it when it reconciles the two; it moves no value. A note on a Mature Growth row, `thin-ntm` (5.11, section 8), not counted and moving no figure, says when a period of the NTM window has fewer than 5 EPS analysts or no count, silent under an applied `earnings.ntm`; and every result whose series is not empty carries `margin_history`, the ten-year median operating and net margins with their counts of years (section 8), the series gaining each fiscal year's net income, which the classifier does not read (3.1). |
| 1.4, policy v14 | 2026-11-14 | The research round, plan 2 (spec 2026-11-13). The refresh fetches the quarterly balance sheet, eight periods, as the dataset `balance-quarter`, not a required call (section 2), and the debt and cash of the revenue-growth, Decline and Start-up routes, the Decline route's liquidation floor and the secondary valuation's default debt and cash read the newest balance sheet on file, a quarter's or a fiscal year's, the fiscal year's on a tie, each input's note naming the sheet's period (4.2, 5a.9, 6a.5 to 6a.7, 6b.2, 6b.4, 15.3), while the capital years of the returns and of sales-to-capital and the balance years of Altman's Z″ and Piotroski's F stay on fiscal years. The minority interest, the newest sheet's `minorityInterest` line at book, nil where the sheet does not present it or holds it at or below zero, is subtracted on the three routes that value the firm, each citing it as the DATA input `minority_interest`: from the revenue-growth route's equity (5a.9), from the Decline route's going-concern value, liquidation floor and distress-sale value (6a.5 to 6a.7), and from the Start-up route's cash floor, while its enterprise value at the price adds it (6b.2, 6b.4); the two mature routes do not read it, since their earnings are after the minority's share. An agent may set `balance.minority` with evidence on those three routes (14.2 to 14.5), the minority interest as a total in the reporting currency, $0 or more, period-tied only, its bridge line after cash, for a market value or a redeemable stake the line leaves out; policy v14 adds its pair in `override_ranges` and no value, and every v13 value is unchanged. Where a period of the NTM window in F, the last fiscal year the window reaches, has fewer than `analyst_min` (5) EPS analysts or no count, the raw NTM EPS is the sum of the window's periods plus the window's share of F's periods times the gap between F's annual EPS estimate and the sum of F's periods, when that estimate rests on 5 EPS analysts or more and every period of F is estimated, else the sum of the periods as before; the fiscal year partly reported is never corrected and its annual estimate never read (5.2); the rule holds wherever the anchor is read and moves only the Mature Growth value, the `anchor` entry records the source and the correction, and `thin-ntm` now shows only on a thin period left as it is (5.11, section 8). `earnings.sbc` may be lasting at 0, for a company that pays no stock, and at no other figure: such an override holds across periods and drops when the store's trailing stock pay is above zero, and is refused where the snapshot's own trailing stock pay reads above zero (14.2, 14.3, 14.6). The flags a reviewed row shows move no value: where a review fills a row, the screen's Flags, Warnings and Clean and the drawer's flag list read the review's flags, not the snapshot's (14.5, section 8). |
| 1.4, policy v15 | 2026-11-14 | The sheet-shift note (spec 2026-11-13 12.5). The method is unchanged at 1.4: no value moves. A note, `sheet-shift` (section 8), not counted, on a revenue-growth, Decline or Start-up row whose newest balance sheet is a quarter's: one sentence for the quarter's debt read as nil where the newest annual sheet holds debt, one for each cash line so read, and one for a minority interest more than 3 times apart from the annual sheet's either way, a figure against none included. A balance that only rose or fell raises nothing. In a review each sentence is silent under the input that decides its figure, `balance.debt`, `balance.cash` or `balance.minority` (14.4). Assembly hands the engine the five lines of the sheet in use and, beside a quarter's, of the newest annual sheet, which no value reads (4.2). Policy v15 adds `sheet_shift_multiple` (3), not a setting (4.1); every v14 value is unchanged. |
| 1.4, policy v16 | 2026-11-14 | The inputs an agent could not set (spec 2026-11-14). The method is unchanged at 1.4: no mechanical figure moves, since each change reads an agent's input or acts on a review. An agent may set `shares.committed` with evidence on every route, period-tied only: shares the company has committed to issue that neither its diluted count nor the analysts' EPS holds yet, which join the diluted count, or an agent's `shares.diluted` in its place, in the count that divides every value among the owners, while on Mature Growth the NTM net income and the stock pay a share, and on Mature Stable the two gaps a share, stay on the count without them, so x committed shares on a count of n lower a mature route's value a share by x ÷ (n + x), and `share-count-gap` reads the count without them, since the market cap holds no share not yet issued (4.2, 4.8, 5.2, 5.6, 5.7, 5.12, 5.13, 5a.9, 6.1, 6.4, 6a.5 to 6a.7, 6b.4, section 8, the glossary of section 13, 14.2 to 14.5). An agent may set `earnings.ntm_nri` with evidence on Mature Growth, period-tied only: an amount a share, on the count the consensus is on, that the NTM consensus carries and that does not recur, gains on an asset added through `value.outside` say, which comes off the NTM EPS after the stock-pay adjustment and before any cycle gap, a negative amount raising it, its bridge line right before the cycle gap's, and which applies with a bridge line of zero under an applied `earnings.ntm`; a cross-check does not read it (5.2, 5.12, 14.2 to 14.5). An agent may set `revenue.step_2` and `revenue.step_3` with evidence on Mature Growth, period-tied only, each the revenue growth the charge pays for in its year in place of the consensus revenue step, or of the earnings step where the consensus gives none, the engine's reading being the consensus step as found, which a cross-check still reads; the `charge` entry gains `revenue_steps_found` and `revenue_step_source` beside `revenue_steps`, the steps the charge read, and `growth-above-return` names an agent's step revenue growth (4.8, 5.3, 5.11, 5.12, 6.4, 14.2 to 14.5, 14.9). An applied override in dollars a share is divided, and one in shares multiplied, by the product of the ratios of the splits that the figures of the snapshot reviewed saw and those of the snapshot it was made on did not, a snapshot's figures seeing the splits dated at or before its basis date, the earlier of the price date and the data date of the snapshot that assembled them, the stored override keeping the figure entered and the review carrying the note `override-split-scaled` (section 8, 14.4, 14.6, 14.8). At a full or scoped refresh, a revalue and the web's recompute, the system drops an applied override whose tag the row's route does not read, the route of the `phase` override still applied, else of the mechanical phase, with the reason "the <route> route does not read the input", at a refresh after the `phase` override's own tests and before the other overrides', the web's answer naming each such drop, while an unclassified row keeps its overrides (14.3, 14.6). A guidance record may hold `net_income`, a total in the reporting currency stepped against the prior year's net income, and an `earnings.ntm` may cite a `gaap` fiscal-year record of `eps` or of `net_income`, the agent dividing a net income's midpoint by the count the company's EPS is per, the guided-year drop reading either (14.2, 14.3, 14.6, 14.9, 16.2). The inputs an agent may override become twenty-five. Policy v16 adds the pairs of `shares.committed`, `earnings.ntm_nri`, `revenue.step_2` and `revenue.step_3` in `override_ranges` and no value (4.1); every v15 value is unchanged. |
| 1.5, policy v16 | 2026-11-15 | A thin revenue step is not read (spec 2026-11-14 6.2). On the Mature Growth route a consensus revenue step above `thin_revenue_step_max` (50%) whose landing year has fewer than `analyst_min` (5) revenue analysts, or no count, the landing year's count alone, is not read: the charge takes the earnings step of that year as resolved, as it does for a missing revenue step, so an earnings step override moves the charge of that year, while an agent's `revenue.step_2` or `revenue.step_3` stands over the rule, since an agent's step is never thin (4.1, 4.8, 5.3, 14.2, 14.4). A step at or below 50% is read on any count. The rule reaches the Mature Growth value and every growth-route cross-check, a Mature Stable row's and a revenue-growth row's, whose values and levels do not read the cross-check (5a.11, 6.4). The `charge` entry's `revenue_steps` holds none on a thin year and `revenue_step_source` reads `thin`, while `revenue_steps_found` keeps the step found, which stays the engine's reading of the two inputs, so an agent's step at the step found moves the value there (4.8, 5.12, 14.4). No flag says a step is thin, and `growth-above-return` calls the year's growth "growth", an earnings step standing in (5.11). Policy v16 gains one value for the rule, `thin_revenue_step_max` (50%), not a setting (4.1), beside the four override ranges of the row above. Only a row whose consensus holds a thin revenue step moves: on a copy of refresh 61 (2026-11-14), four rows, ACM, HESM, FRO and BIPC. Without the size test the rule left 404 steps unread on 309 rows and moved 298 values, 221 of them down, since the earnings step that stands in carries the change of margins (spec 2026-11-14 6.2 and its decision log, 2026-11-15). No golden holds one. |
| 1.5, policy v16 | 2026-11-15 | The runway return's rulings (founder 2026-11-15, on B-8 and B-170, with that day's measurements). The method is unchanged at 1.5: no mechanical value moves. An agent's `return.runway` is the return in force, so the moat's terminal rule reads it in place of the measure on every row: Wide, the override capped at r plus the spread and never below r; Narrow or None, r. Before, the terminal return stayed on the measure save on a row started at the return without goodwill, so a Wide row kept a terminal return above an agent's lower start, and a review's value at a return differed from the row's own return sensitivity at it (4.6, 4.7, 5.8, 14.4). On the two mature routes `runway_return` gains `constant_margin`, the five-year marginal return at a constant margin, shown and never used: the newest NOPAT margin times the revenue added over the capital added across the marginal span, which leaves out a margin that rose on the revenue already there; the engine keeps the marginal, and the agent weighs the two in the `roic` judgment (4.6). |

A snapshot names the method and policy versions and the settings hash that produced it. A price refresh refuses to reprice a snapshot from another method version.

## 13. Glossary

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Value at belief | The value a share at the growth belief; the one price target |
| ADS | An American depositary share: the unit an ADR's quote prices, a number of ordinary shares, its ADR ratio; FMP states an ADR's count, EPS and consensus per ADS, and every per-share figure of an ADR is per ADS (section 7) |
| Reporting currency | The currency of the newest annual income statement, else the universe row's; the currency the route values a company in (section 7) |
| Pair | The FMP forex symbol `USD<code>`, whose close is the currency's units per USD: the rate a figure comes into the currency at (×) and goes back to USD at (÷) (section 7) |
| Inflation differential | d = (1 + π_currency) ÷ (1 + π_USD) − 1, π the mean of the IMF projections in the inflation table; it restates a nominal USD rate in the currency as (1 + rate) × (1 + d) − 1 (section 7) |
| Cadence | How often a company reports, read from its two newest quarterly statement rows: quarterly when they end 80 to 100 days apart, half-yearly when 170 to 195 and the row before them does not end a quarter before the older. The trailing figures sum four quarters or two half-years, and the NTM window reads the next four quarters or two half-years; the fiscal-year fallback keeps the cadence the rows gave, quarterly when they gave none (4.2, 5.2) |
| Belief | The growth rate for years 4 to N before the fade: the base-rate median plus 2 points |
| Runway N | The years of growth above the terminal rate, set by the moat class |
| Runway return | The return on new capital the charge starts at, held through the hold K, then faded to the terminal return in year N (4.8) |
| Terminal return | The return on new capital after the runway |
| Charge | The share of earnings held back to pay for the next year's revenue growth, h ÷ return; above 100% when the growth outruns the return |
| Charge off | The runway years are not charged, because measured capital is at or below zero |
| Fade | The five equal steps from the year-3 consensus growth down to the belief |
| EPV | Earnings power value: clean trailing earnings ÷ r, the value with no growth |
| Designation | Where the price stands against the value at belief: one of five bands on price ÷ value (4.10); a fact about the price, never an action |
| Non-recurring gap | GuruFocus EPS trailing less EPS without non-recurring items |
| Growth word, margin direction | The classifier's reading of the deciles for revenue growth and for the margin trend |
| Check | A classifier warning that never changes the phase |
| Snapshot | One company's result under one refresh, with the versions and the settings hash that produced it |
| Setting | A policy value the user may change on the Settings view (4.1) |
| Effective policy | The policy file's values with the user's settings applied over them; the policy the engine runs (4.1) |
| Settings hash | The sha256 of the user's settings that change a result's figures; it names the settings a result was valued under, as the policy version names the file (4.1) |
| Repricing horizon | H: the years over which the expected return assumes the price meets the value (5.10) |
| Quality warning | One of the twelve flags that say the value may be wrong because the business or its figures are unusual; a row's flag count counts them (section 8) |
| Note | Any other flag: shown, never counted (section 8) |
| Mature peers | The company's FMP `stock-peers` that the latest universe lists and the classifier puts in Mature Growth or Mature Stable; when fewer than `peer_min` of them count, the largest mature companies of the company's FMP industry |
| Target margin | The operating margin the revenue-growth route moves to by year 10, and the Start-up ramp reaches in year 10: the median ten-year margin of the mature peers |
| Sales-to-capital | The revenue a dollar of invested capital buys: the company's change in revenue ÷ its change in capital, its newest kept year's revenue ÷ that year's capital (6a.4), or a peer's revenue ÷ its capital. The revenue-growth route reinvests the next year's revenue increase ÷ sales-to-capital |
| Margin the price needs | The target margin at which the revenue-growth value a share equals the price |
| Return on new capital at the target | Target margin × (1 − tax rate) × sales-to-capital: the after-tax return on new capital once the margin reaches the target. It sets the terminal return by the moat's rule and is checked against the peers' ROIC |
| Held rate | The growth the revenue path holds in years 4 and 5 before its fade: g_3 as resolved, or, for the implied growth, the rate at which the value equals the price. A held step is a missing consensus step that takes the rate of the year before |
| Run-off | The Decline route's DCF: a firm that shrinks year by year, releasing the capital its lost revenue needed, until its decline fades to zero by year N, and is valued in the terminal as a business of year N's size that neither grows nor shrinks (6a) |
| Decline rate | d: the five-year revenue slope over the last fiscal year's revenue, at most zero and floored at −50%; the run-off's rate after the consensus years, fading in a straight line to zero by year N (6a.2). The implied decline rate is the d at which the value equals the price (6a.9) |
| Normalized margin | m_n: the median operating margin of the last five fiscal years when at least three have one, else the trailing margin; held in every year of the run-off (6a.3) |
| Synthetic rating | The bond rating that a company's interest coverage, EBIT_n over the trailing interest expense, reads off Damodaran's table; its ten-year default probability is π (6a.7) |
| Distress-sale value | What equity gets in a distress sale: 60% of the firm value plus cash, less debt and the minority interest, not below zero, a share (6a.7) |
| NCAV | Graham's net current asset value: total current assets less total liabilities; less the minority interest and over the count in force, the diluted shares plus any committed shares, the run-off's floor when above zero (6a.6, 14.4) |
| Requirement | A Start-up row's price-implied requirement: the revenue in year 10 at which a ten-year ramp to the mature peers' margin is worth the market cap plus debt and the minority interest, less cash (6b.2) |
