| Cash floor | A Start-up row's net cash a share, (cash − debt − minority interest) ÷ the count in force, the diluted shares plus any committed shares (6b.4, 14.4) |
| Burn cover | The years a Start-up row's cash covers its trailing burn, the trailing operating cash flow plus capital spending when below zero; "not burning" otherwise (6b.4) |
| Override | A change an agent makes to one tagged input, with its reason, its evidence and its lifetime (lasting, or tied to a period); applied, or refused with the rule it breaks, and stored either way. The engine, not the agent, computes the value it gives |
| Review | The engine's valuation of a company under its applied overrides: the reviewed value, its levels and designation on the same edges, and the bridge from the mechanical value, one line per override. It follows the company's snapshots and cites `fairband-review/1` |
| Finding | A dated fact an agent records with its source (a filing, a transcript, a URL, a vendor response or a base rate); a finding or a guidance record is the only way a number from outside Fairband enters a report |
| Judgment | One of a report's key judgments (the phase, the moat, the belief, the ROIC, the target margin): the record of the decision, in words, and the override it produced, if one. An input an override sets is tagged JUDGMENT, beside DATA and RULE |
| Secondary valuation | A second valuation the engine computes from an agent's inputs, in one of two forms: the standard form projects the free cash flow to the firm from the agent's drivers (the revenue growth and the operating margin year by year, the tax rate, sales-to-capital, the terminal growth); the parts form discounts an explicit free-cash-flow path, one part or a sum of parts. Both discount at r with a terminal at g_T or below and carry a three-by-three grid at other rates and growths, a display and not a value: the fallback where neither the route nor the review gives a value, refused where either does. It cites `fairband-secondary/2` |
| Work item | A request in the inbox for an agent's research on one company, a valuation review or a full report, posted by the owner, an agent or the expiry rule; pending, claimed, done, declined or dropped |

## 14. Reviews: overrides and the reviewed value

An agent changes the method's inputs only through overrides, and the engine, not the agent, computes the value they give: the reviewed value. A review cites `fairband-review/1` (section 11). The mechanical method does not move: with no override every figure is the mechanical one, bit for bit.

### 14.1 What an override is

A change to one tagged input of 14.2, with its reason, its evidence and its lifetime (spec 2026-11-06 Tier 2, 10.1). The evidence is one item or more, each a kind (the kinds of a finding's source, a filing, a transcript, a URL, a vendor response or a base rate; a finding by its id; or a guidance record, `guidance:<id>`, 14.9), a reference and a note. The lifetime is lasting, or period-tied: a correction tied to one period, which keeps the value it corrected and holds until the company reports a newer period or the engine's reading of that figure changes (14.6). The record keeps the input as the engine read it on the snapshot it was made on (its old value, 14.4), the new value and its unit, the principal and the actor (the model identifier as it arrived), the time and the state: `applied`; `refused-locked`, `refused-not-input`, `refused-route`, `refused-unit` or `refused-evidence` with the rule's sentence (14.3); or `dropped` with the reason. A refused override is stored and applies nothing. One input has one applied override: a new one on the same input drops the old, "replaced by override <n>". Only an agent adds or drops an override this round.

### 14.2 The inputs an agent may override

| Tag | Routes that read it | The input | Range, the ends included |
|---|---|---|---|
| `phase` | every route | the phase (3.5); the route follows it | the six phases |
| `earnings.ntm` | Mature Growth | the NTM EPS of the anchor (5.2), from a clear full-year EPS guidance, or a net income one over the count the company's EPS is per (14.9) | above $0 to $1,000 a share |
| `revenue.ntm` | High Growth, Young Growth, Decline | the NTM revenue, year 1 of the path (5a.2) or the run-off (6a.2), from a clear full-year revenue guidance (14.9) | above $0 ($10^15 at most) |
| `growth.belief` | Mature Growth | the belief (5.4) | −20% to 60% a year |
| `path.hold` | High Growth, Young Growth | the held rate of years 4 to H (5a.3) | −20% to 300% a year |
| `path.step_2`, `path.step_3` | Mature Growth, High Growth, Young Growth, Decline | the consensus steps g_2 and g_3 (5.3, 5a.2, 6a.2) | −80% to 300% |
| `revenue.step_2`, `revenue.step_3` | Mature Growth | the revenue growth of year 2 or year 3 that the charge pays for (4.8, 5.3), in place of the consensus revenue step of its year, or of the earnings step where the consensus gives none or a thin one (5.3); period-tied only; its bridge lines "revenue step 2" and "revenue step 3", right after the consensus steps' (spec 2026-11-14 6.1) | −80% to 300% |
| `moat.class` | Mature Growth, Mature Stable, High Growth, Young Growth, Start-up | the moat class (4.9) | None, Narrow or Wide |
| `margin.target` | High Growth, Young Growth, Start-up | the target margin (5a.4, 6b.3) | −50% to 60% |
| `capital.sales_to_capital` | High Growth, Young Growth, Decline | sales-to-capital (5a.5, 6a.4), a multiple | 0.1 to 10 |
| `return.runway` | Mature Growth, Mature Stable | the runway return (4.6) | above 0% to 500% |
| `earnings.nri_gap` | Mature Growth, Mature Stable | the non-recurring gap (5.9, 6.1) and the anchor's basis (5.2) | −$100 to $100 a share |
| `earnings.sbc` | Mature Growth | the trailing stock compensation the anchor reads (5.2), a total in the reporting currency, in the basis test and the adjustment, from the cash flow statements of the trailing periods' filings; period-tied; lasting at 0 only, for a company that pays no stock (spec 2026-11-13 7.2, 7.3) | $0 or more ($10^15 at most, the policy's stand-in for no bound) |
| `earnings.sbc_basis` | Mature Growth | the anchor's stock-compensation basis (5.2), in place of the basis test, from the company's own definition of its adjusted EPS; lasting or period-tied (spec 2026-11-13 7.2) | `includes` or `excludes` |
| `earnings.ntm_nri` | Mature Growth | a one-time item a share that the NTM consensus carries and that does not recur, gains on an asset outside the business say, on the count the consensus is on: it comes off the NTM EPS before the cycle gap (5.2), a positive figure lowering it and a negative one, a one-time loss, raising it; period-tied only; its bridge line "one-time item in the NTM consensus", right before the cycle gap's (spec 2026-11-14 5) | −$100 to $100 a share |
| `earnings.cycle_gap` | Mature Growth, Mature Stable | the correction that takes the anchor's earnings to mid-cycle for a cyclical business: E on Mature Stable (6.1), the NTM EPS on Mature Growth (5.2); a positive figure lowers them, a negative one lifts a trough (policy v9) | −$100 to $100 a share |
| `decline.rate` | Decline | the decline rate d (6a.2) | −50% to 0% a year |
| `balance.debt`, `balance.cash` | High Growth, Young Growth, Decline, Start-up | the newest balance sheet's (4.2) debt and cash (5a.9, 6a.5, 6b.2); period-tied only, a correction of a vendor error | $0 or more ($10^15 at most, the policy's stand-in for no bound) |
| `balance.minority` | High Growth, Young Growth, Decline, Start-up | the newest balance sheet's (4.2) minority interest (5a.9, 6a.5 to 6a.7, 6b.2, 6b.4), a total in the reporting currency, at book unless an agent sets it: for a market value, or for a redeemable stake that the line leaves out; period-tied only; its bridge line "minority interest", after cash, and its JUDGMENT entry in place of the DATA entry `minority_interest` (spec 2026-11-13 13) | $0 or more ($10^15 at most, the policy's stand-in for no bound) |
| `shares.diluted` | every route | the diluted share count of 4.2, for a share class or units that convert to shares that the reported count leaves out (an Up-C's units); period-tied only. Shares not yet issued are `shares.committed`'s | above 0 to 10^12 shares |
| `shares.committed` | every route | shares the company has committed to issue that neither the diluted count nor the analysts' EPS holds yet (warrants, a forward sale, a mandatory convertible), which join the count in force; period-tied only; its bridge line "committed shares", after the diluted count's, and its JUDGMENT entry beside the DATA entry `shares_diluted` (spec 2026-11-14 4) | above 0 to 10^12 shares |
| `value.outside` | Mature Growth, Mature Stable, High Growth, Young Growth, Decline | a figure a share outside the business, after tax, added to the route's value a share: a stake, an asset held for sale, a settlement still owed (negative) | −$1,000 to $1,000 a share |

The numeric ranges are the policy's `override_ranges` (4.1), not settings. On a company in another currency the money ranges, the two NTM anchors, the two gaps, the one-time item, the stock pay, the balance corrections and the value outside the business, are read in the reporting currency: each end × the snapshot's rate (section 7), an end of $10^15 staying no bound, and the refusal names the currency after the figures, "earnings.ntm takes above 0 to 32,956 TWD a share" at 32.956 TWD per USD, "balance.debt takes 0 TWD or more"; the override's unit names the currency, `TWD a share`. A scaled end rounds half up to two decimals before the check, and the refusal prints it with the decimals it has, at most two ("−3,295.6 to 3,295.6 TWD a share"). A row refused for its currency, which names its code with no rate (7.4), has its ranges named in that code at 1.0: "earnings.ntm takes above 0 to 1,000 ARS a share". A guidance record's figures are in the reporting currency as the company states them; the two NTM anchors exclude their lower end, 0, as the routes refuse an anchor at or below zero, and so do the share count, as the guard refuses a reported count at or below zero (5.1), and the committed shares, and the runway return, which the charge divides by (4.8); an upper end of $10^15 stands for no bound, and a refusal names such a range by its lower end alone, "balance.debt takes $0 or more", "revenue.ntm takes above $0", "earnings.sbc takes 0 SEK or more" on a row in SEK; the three word inputs take their words. The Mature Stable value reads no consensus step (its cross-check, 6.4, reads the consensus as found), so that route does not read `path.step_2` or `path.step_3`. The two revenue steps are Mature Growth's alone: on the revenue-growth and Decline routes the path is the revenue series, so `path.step_2` and `path.step_3` are its revenue steps already.

Locked, refused `refused-locked` with the sentence given: r, "r is a locked input: the hurdle rate"; g_T, "g_T is a locked input: the terminal growth ceiling"; the runway years and the margin of safety, which the moat sets; the fade years, the fair band and the trim multiple, policy values; the terminal return, "the terminal return follows the moat"; the deciles and the growth word; and the reported figures, price, diluted shares, market cap, revenue, operating income and EPS, "<name> is reported data". Debt, cash and the minority interest change only through the three balance corrections, the diluted count only through `shares.diluted` ("shares_diluted is reported data" stays: the tag is the one door), the shares not yet issued only through `shares.committed`, and the NTM consensus only through the two NTM anchors, which a clear guidance may replace (14.9), and through `earnings.ntm_nri`, which takes a one-time item out of the NTM EPS. Any name not in the table is not an input.

### 14.3 The rules, in the order they are checked

`overrides.check_override` answers with the first rule an override breaks, else `applied`:

1. A locked input: `refused-locked`, the sentence of 14.2.
2. A tag that is not one of the twenty-five: `refused-not-input`, "<tag> is not an input".
3. A route that does not read the tag: `refused-route`, "the <route> route does not read <tag>". The route is the one the row takes after any applied `phase` override, so a Start-up row overridden to Young Growth takes `margin.target` on the revenue-growth route. The route is checked again at every recompute, at a refresh and in the web after an override is written or dropped: an applied override whose tag the row's route no longer reads, once a `phase` override is added, replaced or dropped or the classifier moves the phase, is dropped by the system, "the <route> route does not read the input" (14.6).
4. A value the input cannot take: `refused-unit`. A word outside the input's words, "moat.class takes None, Narrow or Wide", "earnings.sbc_basis takes includes or excludes"; a number outside its range, or a word where a number is due, "growth.belief takes −20% to 60%"; an NTM anchor at 0 or below, "earnings.ntm takes above $0 to $1,000 a share", or on a company in another currency the range at its rate (14.2), "earnings.ntm takes above 0 to 32,956 TWD a share"; a share count at 0 or below, "shares.diluted takes above 0 to 1,000,000,000,000 shares", and committed shares at 0 or below, "shares.committed takes above 0 to 1,000,000,000,000 shares", neither range scaled by a rate, since a count is not money; a stock pay below 0, "earnings.sbc takes $0 or more"; a minority interest below 0, "balance.minority takes $0 or more"; a one-time item outside its range, "earnings.ntm_nri takes −$100 to $100 a share"; a revenue step outside its range, "revenue.step_2 takes −80% to 300%"; a lasting override of a period-tied input, the three balance corrections, the diluted share count, the committed shares, the one-time item and the two revenue steps, "balance.debt is period-tied only", "balance.minority is period-tied only", "shares.diluted is period-tied only", "shares.committed is period-tied only", "earnings.ntm_nri is period-tied only", "revenue.step_2 is period-tied only"; and a lasting stock pay at any figure but 0, "a lasting earnings.sbc takes 0 only: a figure is period-tied": the stock pay is period-tied, and only a company that pays no stock keeps its figure from one period to the next (spec 2026-11-13 7.3). The range comes first, so a lasting stock pay below 0 reads "earnings.sbc takes $0 or more".
5. The evidence: `refused-evidence`. None at all, "an override needs evidence"; an NTM anchor, `earnings.ntm` or `revenue.ntm`, without an item of kind `guidance` among its evidence, "an NTM anchor override needs a guidance record among its evidence"; or a `growth.belief` or `path.hold` above the sales class's `sales_p90_5y` (4.1, `base_rates`) without a filing or a transcript among its evidence (the class of the snapshot's trailing revenue, its `revenue_ttm` input, as every route finds it: on a company in another currency the revenue ÷ the snapshot's rate, the class bounds being USD, and the class's `sales_p90_5y` restated by the row's differential (section 7); so the rule holds on a row a `phase` override moves onto a route that reads the input, a Start-up put in Young Growth say; no cap when the snapshot cites no trailing revenue), while `override_primary_evidence_above_p90` holds: "a belief above the base rate's 90th percentile needs a filing or a transcript", or "a held rate above …".

Rules 1 to 4 on the input's words or range, without the lifetime, are `overrides.check_input`, which a sensitivity reading's values meet (14.8).

Before the rules, the override route checks each evidence item of kind `guidance`: its reference names a current guidance record of the company as `guidance:<id>`, refused 400 "a guidance item cites its record as guidance:<id>" for any other form, 404 "no guidance record <id> for <symbol>" for a record that is not the company's and 409 "guidance record <id> is superseded by record <id>" for one a later call's record superseded (14.9), so an NTM anchor never rests on a record a later call replaced; nothing is stored. An override applied before a later record superseded the one it cites stays applied, and Appendix A shows the record's state (16.6). An override of an NTM anchor whose evidence cites guidance must cite, among its records, a gaap record of one of the anchor's metrics for a fiscal year, `eps` or `net_income` for `earnings.ntm` and `revenue` for `revenue.ntm`: else `refused-evidence`, "an NTM anchor override needs a gaap guidance record of eps or net_income for a fiscal year" (or "... of revenue ..."), answered 400 with nothing stored, as the reference checks are. A full year on the reported basis is the only guidance that may stand in for the consensus (14.9). The check reads the record, not the override's figure against it: an `earnings.ntm` on a `net_income` record is the agent's division of its midpoint by the count the company's EPS is per, a cited step (14.9, spec 2026-11-14 9). The rules then read the kind alone, as they read the others.

After the rules, the override route checks a lasting `earnings.sbc` of 0 that they applied against the snapshot it is made on (spec 2026-11-13 7.3): where the snapshot's own trailing stock pay, the engine's reading of the input on it (`sbc_ttm`, 14.4), is above zero, the store contradicts the nil, and the write is refused 400, "a lasting earnings.sbc of 0 is refused: the snapshot's trailing stock pay is above zero", with nothing stored. The period-tied form is the one for a period the vendor has wrong. A snapshot whose reading is 0 takes it, and so does one with no reading, a row refused before its route wrote one.

An override, a drop and a recompute are refused with the price refresh's own sentence, "settings changed: run a revalue" (or "policy changed", "method changed"), when the effective settings hash, the policy version or the method version is not the latest snapshot's (section 10); so the bridge's mechanical term is always the row's own figure. For the same reason the recompute first runs the engine with no override on the inputs it builds from the store that day, on the latest deciles, a row in another currency at the snapshot's own rate and its date (its `fx_rate` and `fx_date`, the `fx_rate` input's source with them), which are snapshot data as the price is (section 10): so a price refresh's pair quote dated after the price date, or a scoped refresh of a company in the same currency, which fetches the pair's history again, moves no snapshot's reproduction. When that run's route or value a share is not the latest snapshot's, the override or the drop is refused, "the store moved since snapshot <id>: run a revalue", and nothing is stored. A store change after the snapshot, a full refresh's deciles stored before its value stage or a peer's figures moved by a scoped refresh, would otherwise land on the bridge's first line as the agent's.

### 14.4 How a route reads an override

`route.value_company` takes the applied overrides by tag, and every route reads each input of 14.2 through one accessor, `route_steps.chosen`: the override's value when one applies, the route's own reading otherwise. What the route computes from the input follows it, its flags included; nothing else moves.

- `phase` replaces the classifier's phase right after the classification, the rest of its evidence as it was, so every guard and the route read it. A Start-up row overridden to another phase takes that phase's route and, as any such row, is refused without trailing figures; a row overridden to Start-up takes 6b. A row put in Decline without a five-year revenue trend or a positive last revenue, and with no `decline.rate` override, is refused "no revenue trend".
- `earnings.ntm` replaces the NTM EPS of the anchor (5.2), the consensus less the SBC adjustment, before the cycle gap comes off it: the guided figure stands in for the consensus with its items, so a cycle gap still applies to it and a one-time item (`earnings.ntm_nri`, below) does not, and "no positive forward anchor" reads the figure so set and lowered. The NTM window's periods are still required (5.2: four quarters, or two half-years for a half-yearly reporter), and a row without them is refused "incomplete NTM consensus" as before. The raw sum, the SBC adjustment, the basis test, the unit-error check, the forward P/E and the `ntm_eps` and `ntm_net_income` DATA entries read the consensus as found; the JUDGMENT entry stands in place of `ntm_eps`. The notes `thin-ntm`, `stock-pay-missing` and `sbc-basis-uncertain` (5.11) are not raised: the anchor is the guidance, not the window's consensus. A Mature Stable row's cross-check (6.4) reads the consensus as found.
- `revenue.ntm` replaces the NTM revenue, the sum of the NTM window's revenue means (5.2), before the positive check: year 1 of the revenue path, its g_1 and the rebuild key `revenue_path.ntm_revenue` on the revenue-growth route (5a.2); year 1 of the run-off, `runoff_path.ntm_revenue` and `consensus-recovery` on Decline (6a.2), where a guided figure makes usable a consensus whose sum is at or below zero. A row without the window's periods, or with one lacking its revenue mean, reads no override: the revenue-growth route refuses it "incomplete NTM consensus", and the Decline route shrinks years 1 to 3 at d. The `ntm_revenue` DATA entry, which cites the consensus as found, gives way to the JUDGMENT entry; the cross-check reads the consensus as found.
- `growth.belief` replaces the belief's rate (5.4); the fade runs to it.
- `path.step_2` and `path.step_3` replace a consensus step before anything reads it: on Mature Growth before the check that the path stays above −100% (5.3); on the revenue-growth route before the cap, the fall check and the path's flags (5a.2, 5a.3); on Decline as the revenue-growth route reads them (6a.2), except on a row without a usable consensus, whose years 1 to 3 shrink at d and read no step. A replaced step is never held, and `few-analysts` names only the steps the consensus still gives.
- `revenue.step_2` and `revenue.step_3`, an agent's revenue growth for year 2 or year 3 (spec 2026-11-14 6.1), replace the consensus revenue step of that year that the Mature Growth charge pays for (4.8, 5.3), or stand where the consensus gives none, in place of the earnings step: h of that year, and from year 3's the fade to the belief, follow it, and `growth-above-return` names it revenue growth (5.11). Nothing else reads them: the earnings path, the hold and the value's earnings stay on the earnings steps, so `path.step_2` and `path.step_3` still move the earnings steps, and the charge of a year only where no revenue step, the consensus's or an agent's, stands. A thin consensus revenue step (5.3) does not stand: the charge of its year takes the earnings step, which `path.step_2` or `path.step_3` moves, and an agent's revenue step stands over the rule, never thin. They are for a consensus revenue step that rests on few analysts or outruns the business's record, set from a guidance record (14.9) or a filing. The route's own path reads them; a cross-check does not read them (6.4). The route's `charge` entry keeps the steps read beside the steps found (5.12), and the engine's reading is the consensus revenue step as found, none where the consensus gives none (below). On a thin year the reading is still the step found, while the charge took the earnings step: so an agent's step at the step found moves the value, the charge reading it where it did not read the consensus's. Period-tied only: the override drops when that step moves or a newer period is on file (14.6); a lasting step would name another year once the base year rolls.
- `path.hold` replaces the held rate after the cap (5a.3): the agent's rate, which the cap does not bind. `consensus-above-base-rate` is not raised, and `held_growth_cap` notes "does not bind: the held rate is an agent's override".
- `moat.class` replaces the class (4.9), so the runway, the terminal return, the margin of safety and the levels move with it (4.7, 4.10); the cross-check (6.4) reads it too.
- `margin.target` replaces the peers' median (5a.4, 6b.3), the peers' lowest and highest margins kept for the sensitivity; on a row with no counted peers, the override stands alone, its own low and high, and the row is not refused for its target margin.
- `capital.sales_to_capital` replaces the measure (5a.5, 6a.4). Its record's `used` is "override", the measures kept beside it, so the peers neither flag `peers-from-industry` on Decline nor cite their rules for it.
- `return.runway` replaces the runway return of the charge (4.6), the return its fade starts at (4.8), and charges the runway years, even where the measured return had the charge off; `return-floored`, `return-without-goodwill` and `charge-zeroed` are not raised. The override is the return in force, so the moat's terminal rule (4.7) reads it in place of the measure, on every row: on a Wide row the terminal return is the override capped at r plus the spread and never below r, and on a Narrow or None row r (founder 2026-11-15; before, the terminal return stayed on the measure save on a row started at the return without goodwill). So the terminal return never stands above an agent's lower start; one override gives a floored row and a row whose measure stands just above r the same terminal return; and the value under the override alone is the row's own return sensitivity at the same figure (5.8). An agent who finds the growth ahead is bought, not built, sets the runway return at the row's r and takes the whole lift back, the terminal's part included.
- `earnings.nri_gap` replaces the non-recurring gap: Mature Stable's E (6.1), the clean EPS, EPV and `large-nri` (5.9), and the anchor's basis test (5.2), which reads trailing GAAP EPS less the agent's correction, the override less the gap found (nil without one): the test's mechanical reading moved by the correction alone, so a gap overridden at the figure found leaves the basis as it was. It keeps that effect on the test only while no `earnings.sbc_basis` is applied, since under one the test is not run. The third reason of `sbc-basis-uncertain` (5.11) reads the GAAP figure the test read, as reported less the correction, so it judges the gap the test judged. The cross-check (6.4) reads the gap too.
- `earnings.sbc` replaces the trailing stock compensation the Mature Growth anchor reads (5.2), a total in the reporting currency: the after-tax SBC a share of the basis test and of the adjustment. The intermediates' `sbc_ttm` is the figure the anchor read, the stored one without the override. In a review the two stock-pay notes (5.11) read the stock pay in force, the agent's figure, else the stored one: `stock-pay-missing` is not raised under the override, the agent having decided, and `sbc-basis-uncertain` judges materiality and the gap multiple on the figure the anchor read, so a row whose stored figure is nil can show it under an agent's figure. Period-tied, so each new period re-asks the question (14.6); or lasting at 0, for a company that pays no stock, which holds across periods until the store holds a trailing stock pay above zero (14.3, 14.6), so the agent does not set the nil again each period. The engine does not read the lifetime: a lasting 0 is any applied nil, the anchor taking no stock pay and `stock-pay-missing` not raised. A Mature Stable row's cross-check (6.4) reads neither stock-pay input.
- `earnings.sbc_basis` replaces the basis test's reading (5.2): the basis is `includes-sbc` or `excludes-sbc` as the agent says, and the test is not run, so no figure it reads is needed and `sbc-basis-unknown` is not raised. Under `excludes` the adjustment still takes the stock pay a share, nil when no stock pay is on file. `sbc-basis-uncertain` is not raised under it: the agent read the company's definition of its adjusted EPS and decided.
- Under an applied `earnings.ntm` the guided EPS stands in for the consensus less the adjustment, so `earnings.sbc` and `earnings.sbc_basis` each apply with a bridge line of zero, as any input an earlier override made moot; the anchor's basis and stock pay, and the forward P/E on the consensus as found, still follow them. Neither stock-pay note (5.11) is raised there, so neither input is needed.
- `earnings.ntm_nri`, a one-time item a share in the NTM consensus (spec 2026-11-14 5), corrects what the analysts' consensus carries and does not recur: gains on an asset outside the business that the consensus holds, say, so that the asset can be added through `value.outside` without counting twice. The NTM EPS on Mature Growth is the consensus less the SBC adjustment, less the item, then less any cycle gap (5.2); a negative figure, a one-time loss, raises it. It is a share on the diluted count, the count the consensus is on, so NTM net income moves by it × that count, and "no positive forward anchor" reads the figure so lowered. The basis test does not read it, and the unit-error check, the forward P/E and the `ntm_eps` and `ntm_net_income` DATA entries stay on the consensus as found; the override's JUDGMENT entry stands beside them, in place of none. Under an applied `earnings.ntm` the guided EPS stands in for the consensus with its items, so the item applies with a bridge line of zero, as any input an earlier override made moot. The route's own anchor reads it; a cross-check does not read it (6.4). The engine finds no such item: the route writes `ntm_nri`, the amount taken off, into its intermediates only under the override (5.12). Period-tied only: the engine's reading is nil, so the override drops when a newer period is on file (14.6).
- `earnings.cycle_gap`, the correction to mid-cycle earnings a share: the cycle gap lowers the anchor earnings, E on Mature Stable and the NTM EPS on Mature Growth, and feeds nothing else beyond the cross-check's anchor. The two gaps are taken off E on Mature Stable, and of the two the cycle gap alone off the NTM EPS on Mature Growth, after any one-time item (above). On Mature Stable the cycle gap comes after the engine's normalization: E, trailing net income less the non-recurring gap on the diluted shares, is scaled to the median margin when the margin flag fires (6.2), and the cycle gap on the diluted shares then comes off the E so scaled, so the engine's scaling and the agent's correction add. That E is `e_normalized`, the figure the value and EPV rest on (6.3) and the refusal of 6.1 reads; `e_trailing` stays E before both. On Mature Growth the NTM EPS is the consensus less the SBC adjustment and any one-time item, or an `earnings.ntm` override in its place, and then less the cycle gap (5.2); "no positive forward anchor" reads the figure so lowered, and the unit-error check reads the consensus as found, a check on the vendor's units. A negative gap lifts a trough's earnings to mid-cycle. The basis test, the clean EPS, the P/E, Mature Growth's EPV and `large-nri` read the non-recurring gap alone, and the `ntm_eps` and `ntm_net_income` DATA entries cite the consensus as found. The cross-check of a Mature Stable row (6.4) is the Mature Growth route's value on that row, so its NTM EPS is lowered as the route's is. The engine finds no cycle gap: a route writes `cycle_gap` into its intermediates only under the override.
- `decline.rate` replaces d (6a.2), never floored: `decline-rate-floored` is not raised.
- `balance.debt` and `balance.cash` replace the balance sheet's figures before a missing one counts as nil (5a.9, 6a.5, 6b.2): the bridge from firm value to equity, the rating's net debt and whether there is a debt line (6a.7), the cash floor and the burn cover (6b.4). A replaced line raises no `line-not-presented` flag. Under `balance.debt` the debt's sentence of `sheet-shift` (section 8) does not show, and under `balance.cash` neither cash line's: the agent read the figure.
- `balance.minority` replaces the newest balance sheet's minority interest before a line not presented, or a figure at or below zero, counts as nil, so the agent's figure is the one the three routes read (spec 2026-11-13 13): it comes off the revenue-growth route's equity (5a.9), off the Decline route's going-concern value, liquidation floor and distress-sale value (6a.5 to 6a.7), and off the Start-up route's cash floor, whose test `below-net-cash` reads it, while the Start-up enterprise value at the price adds it (6b.2, 6b.4). The distress probability's rules read debt and cash alone, as without it. It is how an agent sets a market value in place of the book figure, or counts a redeemable stake that the line leaves out, a tax-equity partner's stake, say. Under it the minority interest's sentence of `sheet-shift` (section 8) does not show. The secondary valuation does not read it (15.3).
- `shares.diluted` replaces the diluted count of 4.2 before the guard "no diluted share count" (5.1), so an agent's count values a row the vendor gives none, and everywhere the count is read: every value a share (the Mature Growth and Mature Stable values, the revenue-growth and Decline routes' equity a share, the Start-up cash floor), NTM net income (5.2, the NTM EPS × the count; the `ntm_net_income` DATA entry stays on the reported count, none without one: the consensus less the stock-pay adjustment in force × the reported count), after-tax SBC a share (5.2, the stock pay after tax over the count: the agent's count can change the basis test's reading where no `earnings.sbc_basis` is applied, and on a row whose basis reads `excludes-sbc` on a stock pay above zero it moves the adjustment, and with it the `ntm_eps` and `ntm_net_income` DATA entries, below), Mature Stable's E, whose two gaps a share come off on the count (6.1), the growth-route cross-check (6.4), `step-series-disagree` (5.3) and `share-count-gap` (section 8), which a count that reconciles the market cap with the price times the count clears. It is for a count that leaves out a share class or units that convert to shares, an Up-C's units say, whose consensus EPS is a Class A share's: there the earnings scale with the count, so on Mature Growth the value a share stands but for the stock pay a share, and the agent reads the consensus EPS's own share basis first. Shares not yet issued are not its case: they dilute the value through `shares.committed`, below. The Start-up requirement is a total on the market cap (6b.2), which the count does not move. Period-tied only, so each new statement re-asks the question (14.6).
- `shares.committed`, shares the company has committed to issue that neither the diluted count nor the analysts' EPS holds yet (spec 2026-11-14 4), joins the diluted count, the reported one or an agent's `shares.diluted`, in the count in force, after the guard (5.1): a row with no diluted count is refused "no diluted share count" as before. The count in force divides every value among the owners: the Mature Growth and Mature Stable values a share (5.6, 6.1) and the values by runway and by return, the growth-route cross-check's (6.4), the revenue-growth route's equity a share (5a.9), the Decline route's going-concern, floor and distress-sale values a share (6a.5 to 6a.7) and the Start-up cash floor (6b.4); and the implied growth, the margin the price needs and the implied decline rate solve to the price × it. A figure a share from the vendor or the consensus that becomes a total stays on the diluted count, the count it is on: Mature Growth's NTM net income and the stock pay a share, in the basis test, the adjustment and `sbc-basis-uncertain` (5.2, 5.11), so the `ntm_eps` and `ntm_net_income` DATA entries stand, and Mature Stable's two gaps (6.1); the forward EPS of the revenue-growth route's multiples too (5a.10). `step-series-disagree` names the diluted count, and `share-count-gap` reads it (section 8), since the market cap holds no share not yet issued. So x committed shares on a count of n lower the value a share by x ÷ (n + x) on every route, the mature routes included. On the revenue-growth, Decline and Start-up routes a `shares.diluted` raised by x gives the same value. The input is a count: cash the issue brings, a warrant's exercise price say, is the agent's to add, through `balance.cash` or `value.outside`, with its evidence. The Start-up requirement is a total on the market cap (6b.2), which it does not move. The intermediates keep `shares`, the count in force, and gain `shares_committed` beside it under the override only (5.12). Period-tied only: the engine's reading is nil, so the override drops when a newer period is on file (14.6).
- `value.outside`, a figure a share outside the business, after tax, applies after the route's value a share and before the levels: the value a share is the route's plus the figure, and the levels, price ÷ value and the designation read the sum (4.10), and so does the expected return's repricing term (5.10), the sum against the price; the multiples and the growth share read the price as they are (5.9). The implied growth (5.7), the margin the price needs (5a.10), the implied decline rate (6a.9) and the cross-check's implied growth (6.4) solve to the price less the figure, the business's share of the price, on a refusal that keeps them too. The values by runway and by return (5.8) and by margin (5a.11) each add the figure, but the value-spread flag reads the business's figures, the values before the figure is added: the flag speaks of the business's sensitivity to its own inputs, which a figure outside would mask or fake. A sum at or below zero refuses the row with the route's reason, "value not positive under the reinvestment charge" on Mature Growth, Mature Stable (6.1) and the revenue-growth route, "value not positive in the run-off" on Decline. The Start-up route has no value and does not read it. The intermediates' `value_per_share` stays the route's own, the business's, so the drawer's arithmetic reproduces, and `outside_value_per_share` carries the figure, written only under the override; a price refresh reads it (section 10). Evidence of any kind, as the other inputs: a filing is the usual source, and a figure taken from secondary coverage says so in the report.

An overridden input is tagged JUDGMENT, kind judgment, beside DATA and RULE. Its entry is named by its tag and carries the number (none for a word, which leads the note), the input's unit, and the override's provenance: the source "override <n>, <principal> <actor>", "override 1, agent claude-opus-5-5" say (the principal alone without an actor, "override 1, system"), entered at the override's time, the reason as the note, and after a split the figure as entered (14.6). Every result, a refusal too, cites these entries last, in the bridge's order (14.5), in place of the route's own entry for the input (`ntm_eps`, `ntm_revenue`, `belief`, `path_step_2`, `path_step_3`, `moat_class`, `target_margin`, `sales_to_capital`, `runway_return`, `sbc_ttm`, `debt`, `cash`, `minority_interest`, `shares_diluted`), so no DATA or RULE entry carries an agent's figure under the vendor's or the policy's provenance, with one exception: the `ntm_eps` and `ntm_net_income` DATA entries of a Mature Growth row are the consensus less the stock-pay adjustment in force (5.2), cited to the vendor's estimates, so they follow each input that moves the adjustment: an applied `earnings.sbc` or `earnings.sbc_basis`, an `earnings.nri_gap` that changes the basis, and a `shares.diluted` on a row whose basis reads `excludes-sbc` on a stock pay above zero or whose basis the count changes, since the stock pay a share is the stock pay after tax over the diluted count; a `shares.committed` moves neither. The two gaps, the one-time item, the two revenue steps, the stock-pay basis, the committed shares and the outside value stand in place of none: the committed shares' entry stands beside the reported count's DATA entry `shares_diluted`. The count's entry and the committed shares' are in shares, the one-time item's and the outside value's in USD a share, the stock pay's in USD or the reporting currency's code, the stock-pay basis's in `basis`, its word leading the note. The intermediates hold the figures the route used.

The engine's reading of an input (`overrides.current_reading`), the bridge's old value and the value a period-tied override corrects, comes from a result's intermediates: the phase; the NTM EPS, the Mature Growth anchor as stored with any cycle gap added back and not the one-time item (`ntm_nri`), the figure an `earnings.ntm` override stands in for to give the same result, since a guided EPS stands in for the consensus with its items; the NTM revenue, the route's year 1 (`ntm_revenue`, else the revenue path's or the run-off's), none on a Decline row with no usable consensus; the belief's rate; the held rate, the hold when one is set, else g_3 as resolved; the steps, the route's `path_steps`, else the revenue path's or the run-off's as found; the revenue steps, the consensus's as found (`charge.revenue_steps_found`, never an agent's step, which `charge.revenue_steps` holds), none where the consensus gives none and on every route but Mature Growth, a result stored before the steps found were kept reading its `charge.revenue_steps`, which then held them as found; the moat, a Start-up row's from its moat class input; the target margin; sales-to-capital; the runway return, none with the charge off (4.6), whose figure is the terminal return and charges nothing; the non-recurring gap; the stock pay, the trailing figure the Mature Growth anchor read (`sbc_ttm`), which a period-tied `earnings.sbc` corrects and which a newer stored figure changes (14.6); the stock-pay basis, the anchor's basis in the input's words, `includes` or `excludes`, none on an unknown basis; the one-time item, nil on a Mature Growth result without the override (on a refusal, the route its phase takes) and none on any other route: the engine finds none; the cycle gap, nil on a Mature Growth or Mature Stable result without the override (on a refusal, the route its phase takes) and none on any other route; d; and for debt and cash the reported figure the result cites, none when no line is on file (where the bridge counts nil) and none on a row refused before its route cites the line, whose intermediates hold only the nil; the minority interest, the line the result cites (`minority_interest`) as the newest sheet presents it, none where the sheet does not present it and a figure at or below zero as it stands, though the route counts either as nil, and none on a row refused before its route cites the line, as for debt and cash; the diluted count, the count the result cites (its intermediates' `shares` less any `shares_committed`, else the reported count's entry or the JUDGMENT entry in its place), so the committed shares never pass for a corrected count; the committed shares, nil on every result: the engine finds none; and the value outside the business, nil on every route that reads it (on a refusal, the route its phase takes) and none on Start-up: the engine finds none. It is none where the result holds no such figure. An override at the engine's reading moves nothing: its bridge line's delta is zero. The one exception is a revenue step on a thin year (5.3), whose reading is the step found while the charge took the earnings step: there an agent's step at the step found moves the value.

### 14.5 The review: the recompute and the bridge

A review is the engine's result under the company's applied overrides (`review.review_company`), built from the mechanical result. The overrides apply one at a time in a fixed order, the method's step order: `phase`, `earnings.ntm`, `revenue.ntm`, `path.step_2`, `path.step_3`, `revenue.step_2`, `revenue.step_3`, `path.hold`, `growth.belief`, `earnings.nri_gap`, `earnings.sbc`, `earnings.sbc_basis`, `earnings.ntm_nri`, `earnings.cycle_gap`, `margin.target`, `capital.sales_to_capital`, `return.runway`, `decline.rate`, `moat.class`, `balance.debt`, `balance.cash`, `balance.minority`, `shares.diluted`, `shares.committed`, `value.outside`. Each line of the bridge is one engine run with that override and every earlier one; its old value is the engine's reading on the mechanical result (14.4), its new value the override's.

Each input has a reader's label for its bridge line (`overrides.LABELS`): phase, NTM earnings, NTM revenue, consensus step 2, consensus step 3, revenue step 2, revenue step 3, held rate, belief, non-recurring gap, stock pay, stock-pay basis, one-time item in the NTM consensus, mid-cycle earnings, target margin, sales to capital, runway return, decline rate, moat, debt, cash, minority interest, diluted shares, committed shares and outside the business, in the order above. The two revenue steps' lines follow the consensus steps' (spec 2026-11-14 6.1), each old value the consensus revenue step as found, none where the consensus gives none (14.4). The two stock-pay lines follow the non-recurring gap's, the figure first and the basis second (spec 2026-11-13 7.2), each old value the engine's reading on the mechanical result (14.4). The one-time item's line follows them, right before the cycle gap's, the order in which the two come off year 1 (5.2); its old value is the engine's nil (spec 2026-11-14 5), and under an applied `earnings.ntm` its delta is zero. The cycle gap's line follows it and reads "mid-cycle earnings", so the reader sees a cycle judgment and not a one-time item. The count's line, the committed shares' and the outside value's close the bridge, after the balance sheet's: the committed shares join the count the line before set, the diluted count or an agent's. The committed shares' old value is the engine's nil, and so is the outside line's, whose delta after a step that valued is the figure.

A line's delta is its value a share less the value of the last step that valued, the mechanical value or an earlier line's, so the deltas sum to the reviewed value less the mechanical value. A line whose step refuses shows the refusal in place of a delta, "value not positive under the reinvestment charge" say, and a review whose last step refuses is a refusal with that reason; the next line that values measures from the last step that valued. A mechanical refusal that an override turns into a value, a Start-up row reclassified, starts the bridge at no mechanical value: the first valuing line carries the whole value as its delta. The reviewed value's levels and designation are 4.10's on the same edges, the levels on the reviewed moat.

A review is stored with its result, its value, levels and designation, the mechanical value, the bridge, the ids of the overrides inside and `fairband-review/1`, and with the policy version, the settings hash and the deciles refresh it ran under, as a snapshot is. Where the review fills the screen's Reviewed column (it counts on the latest snapshot and has a value), the screen's row and the drawer's and report page's meters show the review's fair band, value, buy and trim levels, and price ÷ value and the designation read at the latest snapshot's price on the effective policy's edges, never the word stored at an older price; the Mechanical column keeps the mechanical value beside them (spec 2026-11-06 Tier 2, 18.1 and 18.2). There the row's Flags column and its Warnings count read the flags of the review's result, and with the count the Clean filter and the sort by warnings, and the drawer lists the review's flags under a line that says they are the review's (spec 2026-11-13 8a); the result is stored whole, so nothing is recomputed. A row that a secondary valuation fills keeps the snapshot's flags, since a secondary valuation has none, and so does a row whose review refuses. The Clean filter still needs a value from the engine, so a row the engine refused is not clean, whatever a review made of it. The drawer's inputs and intermediates and the Start-up section's `below-net-cash` test stay the snapshot's.

### 14.6 A review follows its snapshot

A review follows its company's snapshots. Every refresh that writes a snapshot for a company with applied overrides writes a review on it, with its log row (14.7) and no ledger row: the system's review is recorded in the log, the agent's override and drop in the ledger. A full or scoped refresh and a revalue recompute it on the new inputs; a price refresh reprices the latest review, the one on the snapshot it reprices, as it reprices the snapshot (section 10), its value, levels and window kept and its designation moved; for a company in another currency its value in the currency is kept and its value and levels, US dollars, move with the rate the price refresh reads, its mechanical value is the repriced snapshot's and each bridge line's change a share moves by the old rate ÷ the new (section 10). A company the refresh cannot value (a failed fetch or valuation) gets its refusal snapshot's refusal as its review, with no mechanical value and an empty bridge; a review the engine cannot compute is a refusal, "review failed: <class>: <message>", with an empty bridge, and the mechanical snapshot stands. Before a recompute at a full or scoped refresh or a revalue, and in the web's recompute after an override is written, replaced or dropped, an applied override whose tag the row's route does not read (14.2) drops, by the system, with its ledger row resting on the snapshot and the reason "the <route> route does not read the input", the route named as rule 3 of 14.3 names it, "the Decline route does not read the input" say (spec 2026-11-14 8). The row's route is the route of its applied `phase` override, else of its mechanical phase. At a refresh the `phase` override's own tests (below) run first, so the route is the one the row then takes, and the route's test runs before the other overrides' tests, so a period-tied override on a route that no longer reads it drops with that reason, not as a changed reading. So a phase that moves, at a refresh or when an agent adds, replaces or drops a `phase` override, takes with it each override only the old route read, and that override's JUDGMENT entry no longer stands in place of the entry the new route reads (14.4): a stale `moat.class` no longer hides the moat class a Decline row reads. The web's answer names the overrides the system so dropped. An unclassified row, with no `phase` override and no mechanical phase, has no route and keeps every override: a row unclassified for one refresh does not lose its review's inputs. Each period-tied override left is then checked (spec 2026-11-13 11a): it drops, by the system and with the reason, on the first of two tests that holds. First, a newer period is on file: the trailing period end the new snapshot reads is after the trailing period end of the snapshot the override was made on, "a newer period is on file than the one the override corrected"; on the fiscal-year fallback (4.2) the period end is the fiscal year's. Second, the reading changed: the engine's reading of its input on the new snapshot differs from the value it corrected, "the input the override corrected changed". The day the statements were fetched, the data date, drops nothing: a refetch of the same statements leaves every correction applied. From method 1.3 every result whose inputs have trailing figures carries the trailing period end, `trailing_end`; for a result without one, a snapshot made before 1.3 say, the period end is the `as_of` of its diluted share count, which assembly dates at the newest quarterly row's period end, the fiscal year's when that row holds no count (4.2), so such a snapshot may read as an older period, and its correction then drops as "a newer period is on file". The trailing inputs' own `as_of` is the newest fiscal year's end and is not read. Without both period ends the first test is not read and the reading decides. A lasting override of an NTM anchor drops the same way, by the system and with its ledger row, "the guided year has passed", when the fiscal year its guidance record guides (an `eps` or a `net_income` record for `earnings.ntm`, a `revenue` record for `revenue.ntm`; the latest, when it cites more than one that fits the anchor, 14.3) is before the new snapshot's base year, the fiscal year that holds most of the four quarters after the last reported one (5.3), as every route that reads one finds it: the next twelve months have left the guided year behind. Without four such quarters there is no base year and the override stays. A lasting `earnings.sbc`, which takes 0 alone (14.3), drops the same way, by the system and with its ledger row, "the store holds a trailing stock pay above zero", when the trailing stock pay the new inputs assemble (4.2) is above zero: the company now pays stock, or the vendor now holds its figure (spec 2026-11-13 7.3). A trailing figure that is nil, or missing because a period lacks the line, keeps it. The test reads the assembled figure, not the result's `sbc_ttm`, which a row refused before its route does not carry. An override binds to the snapshot it was made on for provenance and applies to the latest snapshot and every later one; snapshots are appended at every price refresh, so there is no stale conflict. A review carries its snapshot's validity window and expires with it, a review recomputed after an override past the snapshot's next earnings date included, and it carries its snapshot's price facts (section 2's price columns), which anchor on the date the refresh valued the snapshot and which no override moves, a review written on a later day from an override included; a review that is a refusal has no value.

An override made before a split applies on the new basis (spec 2026-11-14 7). Where the applied overrides become the engine's, for the review at a full or scoped refresh and a revalue, for the web's recompute after an override is written or dropped and for a sensitivity reading (14.8), an override in dollars a share (`earnings.ntm`, `earnings.nri_gap`, `earnings.ntm_nri`, `earnings.cycle_gap`, `value.outside`) is divided by ρ, and one in shares (`shares.diluted`, `shares.committed`) multiplied by it. ρ is the product of the ratios of the splits on file (section 2; a reverse split's ratio below one) that the figures of the snapshot reviewed saw and the figures of the snapshot the override was made on did not. A snapshot's figures saw a split dated at or before its basis date: the earlier of the price date and the data date of the snapshot that assembled them, the snapshot itself, or, for a snapshot a price refresh made, the company's latest earlier snapshot that a full or scoped refresh or a revalue made, else the snapshot's own two dates. The split rule applies a split only from the price date on (4.2), and the data date is the day the figures were fetched (4.10), so a refresh on a split's day before the US close, whose quote is the day before's, has not seen the split; a price refresh fetches no split and assembles nothing (section 10), so a snapshot it made carries the basis of the figures it repriced. A ratio, a total and a word are never scaled, a ρ of 1 leaves the figure as entered, and so does an override whose snapshot is not on file. Three cases escape the basis date: a refresh whose call for the splits failed beside a good fetch of the statements reads its figures on the splits on file from before (section 2), though its dates may postdate a newer split; a refusal snapshot of a failed fetch or valuation carries the refresh's day as its data date, not its stored figures'; and the statements can be read from an answer fetched on one day and the splits from an answer fetched on another, when a fetch stopped on a 429 after the statements and a full refresh inside the reuse window fetched again only the calls that failed (section 2), or when the vendor lists a split a day late, while the basis date reads the statements' data date, so it can say the figures saw a split they did not, or the reverse. The stored override keeps the figure entered. The bridge line's new value is the figure in force, and so is the JUDGMENT entry's (14.4), whose note adds, after the reason, the figure as entered and the splits in order: "entered as $30.00 a share before the 2-for-1 split of 27 Jan 2027", an amount in another currency after its code ("entered as SEK 30.00 a share"), a count in millions ("entered as 60.0M shares before …"), a reverse split "the 1-for-10 reverse split of …". The review's result carries the note `override-split-scaled` (section 8), naming each such input. The engine knows no split: the store scales the figure where its overrides become the engine's. The drop tests read the figure the override corrected, never scaled: a period-tied override whose reading the split rule itself rescales (`earnings.nri_gap`, `earnings.ntm`, `shares.diluted`, 4.2) drops, as before, as "the input the override corrected changed" once the split moves that reading, so the scaling serves the lasting overrides and those whose reading is nil.

### 14.7 The log

One row for each review written: the time, the company, the refresh, the snapshot and the review; the mechanical phase and value; the reviewed phase and value, none on a refusal; the latest secondary value (section 15), none without one; the price; the mechanical designation; and the latest published report's stance. It measures whether the agents' overrides help, read once it has rows.

### 14.8 A sensitivity reading

How much of the route's value is assumption, as a grid an agent cites and does not compute (spec 2026-11-06 Tier 2, 13.8; `engine.sensitivity`). The agent names two inputs of 14.2 and up to five values of each; the engine runs on the company's latest snapshot's inputs under the applied overrides, each on that snapshot's share basis after a split as the review reads it (14.6), with the two inputs set to each pair of the values, an applied override on either giving way to the pair's value, and each cell is the value a share, or none where the engine refuses ("value not positive under the reinvestment charge", say). The pair in force is marked: the row and the column whose values are the readings the engine runs on, each the applied override's value when its input has one, else the engine's own reading of the input (14.4) on the result under the applied overrides, the review's result or, with none applied, the mechanical one; a number within 1e-12, a word equal; the first such pair, none when no pair is it. A revenue step (`revenue.step_2`, `revenue.step_3`) with no applied override is marked at the growth the Mature Growth charge read for its year, h_2 or h_3 of `charge.growth_by_year` (4.8): the consensus revenue step where the charge read one, else the earnings step as resolved, since an override at that figure gives the result in force, while the engine's reading of 14.4 stays the step found, none where the consensus gives none. On a thin year (5.3) the pair marked is the earnings step's, not the step found's, which the charge did not read. Rows and columns are counted from 1, as the ref `sensitivity:<id>/<i>/<j>` counts them (16.2).

Nothing is overridden: a reading is not an override and the review does not move. Its values are refused as an override of the input would be by rules 1 to 4 of 14.3, with the rule's sentence: a locked input, a tag that is not an input, a route that does not read the tag, a value outside its words or its range. The route is the one the row takes after any applied `phase` override; with the phase on one axis, the other input must be read by the route of each of its phases. The evidence rules and the lifetime do not apply. An axis holds one to five values, "an axis holds one to five values", and the two inputs differ, "the two tags differ". A reading is an agent's write, refused with the price refresh's sentences and "the store moved since snapshot <id>: run a revalue" as an override is (14.3). It is stored as of its snapshot, with the current review it ran under (none without an applied override), the writer, the policy version and the settings hash, and is recorded in the ledger as `sensitivity`.

### 14.9 Guidance

A guidance record is a figure the company itself gave on an earnings call for a future period (spec 2026-11-06 Tier 2, 9; `store.guidance`). An agent writes it with `guidance_add` (`POST /api/companies/{symbol}/guidance`; the owner's post is refused 403, "a guidance record is an agent's write") and reads it with `guidance`; the call's date comes from the `transcript-dates` pass-through and its sentence from the `transcript` one. The record: the company; the metric, one of `revenue`, `eps`, `net_income`, `operating_margin`, `free_cash_flow`, `capex`, `revenue_growth` and `eps_growth`, `net_income` a total in the reporting currency, the net income that belongs to the company's shareholders, as its EPS does (spec 2026-11-14 9); the period, a fiscal year (`2026`) or a quarter (`2026Q3`); the low and the high, equal for a point, a percent as a fraction; the basis, `gaap`, `adjusted` or `constant-currency`; the call's date; the sentence verbatim and its source, the transcript's `response:` reference or a filing; the writer and the time. A value outside these is refused 400 with its sentence, in that order: "metric is one of revenue, eps, net_income, operating_margin, free_cash_flow, capex, revenue_growth or eps_growth", "period is a fiscal year or a quarter (2026, 2026Q3)", "low is above high", "basis is one of gaap, adjusted or constant-currency", "a guidance record needs its text", "a guidance record needs its source". A write is a ledger row of kind `guidance`.

The server computes the midpoint, (low + high) ÷ 2, and for a fiscal-year `revenue`, `eps` or `net_income` record on the `gaap` basis the step, midpoint ÷ the actual of the year before the guided one − 1, that actual the statement line as `fact:<symbol>/revenue/<year>`, `fact:<symbol>/eps_diluted/<year>` or `fact:<symbol>/net_income/<year>` reads it (16.2). There is no step without that actual on file, on an actual at or below zero, on a quarter or on another metric; nor on an `adjusted` or a `constant-currency` record, since the actual is the statement's GAAP figure: an adjusted figure over it is a rate across two bases, no growth, and a constant-currency figure over it is growth at constant currency, which the agent derives and names as such. A step such a record holds from before this rule is not read. So the agent never computes a rate. The call's date orders a company's records of the same metric, period and basis, of which one at most is current, so a GAAP and an adjusted figure of one call both stay current: a new record of a call on or after the current record's supersedes it, and the old record keeps its figures and names its successor; a new record of an earlier call, the previous call's figure written late, is stored already superseded by the current one. Every record stays, so a later report reads the track record, guided against delivered.

The rules (the founder, 2026-11-07). Guidance reaches the valuation only when it is clear: a number or a range for a stated metric, a stated period, on a reported basis; a range takes the midpoint. Words ("upper teens"), one-sided forms ("at least"), adjusted figures with no bridge to GAAP and constant-currency figures are recorded and discussed in the report's call section, and do not enter the valuation: the consensus stands. There is no table of words. A next-quarter figure feeds no override, even when it completes the fiscal year (three quarters reported and the guided fourth): the completed year is behind every input the engine reads, since the next twelve months then run into the year after. The completed year's figure is read against the consensus for that year in the call section, and a quarter that completes no year against the consensus for that quarter. The use is the agent's best effort, never required: a report with no guidance override is a normal report. The server records every record and checks none of these rules but the evidence rule of 14.3, which is the agent's discipline otherwise.

Guidance reaches the reviewed value only through an override of one of the two NTM anchors, of one of the two consensus steps, `path.step_2` and `path.step_3`, or of one of Mature Growth's two revenue steps, `revenue.step_2` and `revenue.step_3` (14.2, 14.4), whose evidence names the record (14.3); an NTM anchor needs such an item, a gaap record of one of its metrics for a fiscal year, and drops once that year is before the base year (14.6); a consensus step or a revenue step takes one as any override takes evidence. `earnings.ntm` stays an EPS. A company that guides net income alone backs it with that record (spec 2026-11-14 9). The agent derives the EPS with the `derive` tool, so the report's number check sees the division: the record's midpoint over the count the company's EPS is per, the count the shareholders' net income is shared among. On most rows that is the reported diluted count, `snapshot:<id>/inputs/shares_diluted`. Where an applied `shares.diluted` corrects the count of the same shares (the vendor's figure was wrong or stale, or it left out a class of common stock that shares the earnings), it is that figure, cited through its bridge line. Where an applied `shares.diluted` adds the units of an Up-C, whose share of the earnings is a minority's (14.4), it is the reported count, since the EPS is a Class A share's. It never holds committed shares. The server does not divide, and checks the record, not the override's figure against it (14.3). The mechanical value reads no guidance record. The review log (14.7) then shows the company's own outlook against the consensus, company by company. A report cites the record's figures as `guidance:<id>/low`, `/high`, `/midpoint` and `/step` (16.2), and its Appendix A lists the guidance on file (16.6).

## 15. The secondary valuation

A valuation computed by the engine from inputs an agent supplies (spec 2026-11-06 Tier 2, 11), the fallback where neither the route nor the review values the company (no phase or route fits, or the route refuses, and no review values it), and only there: where the company's latest snapshot has a value, or its current review on that snapshot has one, the call is refused (15.4), so no second value stands beside a route's or a review's value or weighs on the stance. The agent's judgment is the drivers or the path, built from its own research; the engine keeps the projection, the discounting, the terminal and the bridge to equity, so the number is a tool output. A secondary valuation cites `fairband-secondary/2`; one made before the drivers form and the grid cites `fairband-secondary/1` and reads as it was (section 11). Neither the mechanical value nor the review reads it.

### 15.1 The two forms

A secondary valuation takes exactly one of two forms.

- **The standard form, `drivers`**: the free cash flow to the firm projected by Damodaran's FCFF model from drivers the agent sets. N years, from `secondary_years_min` (3) to `secondary_years_max` (15; 4.1); a revenue growth and an operating margin for each year, N entries each; a tax rate; a sales-to-capital; and a terminal growth g from −100% to g_T (3.0% at the defaults; the currency's g_T for a company in another currency, 15.3), the ends included. The revenue growths, the operating margins, the tax rate and the sales-to-capital lie inside their pairs of `secondary_driver_ranges` (4.1), the ends included: a revenue growth −80% to 300%, an operating margin −50% to 60%, a tax rate 0% to 50%, a sales-to-capital 0.1 to 10.
- **The parts form, `parts`**: one part or more, at most `secondary_parts_max` (10; 4.1). One part is a discounted cash flow, several a sum of parts, for a conglomerate or a company whose cash flows the agent builds segment by segment. A part is a name; the free cash flow to the firm in the reporting currency (US dollars for a company that reports in them, 15.3) for years 1 to N, N from `secondary_years_min` to `secondary_years_max`; a terminal growth g from −100% to g_T; and a note.

With either, a reason and evidence: one item or more, each of the kinds an override's evidence takes (14.1), one at least for the drivers or the path itself, with no `figure` (15.3).

### 15.2 The arithmetic

r is the effective policy's required return, locked as for an override (14.2), restated into the reporting currency for a company in another currency (15.3). The standard form projects from revenue_0, the trailing revenue as the engine reads it (4.2; `fact:<symbol>/revenue/ttm`). In the lines, g_t and m_t are year t's revenue growth and operating margin, t without a subscript is the tax rate, s the sales-to-capital, and ROIC_T the terminal return of 4.7 on the moat class as reviewed:

    revenue_t = revenue_{t−1} × (1 + g_t)
    EBIT_t = revenue_t × m_t;  NOPAT_t = EBIT_t × (1 − t)
    reinvestment_t = max(0, revenue_t − revenue_{t−1}) ÷ s
    FCFF_t = NOPAT_t − reinvestment_t;  PV_t = FCFF_t ÷ (1 + r)^t
    terminal FCFF = NOPAT_N × (1 + g) × (1 − g ÷ ROIC_T) when g > 0; NOPAT_N × (1 + g) when g ≤ 0
    terminal = terminal FCFF ÷ (r − g), discounted by (1 + r)^N
    firm value = Σ PV_t + the discounted terminal

A year whose revenue falls reinvests nothing, so a decline does not create cash; a terminal growth at or below zero releases no capital, so a decline creates no cash in the terminal either. A terminal growing above zero reinvests g ÷ ROIC_T of its NOPAT, the engine's own stable-growth rule (4.8). The formula is the route's, but on a Wide moat ROIC_T here is 4.7's rule on the ten-year aggregate ROIC (15.3), where the route's own terminal takes the runway measure of 4.6 on the two mature routes and 4.7's rule on the return at the target margin on the growth and Start-up routes.

The parts form discounts each part:

    PV_t = fcff_t ÷ (1 + r)^t, t = 1 … N
    terminal = fcff_N × (1 + g) ÷ (r − g), discounted by (1 + r)^N
    part value = Σ PV_t + the discounted terminal

and its firm value is the sum over the parts.

On both forms, equity = firm value − debt + cash; the value a share = equity ÷ the diluted shares. The levels are 4.10's on the moat class: the current review's when the company has one (14.5), else the snapshot's, else None. The designation is 4.10's on the snapshot's price ÷ the value a share. A value a share at or below zero is kept as computed, with no levels and no designation; it is not a refusal.

### 15.3 The trailing revenue, the terminal return, debt, cash and shares

On the standard form the server reads the trailing revenue, revenue_0, at the call as `fact:<symbol>/revenue/ttm` resolves it (16.2): the statements' trailing figures as the engine reads them (4.2), the last four quarters or the last two half-years, else the last fiscal year; `financials` serves the same figure as its `ttm` revenue. It is read from the statements, not from the snapshot's inputs, so a row the classifier leaves unclassified, whose snapshot cites no trailing revenue, can still take the standard form. ROIC_T is 4.7's rule applied to a given return, as for a sensitivity value: r on a Narrow or None moat; on a Wide moat the ten-year aggregate ROIC of the snapshot's intermediates (`roic/aggregate`, which the Mature Growth and Mature Stable routes write), capped at r plus the spread and never below r, and r where the snapshot holds no aggregate (the other routes, or too few ROIC years). The moat is the one the levels take (15.2), the current review's, else the snapshot's. The record stores both figures beside its projection, so the value rebuilds.

The server reads the debt and the cash at the call as assembly reads them for the growth, Decline and Start-up routes (5a.9, 6a.5, 6b.2): the newest balance sheet's (4.2), a quarter's or a fiscal year's, short-term plus long-term debt, and its cash and equivalents plus short-term investments, a line it does not present counting as nil. An applied `balance.debt` or `balance.cash` override (14.2) counts in its place. The agent may pass the debt or the cash itself where the balance sheet is wrong or missing; each figure passed needs its own evidence item, one whose `figure` names it, `debt` or `cash`. The record stores the debt, the cash and the shares used, and where the debt and the cash came from: `statements`, the newest balance sheet's; `agent`, a figure the agent passed or an applied override's; `none`, no line on file, so the figure counts as nil (0). The shares are the snapshot's diluted count: its `shares_diluted` input, else its intermediates' `shares`. So the secondary valuation divides by the snapshot's reported diluted count, and neither an agent's `shares.diluted` nor its `shares.committed` (14.2) reaches it. The secondary valuation subtracts no minority interest, the balance sheet's or an applied `balance.minority`'s: an agent who needs one raises the debt it passes, with evidence (spec 2026-11-13 3).

A company in another currency (section 7) is valued in its reporting currency, on the policy restated by its snapshot's inflation differential (`inflation_differential`, 7.5): r, the terminal growth's bound g_T and the Wide moat's cap at r plus the spread are the currency's, and the grid's rates with them (15.5). The trailing revenue, the balance sheet's debt and cash and an applied `balance.debt` or `balance.cash` override are in the currency already, and the agent passes its path, its debt and its cash in the currency. The snapshot's USD price comes into the currency at the snapshot's rate (its `fx_rate`, 7.3), so the engine runs on one currency; on the way out, as the route's value goes (7.4), the value a share and each cell of the grid are ÷ that rate, US dollars a share (per ADS for an ADR), the levels are 4.10's on that value and the designation reads the snapshot's USD price against it. The firm value, the debt, the cash, the equity, the projection's lines and the parts stay in the currency. The record stores the rate, units of the currency per USD; a USD row's rate is 1 and its differential 0, so its valuation is as before, and a record made before method 1.1 reads at the rate 1. Worked (spec 2026-11-10 11.1's test table, the test company at 8.0 a dollar): at a differential of 2%, r is 11.180%, the Wide moat's cap 15.180% and g_T 5.060%, so a terminal growth of 5% is taken and one of 5.1% refused, "the terminal growth is −100% to g_T, 5.06%", the bound with the decimals it needs, at most two, so the sentence never names the figure it refuses; the value a share, the levels and the grid's cells are the currency's ÷ 8.0, and the firm value stays in the currency.

### 15.4 The refusals

Each is answered with its sentence, and nothing is stored:

- a company whose latest snapshot has a value, "the route values <symbol>: the secondary valuation is the fallback where it refuses", checked after the call's own refusals and the version refusals below and before every other refusal on the snapshot. The founder's ruling of 2026-11-08: no discounted cash flow where the route values the company, the secondary valuation the fallback only. A secondary valuation made before the rule on a valued snapshot stays on file and reads as it was;
- a company whose current review rests on the latest snapshot or on one the latest reprices (16.2) and has a value, an override having routed it (a `margin.target` on a row refused for its target margin, a `phase`), "the review values <symbol>: the secondary valuation is the fallback where neither the route nor the review values the company", checked right after the route's. The review is the main method at work, so the fallback is not needed: the founder's ruling of 2026-11-08, the secondary valuation the fallback where the main method is rejected. A current review that refused does not refuse the call: the company takes the fallback. A secondary valuation made before the override stays on file and reads as it was;
- on the call itself, in this order: a reason that is blank, "a secondary valuation needs a reason"; no evidence, "a secondary valuation needs evidence"; both forms, "a secondary valuation takes drivers or parts, not both"; neither, "a secondary valuation needs drivers or parts"; no item for the drivers or the cash-flow path itself, every item naming a `figure`, "the path needs its own evidence item"; a debt or a cash passed without its own evidence item, "an explicit debt needs its own evidence item" (or cash);
- a company whose latest snapshot was valued under another policy version, other settings or another method version: the price refresh's sentences, "settings changed: run a revalue" (or "policy changed", "method changed"), as for an override (14.3);
- an evidence item of kind `guidance` that names no current guidance record of the company: the override's 400, 404 and 409 (14.3);
- a snapshot refused for a reason no call can meet, answered with the refusal's own sentence (spec 2026-11-10 7.6): the reporting currency's four, "reporting currency unknown", "no exchange rate for <code>", "no inflation projection for <code>" and "<code>: expected inflation <π>% above the 10% bound" (7.6), since the valuation would have no currency, rate or restated rates to run on; and the listed-share basis's, "share basis unclear: ..." (4.2), since the share count it would divide by is on no known basis. The ADR flag and a currency other than USD are no ground of their own: method 1.1 values such a row (section 7), and the foreign-filer refusal of earlier methods, "foreign filer: currency and share-unit handling not built yet", which a snapshot valued before 1.1 may carry, is met first by "policy changed: run a revalue", since such a snapshot was valued under a policy before v11 and the policy version is checked first;
- a snapshot with no positive diluted share count, "no diluted share count";
- on the standard form, no trailing revenue on file, or one at or below zero (15.3), "no trailing revenue on file";
- the standard form's bounds, in this order, each naming its rule with the effective policy's numbers: an N below `secondary_years_min` or above `secondary_years_max`, "a secondary valuation runs 3 to 15 years"; a list without N entries, the revenue growth's checked first, "revenue_growth has <n> entries for <N> years" ("1 entry" when n is 1), or the same for `operating_margin`; a driver outside its pair of `secondary_driver_ranges`, every entry of a list checked, in the order revenue growth, operating margin, tax rate, sales-to-capital, "revenue_growth is −80% to 300%", "operating_margin is −50% to 60%", "tax_rate is 0% to 50%", "sales_to_capital is 0.1 to 10"; a g below −100% or above g_T, or not a number, "the terminal growth is −100% to g_T, 3.0%" (the currency's g_T for a company in another currency, with the decimals it needs, at most two, "5.06%", 15.3);
- the parts form's bounds, in this order, each naming its rule with the effective policy's numbers: no part, "at least one part"; more than `secondary_parts_max`, "at most 10 parts"; a path shorter than `secondary_years_min` or longer than `secondary_years_max`, "a part has 3 to 15 years"; a g below −100% or above g_T, or not a number, "the terminal growth is −100% to g_T, 3.0%".

### 15.5 The grid, and the record as of its snapshot

Every secondary valuation carries a three-by-three grid: the value a share by the same arithmetic at the rates r − 1 point, r and r + 1 point (the columns) across the terminal growths g − 1 point, g and the lower of g + 1 point and g_T (the rows), every other input held, ROIC_T among them. On the parts form a row moves every part's terminal growth together, each capped at g_T, and the rows read as the shift applied: −1 point, 0, and +1 point while any part rises a full point under g_T, else the largest rise to g_T, +0 when every part is at g_T; a top row below a point is labelled "capped at g_T".
