The centre cell is the value a share; for a company in another currency the rates are the currency's and each cell is ÷ the rate, US dollars a share as the value is (15.3). The grid is a display of how much of the value is assumption, never a value: the rate in force stays r, and no level, designation or log reads a cell. A grid whose lowest rate is at or below its highest growth has no terminal in that corner; only settings that put r − 1 point at or below g_T allow it, and that valuation carries no grid.

A secondary valuation is stored on the company's latest snapshot with its form, `drivers` or `parts`; on the standard form its drivers as the agent set them and its projection, every year's revenue, EBIT, NOPAT, reinvestment, free cash flow and present value, the terminal free cash flow, the terminal and its present value, with the trailing revenue and the terminal return it was projected from (15.3); on the parts form its parts, each with its path, its g, its note, its present values, its terminal, the terminal's present value and its value; its grid, the rates, the rows (the terminal growths, or on the parts form the shifts) and the nine values a share, none where it carries no grid; the firm value; the debt, the cash and the shares with the two sources; the equity; the value a share; the levels, price ÷ value and the designation at the snapshot's price; the snapshot's rate (15.3); the reason and the evidence; the writer (the principal, and an agent's model identifier and client) and the time; and the policy version, the settings hash and the deciles refresh it ran under. It is a ledger row, `secondary`. It is never changed, and no refresh recomputes or reprices it: it is as of its snapshot, its window that snapshot's validity window (4.10), and it stays in the company's history after later refreshes, read with its date. The agent makes a new one at its next review while neither the route nor the review values the company. The owner may make one too: it is a tool's output, not an override. The log (14.7) reads the newest one's value a share. On a refused row whose Reviewed column it fills, the screen's row and the drawer's and report page's meters show its band and levels, and its designation read at the latest snapshot's price as a review's is (14.5).

### 15.6 Worked numbers

The tests pin these within 1e-4 (spec 2026-11-06 Tier 2, 20.1). r 9%, g 3%; one part, free cash flow 100 in year 1 growing 5% a year for 5 years (100, 105, 110.25, 115.7625, 121.550625); debt 200, cash 100, 100 diluted shares. The present values 91.7431, 88.3764, 85.1332, 82.0091 and 78.9996, Σ 426.2614; the terminal 121.550625 × 1.03 ÷ 0.06 = 2,086.6191, discounted 1,356.1592; firm value 1,782.4206; equity 1,682.4206; value a share 16.8242. A second part, 20 flat for 5 years at g 0: Σ PV 77.7930; terminal 20 ÷ 0.09 = 222.2222, discounted 144.4292; part value 222.2222; with both parts the value a share is 19.0464. A g of 3.5% is refused, as are a path of 2 years and an 11th part.

The standard form (spec 2026-11-06 Tier 2, 20.4), pinned within 1e-4: r 9%, ROIC_T 12%, g 2%, tax rate 25%, sales-to-capital 2.0, trailing revenue 1,000, N = 3 with growth 10%, 8% and 5% and a margin of 20% in every year; debt 200, cash 100, 50 diluted shares.

| t | revenue | EBIT | NOPAT | reinvestment | FCFF | PV |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 1,100.00 | 220.00 | 165.00 | 50.00 | 115.00 | 105.504587 |
| 2 | 1,188.00 | 237.60 | 178.20 | 44.00 | 134.20 | 112.953455 |
| 3 | 1,247.40 | 249.48 | 187.11 | 29.70 | 157.41 | 121.549402 |

Σ PV 340.007444; the terminal FCFF 187.11 × 1.02 × (1 − 0.02 ÷ 0.12) = 159.043500; the terminal 159.043500 ÷ 0.07 = 2,272.05, discounted 1,754.439476; firm value 2,094.446920; equity 1,994.446920; value a share 39.888938. The grid's centre cell is that value; its corner at r 8% and g 1% is 44.220636, every cell the same arithmetic at its pair. With growth −5% in year 3 the revenue falls and reinvestment_3 is 0.

## 16. Reports: the number check

An agent's report is published whole or rejected whole (spec 2026-11-06 Tier 2, 13). Every number in it is a cite of a stored value, so the reader part traces to the records the engine and the store hold; the agent writes the sentences, never a figure of its own. The check is `fairband.reports.check`; the references are `fairband.reports.refs`.

### 16.1 A report

A submission names a mode, a stance, the sections, the key judgments, and optionally kill criteria, news items and the work item it completes. A section is a heading and its parts; a part is plain text or a cite, `{cite, ref, period?}`: the text as the reader sees it, the reference of the stored value, and the period it claims. The sections are spec 13.1's twelve, under these headings in this order; a valuation review has ten, leaving out sections 2 and 3 (design.md 7.5). The valuation and its detail are section 8's alone; the rest of the report argues the business and its outlook.

| # | Heading | Mode | Holds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Summary | both | The stance, the thesis in two or three sentences, the value against the price and the levels, the phase in one sentence, three reasons for, three risks, what would change the view, the end of the validity window |
| 2 | The business | full report | The products, the revenue by product and by region, what drives revenue, the customers, the unit economics |
| 3 | Industry and competition | full report | The value chain, the industry's size and growth, the competitors, the company's position, the moat by Damodaran's five sources |
| 4 | Financial performance and health | both | One part for each of the six tables (16.5), plain sentences that cite its cells |
| 5 | The last earnings call | both | The verdict on the thesis, the quarter against expectations, management's story claim by claim with a verdict on each, the guidance, the positives and the negatives, the questions, what the thesis takes from it |
| 6 | The bull case and the bear case | both | The debate about the business and its outlook, in arguments; no valuation arithmetic, input or tag |
| 7 | The thesis | both | A position on each claim of section 5, accepted, rejected or held open, then the case that settles the debate and leads to the stance |
| 8 | Valuation | both | The phase, the growth the price needs, the belief, the mechanical and the reviewed value with each override in words, the secondary valuation where neither the route nor the review values the company, the mid-cycle basis of a cyclical, the sensitivity grid |
| 9 | Price and multiples | both | The price facts, and the trailing and the forward P/E against their history (16.7) |
| 10 | Recent news | both | The dated items that matter to the value |
| 11 | Risks and kill criteria | both | The risks, and the measurable events that would prove the thesis wrong |
| 12 | Key judgments | both | The record: each judgment's decision in one sentence, naming the override it produced |

A rejected report stores nothing but its ledger row, "rejected: <n> findings", which points at no record; the answer lists the findings. A published one is stored as the company's next revision (from 1) on the snapshot its cites rest on, with the review and the secondary valuation it cites (else the company's current review when it rests on the latest snapshot or one it reprices, and on a refused latest snapshot its newest secondary valuation when that one does; where the route or the review values the company, or the company can have none (16.3), a secondary valuation on file is the report's only when a cite names it), its judgments, kill criteria and news items, and it completes the work item it names. A valuation review reuses the company's newest full report, or says there is none. The latest revision is the company's report; the earlier ones stay. The latest report's stance is the one the review log reads (14.7).

### 16.2 The references

A reference is `<form>:<path>`, resolved for the report's company; a record of another company names nothing, and neither does an id that is no whole number in ASCII digits or is past the largest integer the store holds. Each resolves to a value (a number, a word, or none where the record holds no figure), a unit, a period and the snapshot it rests on.

| Ref | Resolves to | Unit |
|---|---|---|
| `snapshot:<id>/value`, `/price`, `/levels/<name>` (buy, fair_low, fair_high, trim) | the snapshot's value a share, price and levels | per-share |
| `snapshot:<id>/price_to_value` | price ÷ value | multiple |
| `snapshot:<id>/inputs/<name>` | a tagged input; a word input (the moat class) reads the word leading its note | the input's |
| `snapshot:<id>/intermediates/<path>` | an intermediate by its keys and list indexes, `belief/rate` | none |
| `snapshot:<id>/phase/<field>` | a field of the classifier's evidence (3.5), `phase`, `growth_3y` | the field's |
| `review:<id>/value`, `/price_to_value`, `/levels/<name>`, `/bridge/<n>/delta`, `/old`, `/new` | the review's figures (14.5); the bridge's line n counted from 1 | per-share; old and new the input's |
| `review:<id>/intermediates/<path>` | an intermediate of the reviewed result by its keys and list indexes, as a snapshot's: the growth the price needs under the review, `implied_growth` | none |
| `secondary:<id>/value`, `/firm_value`, `/equity`, `/debt`, `/cash`, `/shares`, `/price_to_value`, `/levels/<name>` | the secondary valuation's figures (15) | per-share, currency, count, multiple |
| `secondary:<id>/parts/<n>/pv/<t>`, `/parts/<n>/fcff/<t>`, `/parts/<n>/value`, `/parts/<n>/terminal`, `/parts/<n>/terminal_pv`, `/parts/<n>/terminal_growth` | on the parts form, part n's present value and free cash flow of year t, its value, its terminal value, the terminal's present value and its terminal growth; n and t counted from 1 | currency; percent for the terminal growth |
| `secondary:<id>/years/<t>/<line>`, `/terminal_fcff`, `/terminal`, `/terminal_pv` | on the standard form, the projection's line of year t, `revenue`, `ebit`, `nopat`, `reinvestment`, `fcff` or `pv`, t counted from 1; the terminal free cash flow, the terminal and its present value | currency |
| `secondary:<id>/drivers/<name>`, `/drivers/<name>/<t>` | on the standard form, a driver as the agent set it: `years`, `tax_rate`, `sales_to_capital`, `terminal_growth`; year t's `revenue_growth` or `operating_margin`, t counted from 1 | count for the years, multiple for sales-to-capital, percent for the others |
| `secondary:<id>/grid/<i>/<j>` | the grid's value a share at row i (a terminal growth, or the shift on the parts form) and column j (a rate), each counted from 1 | per-share |
| `sensitivity:<id>/<i>/<j>` | a sensitivity reading's value a share at row i and column j, each counted from 1 (14.8); a refused cell holds no value, so a cite of it is `unresolved reference` | per-share |
| `sensitivity:<id>/rows/<i>`, `/cols/<j>` | the axis value row i or column j names, the input's value the cells of that row or column were read at; a word axis (the moat class) its word | the input's |
| `fact:<symbol>/<line>/<year, quarter or ttm>` | a stored statement line as the engine reads it: a fiscal year's (the store's lines by the engine's names), a stored quarter's by its fiscal label (`2027-Q1`), or the trailing figures'; `ttm` reads the trailing figures, a period of digits alone a fiscal year, and any other period is looked up among the stored periods' labels as the quarters read serves them (16.9), a half-year's as FMP stores it among them; a quarter's lines are the trailing figures', of any stored quarter in the reporting currency, and a quarter left out for its currency, or not on file, is `unresolved reference` | currency; per-share for EPS; count for the diluted shares |
| `table:<symbol>/<table>/<row>/<year>` | a cell of the financial tables (16.5) | the row's |
| `estimate:<symbol>/<period>/<field>` | a stored consensus figure, the period a fiscal year (`2026`) or a quarter (`2026-Q4`) | currency; per-share for EPS; count for the analysts |
| `close:<symbol>/<date>` | a stored daily close | per-share |
| `multiple:<symbol>/<series>/<date>`, `multiple:<symbol>/<series>/<stat>` | a point of the trailing (`pe`) or the forward (`pe_forward`) P/E history by its ISO date, or a statistic of the ten years to the series' newest day: `avg_10y`, `median_10y`, `high_10y`, `low_10y` (16.7); a date with no point (a loss, a day off the series, a day before the ten years) is `unresolved reference` | multiple |
| `peer:<symbol>/<peer>/<field>` | a mature peer's figure as assembly reads it (5a.4): `market_cap`, `margin`, `margin_years`, `sales_to_capital`, `roic` | currency, percent, count, multiple, percent |
| `finding:<id>` | a finding's value, unit and period, as the agent recorded it (a percent as a fraction) | the finding's |
| `guidance:<id>/low`, `/high`, `/midpoint`, `/step` | a guidance record's figures (14.9), of its period; a step the record does not take holds no figure, so a cite of it is `unresolved reference` | the metric's: currency for revenue, net income, free cash flow and capex, per-share for EPS, percent for the operating margin and the two growth rates; percent for the step |
| `derived:<id>` | a derivation's value (16.4) | the derivation's |
| `filing:<id>/<sheet>/<row>/<col>` | a cell of a stored filing report by its sheet's served name (16.8) and its row's and cell's indexes from 0 (spec 2026-11-11, 5.5): the cell as served, a number as it is stored or a string as the filing report's reads show it (16.8), every run of white space one space and the ends trimmed, so a cite of a word fits the text the agent read; the name writes `~` as `~0` and `/` as `~1`, as a JSON pointer's token does, and where two sheets serve alike the first is meant; a response of another endpoint, a sheet, a row or a cell that is not there, and a row that is not a label with its cells name nothing | none |
| `response:<id>/<json pointer>` | a field of a stored vendor response (RFC 6901) | none |
| `policy:<name>` | a value the Methodology page cites, by name or by name and a moat class or an index (`runway_years.Wide`, `fair_band.0`) | the value's |

The units are the reads': currency (a total, in the statements' currency), per-share, percent (a fraction), multiple, count, score and years. An engine input's unit maps onto them: a currency code is currency (the share price per-share), "<code> a share" per-share, a ratio percent (but the ADR ratio, `adr_ratio`, ordinary shares per ADS, and the sales-to-capital, `sales_to_capital`, revenue over invested capital, each a multiple), shares and the other counted things count, years and scores as they are; a word, a date or a rate ("<code> per USD", `fx_rate`) has none. So a figure of a company in another currency (section 7), its input, an override's bridge line or a sensitivity axis, reads as a USD one does; the cite puts the code before it in the text (16.3). The period is the fiscal year, the quarter, `ttm`, or the close's or the multiple's date for the forms that have one, the finding's and the guidance record's as recorded, none for the others. A `snapshot:`, `review:`, `secondary:` or `sensitivity:` ref rests on its record's snapshot, a `derived:` ref on its operands'; a `finding:` or `guidance:` ref, a fact of the company, on none.

The reprice rule (spec 13.2): a report rests on the company's latest snapshot. A later snapshot that reprices the cited one, with the same data date, method version, policy version, settings hash and value, counts as the cited one, so a price refresh during the research does not reject a report; the report keeps the cited snapshot, whose price date its header shows. A revalue or a full refresh in between rejects it.

### 16.3 The check

Before the number check run five structural checks, in this order, each refused with one sentence: the mode's section count, twelve for a full report and ten for a valuation review; the stance, one of the policy's `stance_words` (buy, hold, trim, avoid; 4.1); the required judgments, `phase`, `moat` and `belief` on every report, `roic` where the route is Mature Growth or Mature Stable and `target_margin` where it is the revenue-growth route or Start-up, the route of the company's current review when it rests on the latest snapshot, else the snapshot's (a refused row's phase's), one of each kind; the headings, 16.1's in order, a heading named against the one in its place ("section 5 is headed 'The last earnings call', not 'Life-cycle phase'"); and a secondary valuation on a refused snapshot: where the latest snapshot is refused and no current review values the company, the company's newest secondary valuation (15) must rest on it or on one it reprices (16.2), else "the report needs a secondary valuation on snapshot <id>", the latest's id. The rule names both the route and the review, as the secondary route's refusals do (15.4). A valued latest snapshot needs none, and neither does a refused one whose current review rests on it or on one it reprices and has a value; a refused one that neither values needs the fallback, as does one whose current review refused; a secondary valuation made before a revalue does not count. The rule does not apply where the company can have none for a refusal of 15.4 the agent cannot fix (spec 2026-11-10 7.6): a latest snapshot refused for its reporting currency ("reporting currency unknown", "no exchange rate for <code>", "no inflation projection for <code>", "<code>: expected inflation <π>% above the 10% bound") or its listed-share basis ("share basis unclear: ..."), or one with no diluted share count; a snapshot refused "foreign filer: currency and share-unit handling not built yet" before method 1.1 counts among them. The ADR flag and a currency other than USD are no exemption of their own: such a row refused for another reason takes the fallback as any row does. Where the route or the review values the company, or the company can have none, the report publishes without one and Appendix A names the reason (16.6).

Then every part of every section is read, and so is every judgment's decision, and each failure is a finding `{where, what, detail}`: `where` is the section and the part, counted from 1 in the submission, and the text at fault, "section 1, part 3: $12.345", or the judgment and its field, "judgment moat, decision: 11.2%". The six failures, in the server's words:

- `a number with no reference`: a quantity in plain text, or in a judgment's decision, which carries no cite.
- `unresolved reference`: a cite whose ref names nothing, or names a figure the record does not hold.
- `wrong unit`: a cite whose suffix does not fit the ref's unit, or whose number is not the ref's value at the report's rounding; the detail gives the value as the cite should read. A word cited against a number, or a word that is not the ref's, is also `wrong unit`.
- `wrong period`: a cite whose period is not the ref's (`FY2025` reads `2025`, and a quarter reads the same with its dash or without, `2027-Q1` as `2027Q1`); a cite naming no period passes.
- `wrong snapshot`: a cite whose ref rests on a snapshot other than the company's latest or one the latest reprices; the detail names the latest's id. A `review:` cite must be the company's current review: the newest review on its snapshot, with the current review that one or its reprice; the detail names the current review's id. A `sensitivity:` cite must be of a reading taken under the review that is still the company's current one on its snapshot, by the same rule on the review the reading stored (14.8); a reading taken under none is stale once a review is current on its snapshot; the detail says "the review moved".
- `a derived figure the engine has not emitted`: a `derived:` cite with no stored derivation of the company, or one with no value; and a multiple (`2.4x`) in plain text, a ratio written out rather than derived.

A cite gets one finding, the first of: wrong snapshot, not emitted, unresolved, wrong period, wrong unit. A plain part gets one for each quantity.

The token rule. A quantity is a money amount, any number a currency sign leads (`$`, `€`, `£`, `¥`, a letter prefix before it or not) whatever letters follow it (`$1,234.50`, `$5`, `$ 5`, `US$5`, `$391bn`, `$126M`, `$5mm`, `$3.2T`, `€1.5B`, `£126m`); a decimal joined to a one-letter scale, `m`, `b`, `k` or `t` in any case (`1.5B shares`, `2.3M users`, `4.5k`); a number followed by a unit word, with or without a space and in any case, whatever its digits (`12 billion`, `11.2pp`, `50bps`, `5 basis points`, `5 percentage points`, `2 Years`; the words `thousand`, `million`, `billion`, `trillion`, `mn`, `mm`, `bn`, `tn`, `pp`, `bp`, `bps`, `percentage points`, `basis points`, `years`); a percentage (`11.2%`, `11.2 percent`, `5 per cent`); a multiple (`12.3x`, `5 x`, `12.3×`, and `X` joined to the number, `15X`, `1.12X`, never after a space: `5 X-ray`); a decimal; or an integer of two or more digits; a sign (`-` or the minus `−`) or parentheses around it included. Otherwise a one-letter scale does not count: an integer joined to a letter with no currency sign is a name (`3M`, `401k`, `4K`, `Phase 2b`, and so `150M users` too). These are not quantities: a year from 1990 to 2099; an ISO date, or a day of the month beside a month's name (`27 Oct`), unless a decimal, `%` or a scale follows it or a currency sign leads it (`March 12%`, `June 30.5 million`, `US$30 June` are quantities); a number joined to letters (`FY2025`, `Q3`, `COVID-19`, `5G`, `21st`); a filing form (`10-K`, `8-K`, `20-F`); a section's or an item's number (`section 13.3`, `Item 10`); anything in a URL or after a slash (`fairband-review/1`); a list's number at a line's start with white space after it on the same line (`1. `, `12. `, `12) `) and a table's separator row (`|---|---|`), which are marks, not quantities, though a number alone on its line (`12.`) is a quantity; and an integer of one digit with no scale or unit word. Small counts are written in words.

A cite's text is one number: an optional sign, `+`, or `-`, `−` or parentheses for a negative, so a bridge line reads `+$28` (design.md 7.4); `$`; the digits with their thousands separators; a scale word (`thousand`, `million`, `billion`, `trillion`, or `k`, `m`, `mn`, `b`, `bn`, `tn`); and a suffix, `%` (or `percent`, or `pp` or `percentage points`: a difference of two percents, a percent's suffix compared as `%` is, so `2.0 pp` cites 0.02), `x` (or `×`, or `X` joined to the number), `years`, or none. The rounding is design.md 9.2's, by the ref's unit:

| Unit | Written to | Suffix |
|---|---|---|
| currency (a total) | whole dollars, or with a scale word at the cite's own precision | `$` or none |
| a price (`snapshot:<id>/price`, `/inputs/price`, a `close:`) | cents | `$` or none |
| per-share (a value a share, a level, an EPS) | cents, or whole dollars as a value and its levels are written | `$` or none |
| percent | one decimal of the fraction × 100 | `%`, or `pp` for a difference of two percents |
| multiple | two decimals | `x` or none |
| years | one decimal | `years` or none |
| score | two decimals | none |
| count | a whole number, or with a scale word at the cite's own precision; a count takes no `$` | none |

The cite must be written at that rounding or coarser on its grid, so `$12.345` is never a price and `$150` is not one either, and lie within half a step of the value: `22.7%` cites 0.22702, `23%` does not. A ref with no unit (an intermediate, a vendor field) takes any suffix and is compared at the cite's own precision. Four rules more, so a report reads as design.md 9.1 and 9.2 ask:

- A scale word. A total may be cited in its scale with `$` or none, `$391 billion`, `$2.1bn`, `$1.5B`, `$126M`, and a count with none, `12 billion`, compared at the cite's own precision in that scale (the decimals it was written with) and with two significant figures at least: `$2.1 billion` cites 2,123,000,000 and `$2.10 billion` does not; `$2 billion` and `$0.4 billion` are `wrong unit`, the detail naming the rule, as is `$1.0 billion` of a share count. A whole-dollar total still passes.
- Signed outflows. The lines the store signs as outflows, capital spending, dividends and buybacks (as `fact:` lines, the tables' rows of them and the snapshot's trailing inputs; not the net investing or financing flows), may be cited by their size: "capital spending was $126,000,000" cites −126,000,000. A cite with a sign (`−$126,000,000`, `($126,000,000)`) must match the signed value.
- A ratio as a percentage. A `%` cite may cite a multiple that is a ratio of two figures in one unit, a `derived:` ratio or a table's ratio cell, compared at × 100: "stock compensation was 9.5% of net income" cites a ratio of 0.0952.
- Prices to cents. A share price, `snapshot:<id>/price`, its `inputs/price` and a `close:`, is cited to cents; a value a share, a level or an EPS to cents or to whole dollars (design.md 9.2, "values to whole dollars").

A figure in another currency than USD (section 7) takes no new rule (spec 2026-11-10 7.4). `$` is for a figure in US dollars: the price, the value a share, the levels, a close, a grid's cells. A figure of a company in another currency, a statement line, an input in the currency, a guidance record's or an override's figure, a secondary valuation's total, is cited with no `$`, its code or its sign (€, £, ¥) written in the text before the cite, "TWD " and then the cite `331.25`. The check reads the cite as a number with no suffix, which fits a currency or a per-share unit at that unit's rounding; the code or the sign alone in the text is no quantity, while `€1.50` written in the text is one, so the number goes in the cite.

### 16.4 Derivations

The agent's arithmetic is a tool output (spec 13.4): `ratio` (a ÷ b), `difference`, `sum`, `product` (a × b), `growth` (a ÷ b − 1) and `cagr` ((a ÷ b)^(1/years) − 1), over two refs of 16.2 resolved for the company, a table cell included. The unit: multiple for a ratio of two figures in one unit; percent for growth and cagr; a difference or a sum keeps the shared unit; a product with a multiple, then with a percent, keeps the other operand's unit, and a figure a share times a count is a currency total; none otherwise. A division by zero, a cagr over a negative ratio and a result that is not finite have no value. A ref that names no number refuses the call. Each result is stored with its refs and cited as `derived:<id>`; it rests on the snapshot its operands rest on.

### 16.5 The financial tables

The six five-year tables of the report's section 4 (spec 13.5; design.md 9.3), computed by the server from the stored statements and the engine's readings, each cell with its ref: growth and profitability, cash flow, balance sheet and solvency, returns on capital, capital allocation, quality scores. A cell that is one stored line cites it as `fact:`; any other cites itself as `table:`. The agent cites the cells and derives none of them; a cell the lines cannot give holds no figure, and a cite of it is `unresolved reference`. Among the cells' notes, two mark a figure that would mislead: the interest coverage holds no figure on a stored interest expense of zero, "the interest expense on file is zero", and a year's ROIC on average invested capital at or below zero keeps the engine's figure, "average invested capital is at or below zero", a figure with the wrong sign or none and no meaning (4.5). The returns table's hurdle rate is the policy's r; on a company in another currency, whose ROIC is in its currency, it is r restated by the inflation differential of its latest snapshot when that snapshot carries a rate (7.5), the r its route discounts at, so the ROIC chart compares like with like.

### 16.6 The appendixes and the export

Appendix A and Appendix B are generated from the records when the report is read, never written by the agent. Appendix A: the snapshot the report rests on, with the reporting currency its money figures are in (section 7; USD for a row with no rate); every input with its tag (DATA, RULE or JUDGMENT, the review's entries when the report rests on a review), its value at full precision and its provenance; the overrides inside the review with their reasons and evidence; the guidance on file, the company's guidance records written by the report's time, newest first, each with its metric, period, low, high, midpoint, basis and call's date, current or superseded as of the report, and the overrides inside the review that cite it; the bridge, each line with its input's label in a reader's words (14.5); the secondary valuation's currency, its totals' (the snapshot's reporting currency), and the rate it ran at (15.3), which the page shows as a row, "SEK 8.00 per USD", and the export's line names, "Secondary valuation 9, from parts, in SEK at 8.0 SEK per USD, its value a share in USD", on a company in another currency; its drivers and its projection year by year, or its parts year by year, and its grid (15.5), or, for a report with none on a company that can have none (16.3), the reason, "no secondary valuation: the route values the company", "no secondary valuation: the review values the company" (the report's review has a value), or "no secondary valuation: " followed by the snapshot's refusal in its own words where it is one the agent cannot fix, "no secondary valuation: share basis unclear: the newest period's count 1,680.7 m against the quote's 210.1 m (the ADR ratio 10)" or "no secondary valuation: no diluted share count"; the checks that ran, the five structural checks and the six failures, each with its rule; the versions (method, policy, settings hash, the review's and the secondary valuation's methods); and the classifier's evidence. Appendix B: the terms of section 13 the sections use.

The references. The refs the sections cite are numbered from 1 in the order each is first cited across the sections, one number a distinct ref: a ref cited again, with a period or without, keeps its number. Each has its source in words, one sentence a form: a snapshot's "snapshot 412, the price, priced 3 Nov 2026", the path in words and the snapshot's price date; a review's "review 88, bridge line 1 (belief), the change in the value a share"; a secondary valuation's "secondary valuation 9, year 3 FCFF"; a sensitivity reading's "sensitivity 7, row 1 column 2"; a statement line's "the revenue line of fiscal 2025 on file", a quarter's "the revenue line of Q1 of fiscal 2027 on file"; a table cell's "the growth table, revenue growth, fiscal 2025"; a consensus figure's "the consensus for Q4 of fiscal 2026, EPS"; a close's "the close of 3 Nov 2026"; a multiple's "the trailing P/E, 30 Oct 2026" or "the forward P/E, the ten-year average"; a peer's "ACME, margin"; a finding's "finding 88: 10-K 2025, p. 41", the finding's source; a guidance record's "guidance record 5: EPS for fiscal 2027, the midpoint"; a derivation's "derivation 7: the ratio of <a> and <b>", its operands' refs; a vendor response's "FMP segments, observed 1 Nov 2026"; a filing report's cell "FMP filing report of fiscal 2027 Q1, LEASES: <the row's label>, column 0 (3 Months Ended, Sep. 30, 2026), observed 11 Nov 2026", the stored call's fiscal year and period, the sheet's served name, the row's served label, the column's index with its words where the sheet names it (16.8; "column 0" alone where it does not), and the day the report was observed; a policy value's "policy required_return, <the policy version the report's snapshot was valued under>". A date or a name stands where one serves, a record's id where nothing else names the record. A company in another currency (section 7) has its paths in words too: `fx_rate`, an intermediate or an input, "the exchange rate, units of the reporting currency per US dollar"; `fx_date` "the exchange rate's date"; `currency` "the reporting currency"; `inflation_differential` "the inflation differential against the US dollar"; `value_in_currency` "the value a share in the reporting currency"; the input `adr_ratio` "the ADR ratio, ordinary shares per ADS". The report's JSON carries the list as `references`, `[{n, ref, source}]`.

The Markdown export opens with a line naming the company, the mode, the date, the actor and the revision, then the stance and the snapshot with its price date. The sections follow under their headings, each cite as its text followed by its footnote mark, `$150.00[^1]`, so no ref appears in the text; after the last section, under "References", a line `[^n]: <ref> — <source>` for each ref; then Appendix A as tables and Appendix B as a list. Where the report page draws a chart, the export carries the chart's table twin under the section's heading, before its text, every figure written as a cite of it would read (16.3), but a total or a figure a share of a company in another currency with its code before it ("SEK 2,100,000,000"; the closes, the band and the values a share of a reading and of a grid are US dollars), a day as the references write it, and an empty cell where none is on file: in Financial performance and health the six tables of 16.5, one each, titled by the table, each row by its name as the spec writes it ("Altman's Z″", "Ten-year aggregate ROIC"), over the last five fiscal years; in Valuation each sensitivity reading the sections cite (14.8), once, then the grid of the secondary valuation the report rests on, its columns the rates and its rows the terminal growths or, on the parts form, the shift in every part's (15.5); in Price and multiples the price against the band by quarter, each calendar quarter's last close of the five years to the newest close on file with the fair band in force at the quarter's end, the band of the newest snapshot priced on or before it, empty before the first snapshot and on a refusal's, then the four statistics of the trailing and the forward P/E over their ten years with the day each series began (16.7), and each series' last point in each calendar year of the ten years. The tables are read from the store when the report is exported, no vendor called; the JSON does not carry them, since the page reads the series itself: the six tables, the readings the sections cite, the secondary valuation's grid in Appendix A, the closes of the five years, the band's history (`GET /api/companies/{symbol}/band-history`: a point a snapshot that carries a price date, the newest of each day, oldest first, with its price and its four levels, none on a refusal's) and the two P/E series. The page draws a chart from each, every one but the grids with its table twin behind the page's one table toggle (a grid carries every value in its cells and is its own twin), and titles it in the export's words: a part of Financial performance and health under its table's title, each chart by its row's name, buybacks and dividends one measure, their sum; a reading's grid and the secondary valuation's grid by their tables' titles; the price against the band by its table's; each P/E series by its column's.

### 16.7 The multiples history

Where the price sits against its own history (spec 2026-11-06 Tier 2, 13.7): two P/E series on file, served by `GET /api/companies/{symbol}/multiples-history` and the tool `multiples_history(symbol)`, each point and each statistic with its `multiple:` ref (16.2). The history is context for the report's price section; the mechanical multiples stay the snapshot's intermediates at the snapshot's price (5.9), and no route reads the history.

- **The trailing P/E** (`pe`): GuruFocus's daily `pettm` series, fetched through the `pe-history` pass-through (provider `gf`, GuruFocus's name in the store, endpoint `pettm`), stored as a vendor response and reused for 168 hours, seven days, whatever `reuse_hours` is. A row is `[date, open, high, low, close]`, the close the day's P/E, or `[date, value]`. A value that is not a positive number is a loss (GuruFocus writes a loss day as "At Loss" and three zeros): it has no point and nothing fills its days, so the series keeps the gap. A row without an ISO date is left out. GuruFocus answers some failures with HTTP 200 and a JSON body, and its catch-all with every value zero, so a payload with no dated row, or with no point above zero, is stored with the error `unusable payload: ...` and never reused, and the next read fetches again. The read then serves the newest usable stored series, whatever its age, with `peNote` "the series could not be read", so the read and the refs agree; with none stored, it answers the fetched one with no points and that note. When the fetch cannot be had (no GuruFocus key, the pass-throughs busy, the call failing), the read serves the newest usable stored series, whatever its age, with the reason in `peNote` ("no GuruFocus key: the pe-history pass-through is off", the busy sentence or the vendor's); the series is `null` only when none is stored. A ref reads the same newest usable stored response, so the read and the refs agree.
- **The forward P/E** (`pe_forward`): Fairband's own log, since no vendor on file keeps a forward P/E history. Every snapshot the value stage writes that carries a price and its date (a full or scoped refresh's and a revalue's, a refusal's among them) and every reprice logs a row in the snapshot's transaction: the company, the price date, the price, and the reported, clean and forward P/E of the result's intermediates (5.9), none where the result has none (a refusal has none; Mature Stable has no forward P/E, 6.5), with the snapshot and the refresh. A result with no price logs nothing, and a symbol's failure (a failed fetch or valuation) logs nothing: its price is a stored quote or a quote the run fetched, and a day without a valued snapshot has no multiples to log. The newest write of a day replaces the row's price, snapshot and refresh and each multiple it carries; a multiple it lacks keeps the figure the row holds, so a refusal never blanks a valued day, and a second price refresh the same day keeps one row with the later figures. A row with no forward P/E has no point.

Each series serves the points of the ten years to its newest day on file, from the same day ten years before, oldest first, and four statistics over them: the average (`avg_10y`), the median (`median_10y`), the highest (`high_10y`) and the lowest (`low_10y`); a series with no point has none. `since` is the series' first day on file, a loss day or a row with no forward P/E included: when GuruFocus's series begins, and when the log began, which the page says while the log is short.

### 16.8 The filing reports and the filing list

What a company filed, read from FMP and stored (spec 2026-11-11 filings access): the filing list with each filing's links, and the filing report, FMP's JSON form of a 10-Q or a 10-K, served a part at a time, so that no answer holds a whole report (1 to 1.5 MB). A cell of a report is cited by its `filing:` ref (16.2), and the references list names it by the report's fiscal year and period, the sheet, the row's label and the column with its words (16.6).

Three pass-through kinds fetch them, each stored as a vendor response under provider `fmp` with its body scrubbed of the key, and reused as every pass-through is: the newest response to the same call observed within the kind's window, never one stored with an error. `filings` stores FMP's `sec-filings-search/symbol` under the endpoint `sec-filings`, page 0 of 1,000 rows, the call being the symbol, `from`, `to` and the page size, reused for `reuse_hours`. `filing-reports` stores `financial-reports-dates` under `filing-report-dates`, every report on file, reused for `reuse_hours`. `filing-report` stores `financial-reports-json` under `filing-report`, the report of a fiscal `year` and a `period` (`FY`, `Q1` to `Q4`), reused for 720 hours, thirty days, whatever `reuse_hours` is, since a filing does not change once filed. A list kind's payload is usable when it is a list, a report's when it is an object without the key `Error Message` that holds a sheet (a key whose value is a list). FMP answers a period it does not hold, or an unknown symbol, with HTTP 200 and `{"Error Message": ...}`: an unusable payload is stored with the error `unusable payload: ...` and never reused, so the next ask calls FMP again. The refresh fetches none of the three; only a request does. They are not kinds of the generic pass-through route `/api/vendor/{kind}/{symbol}`, whose answer is the whole payload; of its kinds `insider-trades` alone takes a cut, `limit` (1 to 100) answering the first of the stored page's trades, the newest, each at its place in the page so a `response:` ref names the same trade, with `tradeCount`, the trades the page holds (the MCP tool asks for 25 unless told). At most four pass-through calls are in flight at once; a call that finds every slot taken waits ten seconds for one, then is refused.

Three routes serve them. Each may call FMP, so each needs the request header `X-Fairband-Request: 1`, as a write does. Each answer carries `symbol`, the exchange form, and `responseId`, `observedAt` and `reused` of the stored response it read. The company need not be on the screen.

- **Served text.** A sheet's name, a row's label and a string cell are served with every run of white space, the no-break space among it, as one space, and the ends trimmed. Offsets and lengths count in the served text; the stored payload is not changed. A sheet's served name is its stored key served so: the sheet list names a sheet by it, the `sheet` parameter is served and then matched against it (`LEASES ` reads LEASES, answered and cited as LEASES), and a `filing:` ref holds it. FMP cuts a sheet's key at 31 characters and makes it unique (`SEGMENT INFORMATION AND GEOGR_1`); where two keys serve alike, the first is meant, and the later one cannot be reached by name: a search hit in it answers `rowRef` null, since its ref would name the earlier sheet's row. A number cell's text is its JSON text (`12.5`).
- **A column's words.** A sheet's columns are periods or members, and a `filing:` ref names neither, so the reads name each column in the served words of the sheet's top rows: the first row's cells are the columns' spans (`3 Months Ended`), and a row labelled `items` among the first three holds their dates (`Sep. 30, 2026`). A column's words are the `items` row's cell at the column, after the first row's cell at the column when the first row holds exactly as many cells as the `items` row (a first row of fewer cells spans two dates or more, so which span is the column's is not known and it is left out), the two joined with ", " (`3 Months Ended, Sep. 30, 2026`); on a sheet with no `items` row, the first row's cell at the column. A number or an empty cell gives no word; a column nothing names has none. A column's words are cut at 200 characters.
- **The budget.** A list an answer holds (the sheets, a sheet's rows with the words of their columns, the filings, the search hits) stops before the item that would take the list past 30,000 characters as the MCP process prints it, one item at least, and the answer says that more remains (`nextRow`, `more`). The MCP process prints an answer's JSON indented by two, which makes a list dense with small items 2.5 to 4 times longer than its compact JSON, so each item is measured as it is printed inside the answer: its JSON indented by two, 4 more for each of its lines (it sits two levels deep), and 2 for the comma and the line break after it. The counts below (100 sheets, 100 rows, 100 filings, 20 hits) are upper bounds beside it. So an answer prints within about 40,000 characters as the agent's host receives it.
- **A wide row.** FMP stores a note as one row: the note's whole text as its first cell, and the note's tables flattened after it into string cells, most of them empty, thousands on a long note (MSFT's `INVESTMENTS` in the 10-Q of fiscal 2027 Q1: 3,377). One row is the one item the budget always serves, so a served row holds its leading cells alone: 100 at most, and as many as keep them at or under 10,000 characters as printed (a cell sits four levels deep, so each of its lines counts 8 more), one at least; a row cut so carries `cellCount`, the count of all its cells. A sheet's `columns` are printed with its rows, so a page's rows and the words of the columns they reach are held to the budget together. A search hit names the first 60 number cells of its row, and `numberCount`, their count, where the row holds more. No cut changes an index: the cell's view and a `filing:` ref read every cell, and a cell past a cut still has its column named, by the hit that matched it (`column`) and by the cell's view (`column`).
- **The filing list**, `GET /api/companies/{symbol}/filings?from=&to=&forms=&fromRow=`. The window runs from `from` to `to`, ISO dates; `to` is the day's date when left out, and `from` 365 days before `to`. `forms` is a comma list of form types matched whole and without regard to case (`10-K,10-Q,8-K`), or `all`; left out, every form but the ownership forms `3`, `4`, `5` and `144` and their amendments (`4/A`), which `insider_trades` serves. The answer: `from` and `to`; `filings`, newest first as FMP lists them, from `fromRow` of the kept filings (0 when left out), at most 100 and within the budget, each `formType`, `filingDate` (the date of FMP's `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`, a value that does not start with a date as FMP gave it), `acceptedDate` as FMP gives it, `link` (the filing's index page on sec.gov) and `finalLink` (the main document); `nextRow`, the next `fromRow`, or null; `count`, the filings the filter kept; `leftOut`, the rows it dropped, a row that is not an object among them; and `truncated`, true when FMP answered 1,000 rows, so older filings of the window are missing.
- **The filing report**, `GET /api/companies/{symbol}/filing-report?year=&period=&sheet=&fromRow=&row=&col=&offset=`, four views by the parameters given; a parameter the view does not read is not read. Neither `year` nor `period`: `reports`, the reports on file as FMP orders them, each `{year, period}`, its fiscal year and period, a row without one of them left out and the links dropped. `year` and `period`: `year`, `period`, `sheets` in the report's order from `fromRow` (0 when left out), at most 100 and within the budget, and `nextRow`, the next `fromRow`, or null after the last sheet; each sheet `{name, title, rows, chars}`: the sheet's served name, its first row's served label (the sheet's title with its scale, `$ in Millions`), its row count and the length of its JSON text. With `sheet`: `sheet`, `title`, `columns`, `rows` from `fromRow` (0 when left out), at most 100 and within the budget, and `nextRow`; `columns` holds each column's words, as many as the widest row of the page serves cells, null where a column has none, so a later page names them too. A row is `{row, label, rowRef, cells}`: its index in the sheet from 0, its served label, its ref (the `filing:` ref without the column, so a cell's ref is the row's plus `/<col>`) and its cells in order, a number or a null as it is, a string served and, past 2,000 characters, cut, as `{text, chars}` with its full length; a wide row holds its leading cells and `cellCount` (a wide row, above). A row that is not a label with its cells (one key whose value is a list) is served with `label` and `rowRef` null and one cell, its JSON text, which no ref cites. With `sheet` and `row`: one cell's text, `sheet`, `row`, `col` (0 when left out), `column` (the words of the cell's column, or null), `label`, `chars`, `offset` (0 when left out), `text`, at most 20,000 characters from `offset`, and `nextOffset`, or null after the text's end; a number cell's text is its JSON text.
- **The search**, `GET /api/companies/{symbol}/filing-report/search?year=&period=&text=`: the served text of every label and every cell of one report searched for `text`, served, without regard to case; a number cell is searched by its JSON text, and also for `text` with its commas removed, so a number as it is printed finds its cell (`33,165` finds `33165`), while a text cell is matched as it reads. A hit is a row, `{sheet, row, col, column, label, excerpt, offset, rowRef, numbers}`: `col` the first cell that matches, or null when only the label does, and `column` the words of that cell's column, or null; `excerpt` 300 characters each side of the first match; `offset` the match's place in the cell's served text (the label's when `col` is null), an `offset` the cell's view opens at; `numbers` the row's number cells as `{col, value, column}`, `column` the column's words or null, the first 60 with `numberCount` where the row holds more. At most 20 hits and within the budget, in the report's order, and `more` true when a further row matched. The answer carries `year`, `period` and `text`, the text searched.

The refusals, in the order they are judged. Every one above "Every slot taken" is judged before any call, so a request it refuses calls no vendor and stores nothing:

| Case | Status | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| No request header | 403 | `missing X-Fairband-Request header` |
| `year`, `fromRow`, `row`, `col` or `offset` not a whole number | 400 | `<name>: <what is wrong>` |
| The server has no FMP key | 503 | `no FMP key` |
| A symbol that is not one | 400 | `'<text>' is not a symbol` |
| `sheet`, `row`, `col`, `offset`, `fromRow` or `text` without `year` and `period`, or one of the two without the other | 400 | `a filing report needs year and period` |
| A `period` other than `FY` or `Q1` to `Q4`, read upper-cased (`q1` is `Q1` to the check, the call and the reuse key) | 400 | `period is FY or Q1 to Q4` |
| `text` shorter than three characters, served | 400 | `text needs three characters or more` |
| `from` or `to` not an ISO date, `from` after `to`, or a `to` whose default `from` would fall before the calendar's first day | 400 | `from and to are ISO dates, from on or before to` |
| Every slot taken | 503 | `the pass-throughs are busy: four calls are in flight, try again` |
| The vendor call fails | 502 | the vendor's sentence, scrubbed of the key |
| FMP holds no such report | 404 | `FMP holds no filing report for <symbol> <year> <period>: ask without year and period for the reports on file` |
| FMP's filing list, or its list of reports, is not a list | 502 | `FMP answered no filing list for <symbol>`, `FMP answered no filing reports for <symbol>` |
| No sheet serves as `sheet` | 404 | `no sheet named <name>: ask without sheet for the sheet list` |
| `row`, `col`, `fromRow` or `offset` out of range | 400 | `<name> is 0 to <last>`, or `<name> is out of range: there is none` where the sheet has no row or the row no cell; on the filing list, with no filing kept, 0 alone is in range |

### 16.9 The stored quarters

A single quarter's statement lines as the store holds them (spec 2026-11-11 filings access, 7), served by `GET /api/companies/{symbol}/quarters` and the tool `quarters(symbol)`, each line with its `fact:` ref (16.2), so a report cites a quarter's figures from the store rather than from a press release. The read calls no vendor and answers 404 "no financial statements for <symbol>" without an annual income statement, as `financials` does; `financials` does not serve the quarters.

The answer: `symbol`; `currency`, the reporting currency as `financials` names it, the newest annual income statement's; `quarters`, the newest eight stored periods of the quarterly statements in the reporting currency, oldest first, none without quarterly rows; and `otherCurrency`, the count of the stored periods left out for another currency. The refresh fetches eight quarters a run (section 2) and the store keeps every period it stored, so the read stops at eight; a `fact:` ref reads any stored period in the reporting currency, the newest eight or older. A period is `{period, periodEnd, fiscalYear, fiscalPeriod}`, `period` its stored fiscal label `<fiscalYear>-<fiscalPeriod>` (`2027-Q1`, the form a consensus quarter takes), and one figure `{value, unit, ref}` a line of the trailing figures (4.2): revenue, operating income, net income, diluted EPS, stock compensation, cash from operations, capital spending, dividends, buybacks, equity issued, interest expense, depreciation, income tax and pre-tax income, each ref `fact:<symbol>/<line>/<period>`.

- A quarter's figure for a line is that period's own term in the trailing figures' sum, read by the same reading: signed as stored, the interest expense by its size, none where the period lacks the line. Its cash-flow lines are the quarterly cash flow's of the same period end, none without one. So where the trailing figures are the sum of four quarters (4.2), the newest four add to the trailing figure of every line both hold; for a half-yearly reporter whose trailing figures sum two half-years, the newest two add; on the fiscal-year fallback (`trailing-on-fiscal-year`) the trailing figures are a fiscal year's and the newest periods need not add to them.
- A period whose income statement names another currency than the reporting one, the newest annual income statement's, is left out and counted: the trailing figures do not sum such a period either. A period with no currency on file counts as the reporting one's; with no reporting currency on file, none is left out.
- A half-yearly reporter's stored periods are its half-years, served by their stored labels.
- A period without a fiscal year or a fiscal period on file is left out, uncounted; of two with one label, the one with the later period end is served.
- The diluted EPS is the statement's as stored. On a row flagged `split-pending` or `basis-scaled` it may sit on another share basis than the quote's.
